        - 5c: recording replay and the files-changed tab.
        - 5d: sessions run outside Marshell, and Codex conversations.
        - 5e: retention, the backup mirror, disk usage, recording compression and the exit check.
     2. 5a lists the Claude Code sessions run in a Marshell tab, which slice 3e1 already archives. Conversations from a plain terminal come in 5d, which watches Claude's transcript folder read-only and copies them into the archive.
     3. The History view takes the main area in place of the panes, and the sidebar stays. Ctrl+Shift+Y opens and closes it, it opens with its search box focused, and Esc goes back to the sessions.
     4. (A ruling made in planning, following slice 3e decision 3.) The search index is built by parsing Marshell's archive copy of each transcript (format: `docs/research/2026-10-23-claude-transcripts.md`). This amends slice 3e decision 5 for search only: the sessions list is still rebuilt from the sidecars, and a transcript that can't be parsed still lists.
   - **Slice 5b decisions (2026-10-23).**
     1. A conversation and its tab share one name. Renaming a tab (F2) names its conversation in History, renaming in History renames the live tab when one is running, and Resume and Fork open their tab under that name.
     2. A bookmark can be made in both places: Ctrl+Shift+B in a live Claude Code session marks the current turn, and the conversation view can bookmark any turn. A bookmarked turn has a mark, the list has a Bookmarked filter, and opening a bookmark jumps to its turn as a search hit does.
     3. Export in 5b covers Copy as markdown and Export as markdown or JSON, through a Save dialog with a preview. Redaction is on by default, with a toggle, and uses the diagnostics redactor. The asciicast export moves to 5c, beside the recording replay it depends on.
     4. Pinned conversations form a "Pinned" group above the project groups. Archived conversations leave the list and the search results until a "Show archived" filter is on. "Delete Marshell's copy" asks first and never touches Claude Code's own files.
   - **Slice 5c decisions (2026-10-24).**
     1. Recordings play in Marshell's own player, built on xterm.js with the live terminal's renderer, theme and font. No new dependency. Its keyboard controls are play and pause, speed, an idle-time cap and jumps between prompt marks. (A ruling made in planning: the jumps also stop at each turn's start, because Claude Code prints no prompt marks of its own, so a conversation's stretch would otherwise have none.)
     2. A conversation's Recording tab opens on that conversation's stretch of its tab's recording, from its first turn to its last. A "Whole recording" switch shows the rest, which can include other conversations and shell time. Each turn in the conversation view has "Play from here", which opens the Recording tab at that moment.
     3. The Files changed tab is built from Claude Code's Edit and Write results in the transcript. It lists each file with its added and removed counts, then its edits in order, each with "Go to turn". Changes made through shell commands are not listed, and the tab says so. No git diff is read.
     4. The Export dialog gains "Asciicast (.cast)". The diagnostics redactor runs over the text of each output event, with the same on-by-default toggle as the other formats, and the file stays a valid, replayable asciicast.
   - **Slice 5d decisions (2026-10-24).**
     1. While Marshell runs, it watches Claude Code's transcript folder (`~/.claude/projects`) read-only and copies each new or changed transcript into its archive a few seconds after the file stops changing. A switch in Settings turns this off. Marshell never writes to Claude's folder.
     2. The first run copies every transcript that exists, so Claude's own cleanup can't lose them later. It runs in the background, with a progress line in History.
     3. Codex CLI conversations are copied the same way, from `~/.codex/sessions` (read-only, with the same switch). Each gets the conversation view, search, a Files changed tab built from `apply_patch`, and Resume through `codex resume <id>`. A rollout format version Marshell doesn't know shows "Status limited".
     4. Codex cloud tasks (`codex cloud list --json`) wait for a later slice, beside phase 6's Accounts. 5d covers local files only.
     5. A conversation that ran outside Marshell has a small "Outside Marshell" mark on its row ("VS Code" when the transcript says so) and sits in the same project groups as the rest. The filters gain "Where: Any / Marshell / Outside".
     6. Resume on an outside conversation whose transcript changed in the last 10 minutes asks first, inline: "This conversation changed 3 min ago and may still be open in another terminal. Resume here anyway?" Otherwise it resumes at once, in a new tab in its folder.
     7. Headless runs (`claude -p`, `codex exec`, Agent SDK apps) are archived like the rest but left out of the list and the search results until a "Show headless runs" filter is on.
   - **Slice 5e decisions (2026-10-24).**
     1. Retention limits (size and age) are off by default and remove only the oldest terminal recordings and image attachments. Transcript copies always stay, since they are small and are what "nothing is ever lost" protects. Pinned and bookmarked conversations are never touched.
     2. Saving a new or tighter limit first shows inline what it would remove now (count, size, the oldest date) and asks once. After that the cleanup runs on its own at startup and every 24 hours, and each run is logged.
     3. The backup folder is a one-way, exact mirror of history: the archive, sidecars, marks and finished recordings. A retention removal or "Delete Marshell's copy" also removes the file from the backup. The index is not copied, because it is rebuilt from the sidecars.
     4. The mirror copies a new or changed file within about a minute, batched, and checks everything at startup. A missing or offline folder shows a warning in Storage and retries; nothing blocks the app.
     5. A recording is compressed with zstd when its session ends, and the player reads both forms. A low-priority pass compresses every finished plain `.cast` already on disk, oldest first, skipping any still being written.
     6. The MB-per-hour budget is measured, then stated: the live check records a heavy Claude Code session and a busy shell, and the budget goes into this section's exit line with headroom over the measured figure.
     7. Disk usage is a Storage section in the History settings dialog: totals for transcripts, recordings, attachments, snapshots and logs, the retention limits beside them, and the backup folder's last copy.
     8. The phase 5 exit check runs on the `phase-5e` head before the review: a Rust test that searches a synthetic 1000-session index under 100 ms, and a live check with a scratch home covering the budget measurement, retention, the backup mirror and disk usage. Its findings go in `docs/research/`.
6. **Agents, integration and the elevated helper.** Slice 6a live check: `docs/research/2026-10-24-codex-adapter-live-check.md`.
   - Accounts, company tabs, extensions and CLI versions (added 2026-10-16; evidence in `docs/research/2026-10-16-accounts-cloud-extensions.md`):
     - **Accounts** (Settings → Accounts, one section per company). Sign in runs the CLI's own login command in a terminal (`claude auth login`, `codex login`). Marshell never collects, stores or brokers OAuth tokens and never reads credential files. Status comes from the CLI's status command: `claude auth status` (JSON: `loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `email`, `subscriptionType`) and `codex login status` (text). The row reads "Signed in as <email>, <plan>, checked <time> ago", with a Sign in button when signed out. There is no expiry countdown, because no CLI exposes one. A value the command does not give is left out (nothing guessed).
     - **Antigravity is terminal only.** It has no login or status command, and Google's consumer terms ban wrapping it in other agents, so it has no account status, doctor or automation. Its Accounts section says why.
     - **Company tabs.** A company's settings tab appears once its CLI is signed in by any method, an API key included. It edits that CLI's own settings (Claude Code `settings.json`, Codex `config.toml`), not Claude Desktop's. Every write goes through the config service (preview, backup, atomic write, undo; section 6).
     - **Extensions** (a universal tab; moved to phase 8 by slice 6a decision 2). It covers MCP servers, skills, hooks and plugins, but only where they port. Skills (`SKILL.md`) are the portable unit. MCP stdio entries can be translated between the Claude JSON, Codex TOML and `agy` JSON formats, with a preview. Hooks, plugins and slash commands stay per CLI.
     - **CLI version and update** (in settings). Each CLI's `--version` and install type, and an update button (`claude update`, `codex update`) with the command's output shown. This pulls part of the v2 installer runner forward; installing a CLI stays in v2.
   - Scope: settings pane (read-only until the company tabs above add editing), the terminal font setting (added 2026-10-18; three bundled fonts and a "Match my terminal" default, specified under "Visual system" and confirmed by slice 6a decision 5), Codex + Antigravity adapters (install both CLIs first; the Antigravity adapter verifies its hook events and any permission hook against a real install), custom agents, repo scripts (`marshell.json`), port chips, the `marshell` CLI and its built-in skill (when the app is closed, `marshell new` says so and offers to start it), project references (`@` picker, alias learning), WSL (S11), "open in system terminal" and "copy resume command", and the `marshell-host` elevated helper. S8 passed with requirements: the token goes over a pipe, never the command line, and the decline and wrong-token paths get tested. Until it ships, elevated sessions open in a separate elevated Marshell window.
   - Exit: fixture suites from the real CLIs pass.
   - **Slice 6a decisions (2026-10-24).**
     1. Phase 6 runs as six slices, the adapters first because the exit needs fixtures from the real CLIs:
        - 6a: the Codex adapter: hook install and removal through the config service, the hook-trust step, live status, approvals, and fixtures from a real Codex (0.163).
        - 6b: the Antigravity adapter: verify its hook events against a real `agy` first, then install and removal, live status, and approvals only if it has a permission hook.
        - 6c: Accounts, CLI version and update, the settings pane wired into the app (read-only), and the terminal font setting.
        - 6d: the company tabs, which edit settings.
        - 6e: the `marshell` CLI (`list`, `send`, `new`) and its built-in skill, "Copy resume command" and "Open in system terminal".
        - 6f: repo scripts (`marshell.json`), port chips, custom agents, and project references (`@` picker, alias learning).
        - 6g: WSL (spike S11 first) and the `marshell-host` elevated helper.
     2. Extensions (MCP servers, skills, MCP translation between formats) move to phase 8, beside the editors and the instruction library.
     3. Codex hooks go in the user-level `~/.codex/hooks.json` (under `CODEX_HOME` when it is set), never in `config.toml`, and in one source only, so Codex doesn't merge a duplicate. The consent card says the hooks don't run until the user trusts them in Codex's `/hooks`, and Marshell never automates or bypasses that step.
     4. Codex's `PermissionRequest` is held for the approve card, Departures and banners, as Claude Code's is. With no decision inside a bounded timeout, Marshell returns none, and Codex shows its own prompt.
     5. The terminal font setting ships the proposed list: Match my terminal (default), JetBrains Mono, Cascadia Mono and Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono, the last two bundled under the SIL OFL with their notices in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`.
     6. The company tabs edit Claude Code's `settings.json` and Codex's `config.toml`, with a form and a raw mode, through the config service. `config.toml` is edited with `toml_edit`, so comments and formatting survive; 6d's plan states the need under the dependency policy.
     7. `marshell send <session> <text>` fills the session's prompt line and never presses Enter, like the diff drawer's comments and drop and paste.
     8. Elevated sessions use `marshell-host` as S8 requires: one UAC prompt, the token over a pipe and never on the command line, tested decline and wrong-token paths, and the elevated tab drawn like any other.
     9. Accounts' Sign in runs the CLI's login command in a new Marshell tab (named "Sign in to Claude Code", for example), and the status is checked again when that tab exits. Marshell never sees the token.
     10. Marshell shows whether Codex trusts its hooks by reading Codex's `config.toml` read-only: each Marshell entry's hash (Codex's own recipe) is compared with the `trusted_hash` under `[hooks.state]`. Setup and the Agents dialog say "Trusted", "Not trusted yet" or "Changed since you trusted them". This adds the `toml` crate (already in the lockfile through Tauri, MIT/Apache) to `marshell-core`, and replaces showing when the hooks last ran.
     11. Marshell's Codex edits never move a user's hook. Codex keys trust by an entry's position in `hooks.json`, so a user group that comes after Marshell's would lose its trust if Marshell's group went. Removal leaves an empty `{"hooks": []}` in place of a Marshell group that has a later group after it, and a reinstall replaces Marshell's entries where they are instead of removing and appending them (Task 1 found Codex counts empty groups and loads them without a warning).
     12. Marshell starts every Codex tab with `--no-daemon`. Codex 0.163 otherwise runs hooks in a shared app-server daemon, which the first Codex in a `CODEX_HOME` starts and which keeps that tab's `MARSHELL_TAB_ID`, so a second tab's status, recap and approve card landed on the first tab's row (the 6a live check). The bridge also ignores the tab id when a hook runs inside Codex's shared app-server, so a Codex started any other way reports to no tab (limited status, and Codex asks in its own terminal) instead of to the wrong one. The cost: these sessions don't appear in Codex's own list of the daemon's sessions.
     13. A tab started in the background through the API gets xterm's late answer to a colour query typed into the CLI's prompt when it is first shown. It is a terminal-layer bug, not Codex's: it goes to a GitHub issue and its own change, and the 6a PR lists it under Known limits.
     14. `--no-daemon` goes only to a Codex whose detected version is 0.163.0 or newer, the first release verified to accept it (the final review). For an older Codex, or one whose version can't be read, Marshell leaves the flag off, and the bridge's check for Codex's daemon (decision 12) keeps the hooks off the wrong tab: they report to no tab, and Codex asks the person itself.
     15. The Codex hook command quotes the bridge path for its shell with single quotes, so a `$` or a backtick in the Marshell home path is never expanded (the final review). The entries' trust hashes change with it, which costs nothing before the first release.
     16. `marshell uninstall` goes on past a Codex `hooks.json` it can't read or plan: it reports the problem and still removes Claude Code's hooks. The bin folder stays while Codex's entries may still point at it (the final review; replaces the Task 3 ruling).
     17. Removing a hook group that ends its array leaves a newline before `]`, and drops a trailing comma, where the install had added them. The value, the user's groups, their positions and their trust are unchanged, and the removal diff shows it. The cause is the shared jsonc array append and removal that Claude Code's settings use too, so it goes to a GitHub issue (#63) and the pull request's Known limits, with its failing-first test kept under `#[ignore]` (the fix pass).
   - **Slice 6a2 decisions (2026-10-25).** A fix slice between 6a and 6b, for issues #60 and #61.
     1. A terminal never answers a query from output written before it attached. The core sends a new frame after each Resume that says where live output starts, and caps the replay there. The terminal drops the replies xterm generates while it parses the replay (colour, cursor-position, device-attribute, mode and setting reports), and sends typing, focus, mouse and replies to live queries as before. This covers a tab's first show, a restart and any remount (#60).
     2. Resume and Restart of a Codex tab use `codex resume <id>` for the tab's latest Codex conversation on record, checked as History's "Open in new tab" checks it (#61).
     3. A Codex tab with no conversation on record (hooks off, untrusted, or never fired) keeps `resume --last`, as Claude's tab keeps `--continue`.
     4. A Codex conversation on record whose rollout file is gone starts Codex fresh, with no resume argument. Marshell keeps no copy of Codex rollouts, so it can't tell a deleted conversation from one Codex never wrote.
   - **Slice 6b decisions (2026-10-25).** Agent manifests and own agents. Evidence: `docs/research/2026-10-25-agent-and-tool-cli-manifests.md`.
     1. Three tiers. Rust adapters are only for the hook-capable CLIs Marshell installs hooks for: Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity. Qwen Code (6l1 decision 2) and Copilot CLI (6n1 decision 2) joined them later. Every other agent CLI is a data manifest. It holds how to launch the CLI with a first prompt, continue and resume, the flags for each mode, the exe names and install folders that identify it, and its mark. Developer tools that aren't agents get tool profiles (6c). Marshell ships a curated set of both, and a person can add their own.
     2. The long tail comes before Antigravity. This pulls custom agents and port chips forward from the old 6f. Phase 6 now runs:
        - 6b: agent manifests and own agents.
        - 6c: tool profiles (launch presets, needs-you patterns, port chips).
        - 6d: the Antigravity adapter (the old 6b).
        - 6e: Accounts, CLI version and update, the settings pane and the terminal font (the old 6c).
        - 6f: the company tabs (the old 6d).
        - 6g: the `marshell` CLI, "Copy resume command" and "Open in system terminal" (the old 6e).
        - 6h: repo scripts and project references (the old 6f, less what moved).
        - 6i: WSL and `marshell-host` (the old 6g).

        Slice 6a's decision 1 keeps the old letters as the record of that day.
     3. Shipped manifests: GitHub Copilot CLI, Cursor CLI, opencode, Amp, Grok Build, Qwen Code, Kimi Code CLI, Aider and Crush. Kimi means Kimi Code CLI (`~/.kimi-code`). The archived Python `kimi-cli` is not covered.
     4. A manifest agent's status comes from the terminal only. Marshell writes no hook or config file for these CLIs and guesses no status.
        - It gets the pty fallback that every agent row without hooks has (slice 3c2 decision 4): Working while output flows and Idle when it's quiet, both marked "Status limited".
        - Its own OSC 9;4 progress also shows Working.
        - Its own notification (OSC 9, 99 or 777) or a bell shows Needs you.

        (Amended the same day: the first answer, "signals only", was given without knowing about the fallback.)
     5. DeepSeek is a Claude Code preset, not a manifest. "DeepSeek" in the picker starts Claude Code with DeepSeek's documented Anthropic-compatible environment (base URL and model names). The key comes from an environment variable the person sets (`DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`) and goes only into that process's environment. Marshell never stores, logs or shows it. The tab shows the DeepSeek mark and keeps Claude Code's adapter for status. With the variable unset, the entry says so and doesn't start.
     6. Own agents live in `~/.marshell/agents.json` (§4). A person edits the file by hand or through a form. Settings isn't wired into the app until 6e, so in 6b the form is an "Add your own agent" section of the live Agents dialog, and it moves to Settings → Agents in 6e. The form covers name, command, the first-prompt flag, continue and resume flags, mode flags and the exe names to recognise. An invalid entry is listed with the reason, and the file is reloaded when it changes.
     7. Grok runs Claude Code's hook files by default (`[compat.claude] hooks`). Marshell filters on its side and leaves Grok's settings alone:
        - The core drops Claude hook events from a tab whose agent isn't Claude Code.
        - The hook bridge exits quietly when Grok's `GROK_HOOK_EVENT` is set, which covers a Grok typed into a shell.

        The person's own Claude hooks still run inside Grok.
     8. Live check:
        - Install opencode, Qwen Code, Crush and Kimi Code into a scratch prefix, and check their launch, resume and mode flags against `--help` and a real start, without signing in.
        - Grok runs a real session in a scratch folder on the person's own account.
        - Copilot, Cursor and Amp are checked from their docs only, and the pull request lists them under Known limits.
   - **Slice 6c decisions (2026-10-25).** Tool profiles. Decisions 1 and 2 were made with 6b; 3 to 10 when 6c started. Evidence: `docs/research/2026-10-25-agent-and-tool-cli-manifests.md`, "Tool profiles". Slice 6o removed tool profiles (6o decisions 2 and 3): decisions 1 and 5 to 9 and the announced-URL half of 3 no longer apply; listener port chips, secret handling and decision 11 stay.
     1. A tool profile can carry three things:
        - launch presets: start the tool from the picker with saved arguments and a folder;
        - needs-you patterns: the text that marks the tab as waiting on the person, such as a `y/N` question or a sign-in prompt;
        - port announcement lines, which become port chips the person can click.
     2. While Marshell can see a command that prints secrets running in a tab, it doesn't scan that output for prompts or ports and doesn't record it. Examples are `op read`, `op item get --reveal`, `supabase status`, `gh auth token` and `kubectl get secret -o yaml`. Marshell sees the command from its process arguments or from the shell integration's command line. The recording gets a marker saying why. It is best effort: a command Marshell can't see is still recorded.
     3. Port chips come from two sources. One is the URL a profiled tool announces, which keeps the scheme and path. The other is the ports that the tab's own processes listen on, as the OS reports them, so any dev server gets a chip without a profile. A chip goes away when its port closes. Docker-published ports and ngrok aren't in the tab's process tree, and their polling fallback waits for a later slice. Auth-callback ports get no chip.
     4. Clicking a port chip opens the loopback URL in the default browser without asking, unlike web links in output, which ask first. The chip's menu (right-click, Shift+F10) offers Copy URL. Chips are reachable by keyboard.
     5. A matched prompt is a full Needs you, the same as a hook's, marked Status limited. It shows in the row glyph and the title and tray count, and it gets a banner and sound under the existing notification settings. It clears when the person types into the tab or the tool prints more output.
     6. Needs-you patterns run only while the foreground command is a profiled tool, including one started through `npx`, `pnpm dlx`, `pnpm exec`, `npm exec` or `bunx`. Ordinary output in a shell never trips them.
     7. The shipped profiles cover every tool in the research note, with only the patterns tagged `[src]`, `[docs]` or `[local]` there. A string marked `[unverified]` ships only after a live check confirms it. The live check runs the installed op, docker, gh, kubectl and gcloud, plus Vite and Next.js through `npx` in a scratch folder.
     8. Own tool profiles live in `~/.marshell/tools.json`, which is reloaded when it changes. The Agents dialog gets a read-only Tools list: shipped and own profiles, and each invalid entry with the reason. The editor comes with Settings in 6e.
     9. Launch presets are a Tools group in the new-session picker. A preset runs as a typed command inside the default shell profile, so the shell stays after the tool exits and shell integration keeps working. Fill-in fields, such as kubectl's `<target>`, are asked at launch and never saved. Own presets live in `tools.json`. Bash, zsh, fish and WSL run a preset the way they run an agent, so the shell after it has no integration. That's a known limit in 6c (decided the same day).
     11. Port chips and secret detection read the OS's TCP listener table and a process's command line, through three more `windows-sys` features on the existing crate (decided the same day).
     10. A command line that carries a secret flag (`docker login -p`, `--token`, `--from-literal`, `az login -p` and the others in the research note) is scrubbed wherever Marshell shows or keeps it: the tab title, the foreground phrase ("Running docker login …"), the recording marker and the logs. It shows as the program name and its subcommand, followed by "…".
   - **Slice 6d decisions (2026-10-26).** The Antigravity adapter: hooks in `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json`, the status line chained in `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`, live status and approvals, all as "Corrections to the brief" item 1 describes and checked against a real agy 1.4.0 first.
     1. The spike runs a real agy in a scratch home. `live-check.ps1` gets an `-AgyAuth` switch that copies `~/.gemini/oauth_creds.json` and `google_accounts.json` into the scratch home and reads nothing else there; `stop` deletes them with the scratch folder (6a's `-CodexAuth` precedent).
     2. History: the hooks record which tab ran which Antigravity conversation, and Resume and Restart use `agy --conversation <id>` for the tab's latest one on record, as 6a2 did for Codex. A tab with none on record keeps `--continue`. No importer of Antigravity's transcripts in 6d: it waits until their schema is captured and settled.
     3. Hooks drive Working, Idle and Needs you. Where agy shows its own prompt with no hook behind it (a released request, or skip-all on), the status line's `tool_confirmation_pending` raises Needs you, marked Status limited. The status line also feeds model and context % (decision 8 leaves the plan meter out). The spike measured the cadence: agy runs the status line only when its state changes, every 0.2 to 0.5 s while working and never on a timer, and `tool_confirmation_pending` turns true about 0.8 s after `PreToolUse`.
     4. A released or timed-out request answers `ask`, so agy shows its own prompt. If the spike finds `ask` skipped while `dangerouslySkipPermissions` is on, a release answers `force_ask` instead, and the plan records the change. *Replaced by decision 7: a release or timeout answers `deny`.*
     5. "Always allow" is refused for Antigravity ("Antigravity can't keep an always-allow rule from Marshell. Allow once, or answer in the terminal."), unless the spike shows `permissionOverrides` makes agy keep the rule itself. Then the plan uses it and records that. *The spike found `permissionOverrides` grants nothing, so the refusal stands, with decision 7's copy.*
     6. Turning the hooks off deletes `hooks.json` when nothing but Marshell's hook is in it, through a new `DeleteFile` edit in the config service. Like every edit it snapshots the file first, checks its hash against the plan, shows the deletion in the diff, and undo puts the file back.
     7. Approvals: cards in Bypass only. The spike (agy 1.4.7, `docs/research/2026-10-26-antigravity-hooks.md`) found that a hook can't approve: `allow` never skips agy's own prompt, even with `permissionOverrides`. Under `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `ask` and `force_ask` let the call run. agy starts with its own mode flags. In Manual, Plan, Auto-edit and Full auto, Marshell passes every call (empty output), agy asks in its terminal, and the row's Needs you comes from the status line (decision 3). In Bypass, Marshell holds risky-list calls for a card: Allow answers `ask`, which agy runs with no prompt under the skip flag; Deny denies it. A card has no Answer in terminal, since agy in Bypass has no prompt to hand over to. This replaces decision 4: a released or timed-out request answers `deny`. It replaces decision 5's copy too: "Antigravity can't keep an always-allow rule from Marshell. Allow this once instead."
     8. The plan meter is left out for Antigravity. Its `quota` has two weekly buckets and no five-hour window, so an agy row shows model and context % only.
     9. No hand-over. agy 1.4.7 ignores `dangerouslySkipPermissions` in `settings.json` (it still prompted, and the binary has no such key). Marshell makes no edit to that key, and a session's mode comes only from its launch flags. The "A CLI setting that skips every prompt" rule under "Session modes" doesn't apply to Antigravity.
     10. In Bypass, a call Marshell can't read is held for a card too, on any agy version: a tool it doesn't know, or a known tool without the arguments it reads. The card shows the tool's name and raw arguments, with the risk "Marshell can't read this call". agy's own `read_file` and MCP tools still pass. This widens decision 7's risky list, so an agy update that renames `run_command` or its arguments can't run a risky command with no card (the final review, 2026-10-26).
   - **Slice 6e decisions (2026-10-26).** Settings, Accounts, CLI versions and the terminal font. Made together for both halves of the split.
     1. 6e runs as two slices, each with its own plan, live check, review and PR. 6b decision 2's other letters stand.
        - 6e1: the Settings page, the dialogs moved into it, the terminal font picker and its bundled fonts, the own-agent form (6b decision 6) and the `tools.json` editor (6c decision 8) in Settings → Agents.
        - 6e2: Accounts, CLI version and update, and the read-only company tabs.
     2. Settings is one page in the main window, the phase-0 design with a section nav. Sessions keep running behind it, and Esc goes back to them. Today's Appearance, Notifications, History settings, Agents, Shell profiles and Diagnostics dialogs become its sections, and the separate dialogs go away. Their palette commands open Settings at that section.
     3. The sections in 6e1, in order:
        - Appearance: app theme, accent, contrast and text size.
        - Terminal: the terminal theme, the font picker, the font size and theme import.
        - Notifications, with the close button.
        - Shell profiles.
        - History, with Storage.
        - Agents: setup per CLI, own agents, and the tools list with its editor.
        - Advanced: Diagnostics (slice 5d decision 5).

        General is left out until it has a live field. 6e2 adds Accounts, Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity.
     4. Settings has no keyboard shortcut. The shortcut audit keeps punctuation out of bindings and has no free letter. "Settings…" sits in the palette's Tools group, and the tray's "Notifications…" becomes "Settings…" (slice 4b decision 4).
     5. The terminal font picker lists Match my terminal, JetBrains Mono, Cascadia Mono, Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono and "Another font…". The last one reveals today's text field. A saved family that isn't one of the bundled names shows as "Another font…", so nobody's font changes.
     6. Cascadia Mono and Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono are bundled as Regular and Bold woff2, like JetBrains Mono. The files are unchanged from official sources: Cascadia Mono's release v2407.24, and Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono's Google Fonts repository at a pinned commit, since it publishes no releases. All three fonts get SIL OFL notices in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`; JetBrains Mono, bundled since phase 1, had none.
     7. The company tabs are read-only in 6e2: the CLI's user settings as a form with scope badges (an en dash for an unset key) and a read-only raw view. 6f adds editing through the config service. A tab appears once its CLI is detected. This amends the "Company tabs" rule above, which waited for a sign-in: Antigravity has no sign-in status, so under that rule its tab could never appear.
     8. Accounts checks `claude auth status` and `codex login status` when Settings → Accounts opens, again when a "Sign in to …" tab exits (slice 6a decision 9), and on a Check again button. There is no background polling and no Sign out button. Codex shows the method its text gives, without an email. Antigravity's section says it has no sign-in status command, so Marshell can't show one. Nothing is read from its running sessions.
     9. A CLI's Update button opens a tab named "Update <CLI>". It runs the command that fits the install type: native `claude update`, npm `npm install -g <package>@latest`, WinGet `winget upgrade <id>`, `codex update` or `agy update`. The version is read again when the tab exits. If tabs of that CLI are running, the button notes that the update may fail while they run.
     10. "Newer version available" comes only from data the CLI already keeps, such as Codex's `version.json`. Marshell makes no network request of its own. Otherwise Settings shows the installed version only.
     11. The install type comes from where the found executable lives: WinGet's `Packages` folder (the package id is in the folder name), npm's global folder, Claude Code's native `~/.local/bin`, Codex's standalone `Programs\OpenAI\Codex` folder, Antigravity's `agy\bin`. A path that fits none of them shows the version with no Update button, and one line naming the update commands to run yourself.
     12. A company tab's form shows a short curated list of that CLI's keys, with labels and help and an en dash when unset. Below it, every other key the file sets is listed by name with its value. The raw view shows the whole file.
     13. With 11 sections, the nav may be taller than the window (720×480 at text size Larger). It keeps its place beside the section and scrolls on its own, with the current section scrolled into view.
     14. A CLI's version, any "Newer version available" note and its Update button sit at the top of its company tab, above the settings form. Accounts holds only the sign-in status.
     15. Values that can hold a secret are masked in a company tab's form and raw view, with no reveal: everything under an `env` table, and keys whose name looks secret (key, token, secret, password, auth, for example Claude's `apiKeyHelper`). They show as "hidden"; the file itself is unchanged.
     16. A "Sign in to …" tab is kept out of the terminal recording and History, as commands that print secrets are, because the CLI's login may have you paste a code or key into it. Update tabs are recorded as usual.
   - **Slice 6f decisions (2026-10-27).** The company tabs edit settings, through the config service (slice 6a decision 6, slice 6e decision 7).
     1. Claude Code's and Codex's tabs become editable. Claude Code's tab edits its user `settings.json` (or `MARSHELL_CLAUDE_SETTINGS`), and Codex's tab edits `config.toml` under `CODEX_HOME`. Antigravity's tab stays read-only.
     2. Raw mode can be edited only while the file holds no value shown as "hidden". Otherwise it stays read-only, and one line says why, so a secret is never overwritten by the word "hidden".
     3. The form changes the six curated keys only, each with a typed control: a choice list for a fixed set of values, a number, a switch, or text. A "Not set" choice removes the key. "Other settings in this file" stays read-only.
     4. Claude Code's tab writes only the user file. A curated key set by a managed policy file is read-only, and names that file as its source. Project and local settings files are out of scope.
     5. Approved copy for the cases the slice plan left open (2026-10-27): a curated row whose value holds a secret is locked with "Holds a value shown as hidden, so the form can't change it."; the plan and undo refusals read "There is no file to edit as text.", "Marshell can't read the file, so it doesn't change it.", "That snapshot isn't of this agent's settings file." and "Marshell can't make this change to the file."; a failed Reload reads "Couldn't read <path> again. Close Settings and try again."; and Reload drops a raw draft when the file changed, with "<path> changed, so your raw edit was dropped. The text is the file as it is now."
   - **Slice 6g decisions (2026-10-27).** The three company tabs get the same editing, and the deferred UX minors from slice 6f are fixed (AGENTS.md: every agent CLI is supported equally). Antigravity's settings are from its docs (`antigravity.google/docs/settings` and the schema at `antigravity.google/schemas/v1/settings.json`), recorded in `docs/research/antigravity-notes/02-configuration-and-hooks.md`.
     1. Antigravity's tab becomes editable, with the same form, raw editing, Review, Apply and Undo as Claude Code's and Codex's, for `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`. The file is strict JSON: Antigravity calls it "a plain JSON file" and rewrites it in its own format at every start. Antigravity documents no managed settings file and no variable that moves its home, so a live check gives it a scratch `USERPROFILE`. The controls: `model` is text, with the help line "The model a new Antigravity session starts with, by the name /model shows, such as Gemini 3.1 Pro."; `toolPermission` is a choice of `request-review`, `proceed-in-sandbox`, `always-proceed` and `strict`; `enableTerminalSandbox` is a switch; `colorScheme` is a choice of `terminal`, `light`, `solarized light`, `colorblind-friendly light`, `dark`, `solarized dark`, `colorblind-friendly dark` and `tokyo night`; `verbosity` is a choice of `high`, `medium` and `low`.
     2. Antigravity no longer reads `enableTelemetry` from the file: telemetry is saved with the person's Google account, and Antigravity deletes the key when it saves. The Telemetry row stays, read-only, with "Antigravity keeps this with your Google account, not in this file. Change it with /config in Antigravity." Marshell never writes the key.
     3. If the live check shows that a running Antigravity session overwrites a change made in Marshell when it saves its settings, Review shows "Antigravity is running in 1 tab. If it saves its settings, it may undo this change." (or "in <n> tabs") while Antigravity runs in a tab, and Apply still works. If it doesn't overwrite, no warning is added. The live check may copy `oauth_creds.json` and `google_accounts.json`, and nothing else, from `~/.gemini` into its scratch home to start a signed-in Antigravity; the copies are deleted at stop.
     4. A value outside what a control offers is shown as it is. A switch offers "<value> (from the file)" as a selected choice. A number field is a text field that accepts digits only: a value like "abc" shows as it is, and a partial entry like "-" shows "Use a whole number of at least N." instead of meaning Not set.
     5. Focus goes back to the last edit. After Discard, it goes to the control changed last, or to the raw text box. After a successful Undo, it goes to a visible line "Put <path> back as it was." that can take focus (until now it was only announced). After Reload, it goes to the control or text box the person was in.
     6. Raw editing for every CLI keeps a file's byte-order mark on save, so a file that starts with one can be edited as text. For Claude Code and Antigravity, whose files are strict JSON, a file that isn't plain JSON (comments or a trailing comma) opens raw read-only with "Raw editing is off: this file isn't plain JSON, and Marshell saves it as plain JSON." The form still works. A file with a repeated key opens raw read-only with "Raw editing is off: a key appears twice in this file."
     7. In forced colours, a busy button in the company section shows as unavailable (`GrayText`), as it already does in the composer, Advanced and the export dialog.
     8. Approved copy from the plan review (2026-10-27): an Antigravity change's review is titled "Change Antigravity settings", and a request to write `enableTelemetry` is refused with the Telemetry row's own line.
     9. (2026-10-28) The live check couldn't run decision 3's probe: Antigravity's first run in a scratch home stops at its "Terms of Service & Data Use" screen, which the check doesn't answer for the person. Antigravity documents that it replaces its whole settings file in one step when it saves, so an overwrite is plausible, and the warning from decision 3 is added anyway. Whether it really overwrites stays open for a later live check.
   - **Slice 6h1 decisions (2026-10-28).** The `marshell` CLI, "Copy resume command" and "Open in system terminal" (6b decision 2's 6g, whose letter went to company tab parity).
     1. The slice runs as two. 6h1: the CLI (`list`, `send`, `new`, `focus`), "Copy resume command" and "Open in system terminal". 6h2: the built-in skill for Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity, after research on each CLI's skill folder, written through the config service. The later slices shift: 6i is repo scripts and project references, 6j is WSL and `marshell-host`.
     2. `marshell new --agent <cli> --prompt "..."` starts the session and fills its prompt line with the prompt, without pressing Enter, as `send` does (slice 6a decision 7). It never passes the prompt as the CLI's first-prompt argument, so it works the same for every CLI, including those with none.
     3. The CLI has `marshell focus <session>`, from the brief. A new local API route brings the window forward and selects the session.
     4. On Windows, "Open in system terminal" starts the resume command in a new console window in the session's folder. Windows 11 hands it to the default terminal app the person chose, so Marshell picks no terminal.
     5. "Copy resume command" copies the resume command by conversation id where Marshell knows one (`claude --resume <id>`, `codex resume <id>`, `agy --conversation <id>`), with the session's mode flags and a `cd` to its folder. With no id (a manifest agent, or no hook reported one), it copies the continue-last form, and the menu item says it continues the folder's last conversation.
     6. "Open in system terminal" is offered on ended and archived sessions. On a running session it is unavailable, with "End the session first.", so two CLIs never write the same conversation. "Copy resume command" works on every session.
     7. When Marshell isn't running, `new` and `focus` ask "Marshell isn't running. Start it? [y/N]" when stdin is a terminal and start it without asking on `--start`; otherwise they print that it isn't running and exit with an error. `list` and `send` say so and exit with an error.
     8. `marshell list` prints a plain table (number, name, agent, state, folder) and, with `--json`, a JSON document with stable fields for scripts and agents. 6h2's skill points agents at `--json`.
     9. `new --prompt` fills when the agent is ready. For Claude Code that is after its SessionStart hook, which runs only once the folder-trust dialog is answered, and then once its output is quiet. For every other CLI it is once output has been quiet for 1.5 s. A first-run dialog that sits quiet (Antigravity's terms, Codex's folder trust) can get the text, and the live check tries each CLI. When nothing is filled within 30 s, the session keeps running and the CLI says the prompt wasn't filled.
     10. Marshell adds `<home>/bin` to the PATH of the sessions it starts, in their environment only, so an agent in Marshell runs `marshell` by name. Nothing is written to the person's own PATH. From another terminal, people use the full path.
     11. "Open in system terminal" shows on every agent session. Choosing it on a running one opens nothing and shows the toast "End the session first." The session menu's rule (items that don't apply are left out, never disabled) is unchanged.
     12. Approved at plan review (2026-10-28): decision 5's hint moves from the menu item to the toast, because a session row doesn't know whether a hook reported an id. The item is always "Copy resume command", and the toast after copying reads "Copied the resume command.", "Copied. It continues the last conversation in this folder." or "Copied. It starts a new conversation: this one was never saved." The rest of the slice's copy is approved as the 6h1 plan's Global Constraints list it.
     13. Decided at the live check (2026-10-30, `docs/research/2026-10-28-marshell-cli-live-check.md`): an agent that has ended counts as ended. When an agent row's hooks report SessionEnd and the tab's shell lives on, `send` and `new --prompt` refuse it ("Session <n> has ended, so it can't take text."), `marshell list` shows it as `ended`, "Open in system terminal" opens it, and History's Resume no longer calls it running in a tab. A later SessionStart (a resume typed in the same tab) makes it running again. A plain shell tab follows its process. "Open in system terminal" starts the console with `CreateProcessW` and no standard handles of the app's, and refuses a profile whose program is a batch file for now. agy's first-run screen taking the fill is a known limit, not fixed.
     14. Approved after the live check (2026-10-30), as written: "Couldn't start Marshell: <io error>.", "Unknown mode `<x>`: use manual, plan, auto-edit, full-auto, bypass or dont-ask.", "Could not read the current folder: <e>", "--agent takes a value.", "focus takes a session number or a tab id.", "send takes a session and the text to put on its prompt line.", "Marshell answered <status>: <body>", "Marshell's answer didn't parse: <e>", the table header "# NAME AGENT STATE MODE CWD", "A plain terminal has no resume command.", "Its folder's name holds a line break or another control character, so there is no one-line command to copy.", "Its shell profile was removed.", "Couldn't open a system terminal: <message>", "Couldn't copy the resume command: <message>" and "Can't open `<program>` in a system terminal: batch files aren't supported." Also approved as written at the final review (2026-10-30): "A DeepSeek session needs Marshell to start it.", "focus takes one session.", "Unknown argument `<x>`." and "Marshell's window isn't there to focus."
     15. Decided at the final review (2026-10-30). The PowerShell copy line is `Set-Location -LiteralPath '<folder>' -ErrorAction Stop; <command>`, so a folder that is gone stops the line instead of starting the agent in the wrong one. `send` and `new --prompt` refuse a session waiting for an answer (a permission card held by Marshell, or a live state of needs permission or needs question): "Session <n> is waiting for an answer, so it can't take text." With `new --prompt` the fill keeps waiting until the 30 s are up. Decided at the live recheck: Claude sends no hook when a question or permission dialog is dismissed with Escape, so a key typed in the terminal since the dialog appeared ends the wait (a card Marshell holds still refuses). An arrow key inside a dialog that is still open ends it too, and the row keeps saying it waits until the next prompt. Marshell keeps the consoles "Open in system terminal" starts, in memory, per conversation (by id, or by agent and folder for a continue-last one), and while one runs, another Open, a Resume, a Restart or History's Resume of that conversation is refused with "It's open in a system terminal."; Fork starts a new conversation, so it goes on. The console gets the environment a session starts from, without Marshell's markers, the inherited Claude Code session variables or Marshell's `bin` folder, and its program is looked up on the tab PATH. "Its command holds a line break or another control character, so there is no one-line command to copy." is new copy for a manifest command with a line break. Known limits: the console is `pwsh -NoExit`, so Marshell follows the window, not the agent, and an `/exit` inside it still holds the conversation until the window closes; the record is forgotten when Marshell quits; a "Status limited" row has no hooks, so text can still land in its dialogs; a batch-file program is refused; agy's first-run screen takes the fill; a `claude -p` run inside a session inherits the tab's `MARSHELL_TAB_ID`, so if a headless run reports SessionEnd, the tab counts as ended while its own conversation still runs (not verified that it does).
     16. Decided at the pull request review (2026-10-30, the cloud review on PR #75). `send` and `new --prompt` also count as waiting a Claude Code row with Marshell's hooks before its first main-agent hook (it may still show the folder-trust dialog; any hook but SessionEnd counts, for a Claude that loaded the hooks after it started) and an Antigravity row whose status line reports its own tool confirmation (a key typed in the terminal clears it until the next status line). A session's PATH is Marshell's `bin` folder in front of the tab PATH (on Windows the registry's current one), not the app's PATH from launch, so an agent installed while Marshell runs is found. "Open in system terminal" starts Codex without `--no-daemon`, the same command "Copy resume command" copies. `marshell new` waits 30 s for the session, and the CLI reads its current folder only for a relative `--cwd`. Known limits: a nested `claude -p`'s SessionEnd also lets a second CLI open the tab's conversation (Open in system terminal, History Resume), since the tab counts as ended; a continue-last console (keyed by agent and folder) and a Resume by id of the same conversation don't see each other; a History open-outside racing a tab Resume can start both; environment variables that aren't valid UTF-8 are left out of the console.
   - **Slice 6h2 decisions (2026-10-30).** The built-in `marshell` skill (6h1 decision 1), after research on each CLI's skill folder (`docs/research/2026-10-30-cli-skill-folders.md`).
     1. The skill is installed at user level, once per CLI, so every session of that CLI has it: Claude Code `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR or ~/.claude>/skills/marshell/SKILL.md`, Codex `~/.agents/skills/marshell/SKILL.md`, Antigravity `~/.gemini/config/skills/marshell/SKILL.md`. Nothing is written into project folders.
     2. Codex's skill goes in `~/.agents/skills`, the folder Codex documents. `CODEX_HOME` doesn't move it, and the older `$CODEX_HOME/skills` is deprecated in Codex's source. Gemini-family CLIs may read `~/.agents/skills` too, so the live check looks for the skill listed twice in agy.
     3. It is offered beside the hooks: onboarding and each agent's Settings section offer "Install the marshell skill" through the config service's preview, diff, backup and undo. When a newer Marshell ships a newer skill, the row offers the update; a file the person edited shows the diff and is never overwritten silently. `marshell uninstall` removes it.
     4. The agent picks the skill itself (model-invoked) when asked to list, start, message or bring forward Marshell sessions. The body says it works only inside a Marshell session (`MARSHELL_TAB_ID` set), to read `marshell list --json`, that `send` and `new --prompt` never press Enter, and to send only to sessions the person named.
     5. One `SKILL.md` serves all three CLIs: the frontmatter holds only `name: marshell` and a quoted one-line `description`, the folder is named `marshell`, and the file stays well under agy's 24 KB limit.
     6. Decided at the reviews (2026-10-30, the final review and the cloud review on PR #76). A skill file the person edited is never removed from Settings (the remove plan is refused, as `marshell uninstall` leaves it), and replacing it opens the diff first. A skill Marshell can't touch (the config guard refuses its path, e.g. a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` outside the home and the projects; it can't be read; or it is over 4 MiB) is `unavailable`: its switch is off and shows why, the hooks plan leaves it out rather than fail, and `marshell uninstall` reports and skips a refused one while removing the hooks and the bin folder. Known limits: undoing a skill install leaves an empty `marshell` folder; focus falls to the Agents heading or the page after a skill action, as after the hooks actions; Claude Code's hooks path doesn't follow `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, though the skill does.
   - **Slice 6i1 decisions (2026-10-30).** Repo scripts (`marshell.json`, brief "Repo scripts"), the first half of 6i.
     1. 6i runs as two slices: 6i1 repo scripts, 6i2 project references. Each has its own plan, live check and pull request.
     2. Running a repo's scripts is running someone else's code, so Marshell asks once per repo: the first time, it shows the exact scripts and asks; after Allow they run without asking for that repo. When `marshell.json` changes, it asks again. The trust covers `setup`, `run` and `teardown` together.
     3. `setup` runs when Marshell creates a task worktree, in that worktree. A session started in the main checkout doesn't run it.
     4. `run` (a dev server) is started from a Run button in the session header and the command palette, as its own terminal tab beside the session. Port chips show the ports it opens, and Stop ends it.
     5. `teardown` runs in the worktree as the first step of the worktree's remove plan (shown with the other commands), before the worktree is deleted.
     6. Format: `{"setup": "…", "run": "…", "teardown": "…"}`, each script optional. Each value is a command string, or an object `{"windows": "…", "default": "…"}` for a team on mixed systems. A script runs in the session's shell (PowerShell on Windows, the login shell elsewhere).
     7. `setup` runs in its own visible tab, and the agent session starts after it whether it passed or failed. A failed setup leaves a warning on the session's row, with a way to open its output.
     8. Only `marshell.json` is read. Other tools' files (Conductor's `conductor.json`, Superset's) are left for later, with research first.
   - **Slice 6i2 decisions (2026-10-31).** Project references (brief §5.11), the second half of 6i. Research: `docs/research/2026-10-31-prompt-context-injection.md`.
     1. Scope: a per-project toggle, a list of aliases the person edits, and the prompt-time resolver. Alias learning from History and the `@` picker are left for a later slice.
     2. The resolver matches the prompt against the project's aliases only (not file names, not past sessions). No match means nothing is added.
     3. An alias points to a file or folder inside the project, checked to exist. A path that no longer exists shows as broken in the list and is never sent.
     4. Aliases live in the Marshell home (`projects/<id>/`), private to the person. Nothing is written into the repo.
     5. A new "Projects" section in Settings picks a project (from the recent folders), turns references on (off by default, brief §2) and edits its aliases. A palette command opens it for the focused session's project.
     6. After a prompt that got references, the session header shows a small chip with the exact note in its tooltip, reachable by keyboard. It clears on the next prompt.
     7. Claude Code and Codex get the note through `UserPromptSubmit`'s `additionalContext`. agy's only route is `PreInvocation`'s `injectSteps`, so a spike runs first in this slice: whether the model sees it, how often it fires, and whether it is saved or shown. If it works, agy gets references once per prompt like the others; if not, the Projects section says agy can't take references yet.
   - **Slice 6j1 decisions (2026-10-31).** Agents inside WSL (S11), the first half of 6j.
     1. 6j runs as two slices: 6j1 WSL agents, then 6j2 the `marshell-host` elevated helper. Each has its own plan, live check and pull request.
     2. Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity running inside a WSL distro get the same hook features as on Windows (live status, approvals, references), each checked by the S11 spike first. A CLI the spike shows can't be bridged gets a line in the UI saying so.
     3. The spike and the live check touch a distro only through scratch folders: each CLI's official Linux build is installed into a `/tmp` scratch folder with `HOME` pointed there, nothing lands in the person's Linux home, and the folder is deleted at the end with the person's OK. Codex's `auth.json` and agy's two sign-in files are copied from Windows into the scratch homes and deleted afterwards; Claude Code is signed in by browser.
     4. The separate elevated Marshell window (the interim fallback in the phase 6 scope line) is dropped. `marshell-host` is built directly in 6j2, and the launcher's elevated option appears only when it ships.
     Asked after the spike (`docs/spikes/S11-wsl-agents.md`). It found the hook path already works from a distro, and Claude Code and Codex cards work in a WSL tab today. What's missing comes from the core never learning which agent runs in a WSL tab, from Linux paths, and from Windows-only setup.
     5. The hooks tell the core which agent runs in a WSL tab. The first Claude Code, Codex or Antigravity hook from a WSL tab marks the tab as running that agent. The mark clears when the agent's session ends or the shell's prompt mark comes back. An agent without hooks in a WSL tab stays a plain shell row.
     6. A distro's hooks are set up per distro in Settings > Agents ("Set up in <distro>" per CLI). The write goes through `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\...` with the same preview, backup, managed-file and undo rules as on Windows, and the bridge is written as its Linux path (`/mnt/c/...`).
     7. History includes agent sessions run in WSL. A Linux transcript path is translated to `\\wsl.localhost\<the tab's distro>\...`, and that one UNC root is accepted. Every other UNC path stays refused.
     8. A WSL tab in a distro with no browser opener (no `wslview` or `xdg-open`) gets `BROWSER` set to a small Marshell script. It opens only `http` and `https` URLs, in the Windows default browser.
     9. The config guard admits a distro's writes only in its agent folders: `<distro home>\.claude`, `\.codex` and `\.gemini` under `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>`, for a detected WSL distro whose home Marshell has looked up. Everything else in the distro stays refused, and the denied list and managed-file rules still apply. (Asked during the slice, 2026-10-31.)
   - **Slice 6j2 decisions (2026-11-01).** The `marshell-host` elevated helper (S8), the second half of 6j.
     1. The core and the helper talk over one named pipe, not loopback TCP. The core creates it, single-instance and with a random name, before launching the helper through ShellExecute `runas`. Each side checks the other's process id: the core checks `GetNamedPipeClientProcessId` against the pid ShellExecute returned, and the helper checks `GetNamedPipeServerProcessId` against the `--core-pid` it was given. (As built, the helper does not check its parent or the server's image. A same-user process could start `marshell-host` for its own pipe, but it could just as well start any program of its own through UAC. This is a known limit, recorded 2026-11-02.) With no token and no port, nothing else can connect. That meets S8's token requirement by having no token at all, so the pipe name on the command line is not a secret.
     2. Any profile can start as administrator: shells, agents and tools. An elevated agent always starts in Manual mode, and switching it to Bypass needs an extra confirmation. Hooks, live status and approvals work as in any session.
     3. Elevation is never remembered. After a restart, an elevated row comes back ended, with its scrollback and a "Reopen as administrator" action that raises UAC only when chosen.
     4. Windows only. On macOS and Linux the Administrator option is shown disabled, with a line saying it is not available there yet. `sudo -i` and `pkexec` come later, once those platforms are verified.
     5. The launcher gets an "Administrator" toggle chip after Pace (a shield when on, Alt+A). The palette gets "New session as administrator", which opens the launcher with the chip on. The choice is never remembered per project.
     6. Approve cards in an elevated session show the shield and offer no "Always allow", so no admin command becomes a standing rule from Marshell.
     7. The live check shows a real UAC prompt: the user declines the first one and approves the rest. Automated tests cover the wrong-peer and single-connection paths, with the helper running unelevated in a test-only mode.
     8. An elevated session takes input, resize and approval answers only from the app's own window, over Tauri commands that call the in-process core. They never come from the token-guarded HTTP or WebSocket API, because any process of the same user can read the token in `run/endpoint.json`. The API refuses those writes for an elevated row, `marshell send` included, with a line saying why. Output still streams over the WebSocket. (Asked while planning, 2026-11-01.)
     9. A non-elevated app can't freeze or force-kill an admin process tree. So Pause is unavailable on an elevated row, with a line saying why, and Close drops the pipe: the helper exits, and its kill-on-close Job Object ends the shell and everything it started. (Asked while planning, 2026-11-01.)
     10. Starting elevated is window-only too. The API refuses an elevated create, and the restart, resume and Reopen of an elevated row, with a line saying why. The launcher, the palette, Reopen and the Bypass bar use Tauri commands. Resume and Reopen always start an elevated agent in Manual mode. (Asked after the final review, 2026-11-02.)
     11. The core reads each agent's own "skip every prompt" setting (Antigravity's `dangerouslySkipPermissions`, and the equivalents in other CLIs' settings), and the launcher and the restart bar treat such an agent as Bypass. Its row says Bypass, and starting it elevated needs the Bypass confirmation. This applies to unelevated sessions too. (Asked after the final review, 2026-11-02.)
     12. Decision 8 keeps the API and other tabs out of an elevated session. It is not a full boundary against a same-user process. This slice makes two cheap fixes: an elevated hook drops saved-rule updates, so there is no "Always allow" end to end, and it answers only an endpoint whose pid is a running Marshell. The remaining gaps are in the PR's known limits and a GitHub issue. (Asked after the final review, 2026-11-02.)
     13. A Codex too old to take `--no-daemon` can't start as administrator, because it could attach to an ordinary-user daemon. The chip is disabled for it with the reason. (Asked after the final review, 2026-11-02.)
   - **Slice 6k decisions (2026-11-03).** A maintenance slice after 6j2: issues #65, #62 and #63, the leads from PR #79's review that were checked and confirmed, and finding 10 of the 6j2 live check. Qwen Code support is the slice after it.
     1. #65: a terminal that isn't shown never fits itself. Its ResizeObserver and the font refit skip the fit while the tab is hidden, and the refit when the tab is shown again stays as it is. A hidden program keeps the size it had when it was last shown.
     2. #62: Codex's or Antigravity's section shows when its CLI is found on PATH or in WSL, as now, and also when the CLI isn't found but Marshell's entries are still in its files (its hooks state isn't "none"). Such a section says that the CLI wasn't found and that Marshell's hooks are still in the file, and its only action is "Hooks off".
     3. #63: appending a last array element and removing it again gives back the file's original bytes. The whitespace before `]` and a trailing comma are kept. The ignored Codex round-trip test is turned on, and Claude Code's hooks get the same test.
     4. From PR #79's review, only the leads confirmed against the code are fixed:
        - Resume, Restart and "Open in system terminal" of an ended WSL agent tab check the row's sidecar with its distro, so they resume the exact conversation instead of falling back to `--continue`.
        - `marshell-open` finds Windows through `%SystemRoot%` instead of assuming `C:\Windows`.
        - The default WSL distro is found without the English "(Default)" marker, and a failed lookup isn't cached for the life of the app.
        - The WSL setup and removal previews make one `wsl.exe` call per distro instead of two.
        - The adapter agent to program table (`claude`, `codex`, `agy`) has one copy, `profiles::detect::agent_command`.
     5. The core sends the WSL distros the Agents dialog may offer, by its own rule, and the frontend drops its rule. The frontend's rule fell back to a profile's name, so a bare `wsl.exe` profile got a button the core then refused.
     6. Leads that were refuted, or that have no effect anyone can see, are left as they are: the hook handler's `spawn_blocking` hops, the ended-session guard's `session_id` (agy has no SessionEnd hook), and `write_meta` keeping a stored distro.
     7. `marshell uninstall` leaves Marshell's hooks inside WSL distros. That is confirmed but deferred to its own issue, #82. When it is done, uninstall removes them in each distro, as the dialog's WSL "Hooks off" does, and names any distro that didn't answer.
     8. Finding 10 of the 6j2 live check: while the launcher is open, Ctrl+Shift+Alt+T (Reopen closed session) closes it and reopens the last closed session. Every other key stays the launcher's.
     9. The 6j2 deferred minors and #80 are not in this slice.
     10. The live check covers #65 (a hidden tab's size, read from the core) and #62 (a leftover Codex hook set with Codex off PATH, in a scratch `CODEX_HOME`).
   - **Slice 6l1 decisions (2026-11-03).** Qwen Code support, after a read of Qwen Code 0.26.5's bundled source (recorded by the spike in `docs/research/2026-11-03-qwen-code-hooks.md`).
     1. Qwen Code support runs as two slices, each with its own plan, live check and pull request. 6l1 is the adapter: hooks, live status, approve cards, the chained status line, resume by id and project references. 6l2 is the company tab, Accounts, CLI version and update, the built-in skill, History import and WSL.
     2. Qwen moves from the manifest tier to the adapter tier (6b decision 1) and becomes the fourth CLI Marshell installs hooks for. Its launch, continue and mode flags stay as its manifest had them.
     3. The hooks go in the `hooks` key of Qwen's user settings (`<QWEN_HOME or ~/.qwen>/settings.json`), through the config service: consent card, preview, backup, undo, orphan detection and `marshell uninstall`, as for Claude Code's `settings.json`. Marshell never writes a project `.qwen/settings.json` or the system settings file, and doesn't use `QWEN_CODE_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`.
     4. Approve cards come from Qwen's `PermissionRequest` hook, which fires only when Qwen would show its own dialog. Allow and Deny answer it. A release or a timeout returns no decision, so Qwen shows its own dialog.
     5. "Always allow" is refused for Qwen, because Qwen 0.26.5 parses a hook's `updatedPermissions` but never applies it: "Qwen Code can't keep an always-allow rule from Marshell. Allow this once instead."
     6. The status line is chained, as Antigravity's is. Marshell's relay becomes `ui.statusLine`, the person's own command is kept in `~/.marshell/agents/qwen/statusline-chain.json` and run after the relay, and removal puts it back. Qwen rows show model and context %.
     7. Project references reach Qwen through `UserPromptSubmit`'s `additionalContext`, checked by the spike. If the model doesn't see the note, the Projects section says Qwen can't take references yet.
     8. The spike and the live check run Qwen against a fake OpenAI-compatible model server on loopback in the scratch folder: no account, no key, no cost, and the same replies every run.
   - **Slice 6l2 decisions (2026-11-03).** The second half of Qwen Code support (6l1 decision 1), after a read of Qwen Code 0.26.5's bundled source (`docs/research/2026-11-03-qwen-code-surfaces.md`).
     1. Accounts: Qwen Code 0.26.5 has no sign-in status command (`qwen auth` was removed, and login is `/auth` inside a Qwen session). Its section says "Qwen Code has no sign-in status command, so Marshell can't show one. Sign in with /auth in a Qwen Code session." Below that line, it shows the auth type that `security.auth.selectedType` in Qwen's user settings names, labelled as what the settings choose, never as signed in. There is no Sign in button, and Marshell reads nothing from `oauth_creds.json` or from running sessions.
     2. The CLI version and Update follow 6e decisions 9 to 11. The standalone install (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\qwen-code`) updates with `qwen update`, and an npm install with `npm install -g @qwen-code/qwen-code@latest`. Qwen keeps no newer-version data on disk (its check is a live registry call), so Settings shows the installed version only.
     3. The skill: the `marshell` skill goes in Qwen's own folder, `<QWEN_HOME or ~/.qwen>/skills/marshell/SKILL.md`, with its own row and switch, as for the other CLIs (6h2 decisions 1 to 6), and the same `SKILL.md`. Qwen also reads `~/.agents/skills`, where Codex's copy goes, so the live check looks for the skill listed twice and records what Qwen does.
     4. History: full parity with Claude Code and Codex. Qwen conversations show in History with transcript view and search, and the importer copies the conversations run outside Marshell from `<QWEN_RUNTIME_DIR, else the Qwen home>/projects/<slug>/chats/<id>.jsonl`, under the same import switch.
     5. The company tab is editable like the other three, for `<QWEN_HOME or ~/.qwen>/settings.json`. The form's curated keys: `model.name` (text), `tools.approvalMode` (`plan`, `default`, `auto-edit`, `auto`, `yolo`), `general.enableAutoUpdate` (switch), `privacy.usageStatisticsEnabled` (switch), `ui.theme` (text) and `general.vimMode` (switch). Masked with no reveal: `security.auth.apiKey`, everything under `env` and `modelProviders`, `mcpServers.*.env` and `mcpServers.*.headers`, besides 6e decision 15's name rule.
     6. Raw editing stays on for a file with comments or trailing commas, because Qwen's settings file is JSONC and Qwen writes it keeping its format. The raw text is saved as typed once it parses as JSONC, and form changes keep the comments. A repeated key still makes raw read-only. Amended 2026-11-04, after the final review: Qwen 0.26.5 loads the file with `JSON.parse(stripJsonComments(stripUtf8Bom(text)))`, and strip-json-comments leaves trailing commas, so a file with one fails to load. A raw save therefore accepts only what Qwen loads: comments and one leading BOM, and otherwise strict JSON with an object at the top. A refused save names the line. A file with comments or trailing commas still opens for raw editing.
     7. WSL: Qwen in a distro gets "Set up in <distro>" like the other CLIs (6j1 decisions 5 to 9), and the config guard admits `<distro home>\.qwen`. The live check installs `@qwen-code/qwen-code@0.26.5` with npm into a scratch prefix under `/tmp`, with `HOME` there, and runs it against the fake model. If the distro has no Node 22, a scratch Node tarball goes in the same folder.
     8. The 6l1 deferred minors fixed here: a leading `~` in `QWEN_HOME` is expanded, as Qwen does; turning approvals off and then Hooks off leaves no empty `PermissionRequest` array; the restart bar names the command a restart really runs (`--resume <id>` where Marshell knows the id; on a PowerShell profile it leaves off the `& ` call operator before a bare program name, amended 2026-11-04); a leading `~` in `QWEN_RUNTIME_DIR` is expanded too, as Qwen does (amended 2026-11-04, after the final review); and a preview or nightly version string is read.
     9. (Asked at plan review, 2026-11-03.) Qwen's tab masks leaves only, because 6e decision 15's name rule would hide all of `security.auth` (with the harmless `selectedType`), `general.gitCoAuthor` and token limits, and so turn raw editing off for almost every Qwen file. For Qwen alone, the tab hides the paths in decision 5, a hook's `env`, `telemetry` headers, and any string value whose own key looks secret. An object with a secret-looking name is walked into, not hidden whole, and numbers and booleans are never hidden. Claude Code, Codex and Antigravity keep today's rule. Following 6l1 decision 3, the system file is read only at `C:\ProgramData\qwen-code\settings.json`, never through `QWEN_CODE_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`.
   - **Slice 6m decisions (2026-11-04).** A maintenance slice after 6l2: issues #85 and #82, and the deferred minors from slices 6j2, 6k, 6l1 and 6l2 that were checked against the code and confirmed. The slice's plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-04-phase-6m-maintenance.md`) carries the evidence for each. Phase 7 (Doctor) is next, starting with slice 7a (below).
     1. One slice, with one plan, one live check and one pull request. The final review is split by area across several reviewers.
     2. #82: `marshell uninstall` looks in every registered WSL distro (Docker Desktop's left out), starting a stopped one, and in any distro that Marshell's `managed.json` names. In each, it removes what the Agents dialog's WSL "Hooks off" removes, for all four CLIs (6k decision 7). A distro that doesn't answer within 20 s is named with "start it and run marshell uninstall again", and the exit code is 1. `<home>/bin` then stays only when `managed.json` says Marshell wrote in that distro, and is removed otherwise. Amended 2026-11-04 (user OK after the final review): a distro that `managed.json` names but that WSL's list no longer has, when that list isn't empty, gets "isn't installed in WSL any more: nothing to remove there." and doesn't keep `bin`. An empty list still keeps `bin`, because WSL may have failed.
     3. #82's live check imports a throwaway distro from Canonical's Ubuntu rootfs, sets hooks up there from a scratch Marshell, and runs uninstall with a scratch `MARSHELL_HOME`, answering n first. It applies only when the preview names no file outside the throwaway distro. The distro is unregistered afterwards, after asking.
     4. `marshell list --json` gets `"elevated": true/false`, and the table gets an ADMIN column only when at least one listed session is elevated. This amends 6h1 decision 8's column list.
     5. Approve-card arming: a pointer press that focuses a card that was already armed answers it. The card still re-arms on mount, when its content changes, and on keyboard or programmatic focus. This amends the Keyboard safety rule in "The approve card". The live check first tries to reproduce the dead first "Allow once" click from the 6l2 live check.
     6. A permission request is held for an approve card only when the tab's detected agent is the hook's own agent, for Claude Code, Codex and Qwen Code as for Antigravity (6d). Otherwise the CLI asks in its own prompt. A request that arrives before Marshell has seen the agent in the tab gets no card.
     7. Hooks off deletes `~/.marshell/agents/<cli>/statusline-chain.json` for Claude Code, Antigravity and Qwen Code, in the same change as the hook removal, so Undo brings it back. The removal preview and `marshell uninstall` list the file.
     8. When hook setup turns a one-line settings file into several lines, the preview says so under the diff: "This file was on one line. Marshell lays it out over several lines." This applies to every JSON settings file Marshell edits.
     9. The other confirmed minors are fixed as the plan's tasks say, following rules already set: AGENTS.md's parity rule, 6k decision 2 (a leftover section's only action is "Hooks off") and 6l2 decision 8 (no empty `PermissionRequest` array). The Qwen system settings file is read at each OS's own path from the 6l2 research note, still never through `QWEN_CODE_SYSTEM_SETTINGS_PATH`.
     10. Left as they are: #80 and #23; Qwen's V1 settings layout, WSL Qwen chat import and the skill inside a distro (features, not fixes); trimming `QWEN_HOME`; systemd's OSC 3008 marks (ignored, as now); and #62's live check, which needs a PC without Codex.
   - **Slice 6n1 decisions (2026-11-06).** GitHub Copilot CLI support, after a read of Copilot CLI 1.0.106's bundled source (the winget `copilot.exe` unpacks itself into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\copilot\pkg\win32-x64\1.0.106\`) and its docs, recorded in `docs/research/2026-11-06-copilot-cli.md`. The user chose on 2026-11-06 to build it after 7c2 and before 7d, so 7d covers five CLIs. Decisions 1, 3, 5 and 8 were asked; 2, 4, 6 and 7 follow Qwen Code's (6l1 decisions 2, 4, 6 and 7) under AGENTS.md's parity rule; 9 to 11 were stated with the questions and not contested. Decisions 12 to 14 came from findings of the plan's spike, put to the user on 2026-11-06 at its decision gate. Each time the user took the recommended option.
     1. Copilot CLI support runs as three slices, each with its own plan, live check and pull request. 6n1 is the adapter: hooks, live status, approve cards (dropped by decision 12), the chained status line, resume by id and project references. 6n2 is the settings tab, Accounts, CLI version and update, the built-in skill, History import and WSL. 6n3 adds Copilot to the Doctor's checks from 7a to 7c: hooks, transcript retention, the CLI doctor line, duplicate notifications, and MCP servers and skills.
     2. Copilot moves from the manifest tier to the adapter tier (6b decision 1) and becomes the fifth CLI Marshell installs hooks for. Its launch, continue and mode flags stay as its manifest had them.
     3. The hooks go in a file Marshell owns, `<COPILOT_HOME or ~/.copilot>/hooks/marshell.json`, which Copilot loads with every other `*.json` file in that folder. The file goes through the config service: consent card, preview, backup, undo, orphan detection and `marshell uninstall`, and removal deletes it. Marshell never writes the inline `hooks` key of Copilot's `settings.json`, a repo's `.github/hooks/`, or Copilot's policy locations. It writes `settings.json` only for the chained status line (decision 6).
     4. ~~Approve cards come from Copilot's `permissionRequest` hook. Allow and Deny answer it. A release or a timeout returns no decision, so Copilot shows its own dialog; the spike checks what an empty reply does.~~ Replaced by decision 12.
     5. ~~"Always allow" is refused for Copilot, because a hook's reply can't save a rule in Copilot 1.0.106: "Copilot CLI can't keep an always-allow rule from Marshell. Allow this once instead."~~ Replaced by decision 12: with no cards, there is nothing to refuse.
     6. The status line is chained, as Antigravity's and Qwen's are. Marshell's relay becomes `statusLine.command` in Copilot's `settings.json`, the person's own command is kept in `~/.marshell/agents/copilot/statusline-chain.json` and run after the relay, and removal puts it back.
     7. Project references reach Copilot through `userPromptSubmitted`'s `additionalContext`, checked by the spike. If the model doesn't see the note, the Projects section says Copilot can't take references yet. The spike saw the note reach the model, appended to the prompt as a `<system_reminder>`.
     8. The spike and the live check run Copilot against a fake OpenAI-compatible model server on loopback (`COPILOT_PROVIDER_BASE_URL`, which Copilot's help says needs no GitHub sign-in) with a scratch `COPILOT_HOME`: no account, no cost, and the same replies every run.
     9. Marshell reads Copilot's own camelCase hook events with Copilot code. It doesn't use the PascalCase names, which switch Copilot to Claude-shaped payloads, so Claude Code's conventions stay out of Copilot.
     10. Marshell starts each new Copilot session with an id it chooses (`--session-id <uuid>`) and resumes with `--resume=<id>`, so it never has to discover the id.
