The spike found that `/clear` goes on under a new id in the same process, so Marshell follows the id the status line reports from then on.
     11. The VS Code `copilot-shim` is never taken for Copilot (6b's `reject_paths`). Marshell uses a `copilot` on PATH outside the shim, or the winget install folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\GitHubCopilotCLI\`.
     12. **No approve cards for Copilot; Copilot asks in its own terminal.** This replaces decisions 4 and 5.
         - Why: `permissionRequest` fires before Copilot's own rules, with the same body, for requests Copilot then allows by itself. That covers `echo`, `git status`, `Get-Content`, and anything under `--allow-all-tools`, `/allow-all` or a "don't ask again". A card would ask about calls Copilot never asks about.
         - Marshell installs no `permissionRequest` entry.
         - A Copilot row shows Needs permission from the `notification` hook's `permission_prompt`, which fires only when Copilot's own dialog shows. The lane shows its one-liner for a session asking in its own terminal.
         - The consent card's approve switch is off, with the reason: "Copilot CLI asks in its own terminal. Its hook can't tell which requests Copilot would allow by itself."
     13. **`marshell uninstall` keeps `~/.marshell/bin` while Copilot's hooks file may remain.**
         - Why: a `preToolUse` entry whose exe is missing makes Copilot refuse every tool call, even under `--allow-all-tools`.
         - A `marshell.json` uninstall can't read, plan or delete keeps the folder, as Codex's, Antigravity's and Qwen's hooks already do, and the note names Copilot CLI.
         - Orphaned Copilot hooks say they block every tool call, as Antigravity's do.
     14. **A tab's inherited Windows Terminal variables are tracked in issue #100, not fixed in 6n1.**
         - What happens: a Marshell started from Windows Terminal passes `WT_SESSION` and `WT_PROFILE_ID` to its tabs, so Copilot offered to set up Windows Terminal from inside a Marshell tab.
         - The live-check script clears both variables before it starts the app.
     15. **Decided at the final review (2026-11-07): a Copilot setup with "Keep my status line working" off reads Installed.**
         - Marshell then writes only `hooks\marshell.json` and leaves the person's own status line as it is, as Qwen's setup does. The Agents section had read Partial from then on.
         - Without Marshell's status line, Marshell can't follow a session past `/clear`. Copilot sends `sessionEnd` (`user_exit`) for both `/clear` and `/exit`, and only the status line names the new id. The row reads Ended until the next prompt.
         - The consent card says so when that switch is off: "With your own status line left as it is, a Copilot CLI row reads Ended after /clear until your next prompt."
   - **Slice 6n2 decisions (2026-11-20).** The second Copilot CLI slice (6n1 decision 1): the settings tab, Accounts, the version and Update, the `marshell` skill, History and WSL. The facts are in `docs/research/2026-11-06-copilot-cli.md`, part 2. Decisions 1 to 4 were asked of the user on 2026-11-20, when the slice was planned, and each time the user took the recommended option. Decisions 5 to 9 and 11 follow Qwen Code's (6l2 decisions 2 to 7 and 9) under AGENTS.md's parity rule. Decisions 10 and 12 to 14 are what the plan settled without asking. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-20-phase-6n2-copilot-surfaces.md`.
     1. **One slice,** with one plan, one live check and one pull request, as 6n1 decision 1 said and as Qwen's 6l2 ran.
     2. **Accounts.** Copilot CLI has no sign-in status command (`copilot login` is its only account subcommand; `/login`, `/logout` and `/user` work inside a session). Its section shows:
        - "Copilot CLI has no sign-in status command, so Marshell can't show one."
        - The account that Copilot's own `config.json` names in `lastLoggedInUser`, as "Copilot's own file names <login> as the last account signed in." (with "<login> on <host>" for a host other than github.com). It is never shown as signed in. Marshell reads the login and the host from that file and nothing else, and the file is never written.
        - A line when `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_TOKEN` or `GITHUB_TOKEN` is set for Marshell, the first of them in that order: "<NAME> is set, and Copilot CLI uses it before a stored sign-in."
        - A "Sign in to Copilot CLI" button. It opens a tab that runs `copilot login`, kept out of the recording and History like the other sign-in tabs (6e decision 16), and the section is checked again when the tab exits.
        - Limit: this PC has no Copilot sign-in, so the account line is tested with a file built from Copilot's source (`loggedInUsers` holds `host` and `login`), not a real one.
     3. **The form's six keys,** for `<COPILOT_HOME or ~/.copilot>/settings.json`: `model` (text), `defaultPermissionMode` (`manual`, `assisted`, `allow-all`), `autoUpdate` (switch), `autoUpdatesChannel` (`stable`, `prerelease`), `theme` (text) and `includeCoAuthoredBy` (switch). Every other key shows under "Other settings in this file" and in the raw view.
     4. **Three leftovers from 6n1 go in.**
        - Copilot's `elicitation_dialog` notification sets the row to Needs question.
        - A `COPILOT_HOME` outside the user profile is admitted by the config guard: that one folder, for Copilot's files. The user's OK for the change to `guard.rs` was given with this answer. Decision 12 has the rule.
        - 6n1's deferred minors, each checked against the code first: `/clear` announcing "has ended" for about 0.17 s, the consent card's approve-needs-status line showing for Copilot, a PATH that holds only the VS Code shim leaving Copilot out with no message, and a `marshell.json` with empty hook lists reading as Marshell's. One that the code doesn't confirm is dropped, and the plan says why. All four were confirmed and fixed (Task 9): an end the person asked for (`/exit` and `/clear` send the same one) waits 500 ms before the row reads Ended and is dropped when the status line names a new session in that time, so `/exit` reads Ended half a second later than before; the shim-only message reaches the Agents section and the launcher through one optional sentence on each of their protocol types, written in Copilot's code; and a hooks file with no entries is Marshell's only when its text is the one Marshell writes. The final review's fix pass has the core remember which session each Copilot tab named last, so the held end is dropped with History off too, and when History's index is slower than the hold.
     5. **The version and Update** follow 6e decisions 9 to 11. The standalone exe in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\GitHubCopilotCLI` (which is also what WinGet installs, outside WinGet's `Packages` folder) updates with `copilot update`, and an npm install with `npm install -g @github/copilot@latest`. Copilot keeps no "newer version" record on disk: a version it has downloaded is a package folder it starts by itself the next time it runs. So Settings shows the installed version only.
     6. **The skill** goes in Copilot's own folder, `<COPILOT_HOME or ~/.copilot>/skills/marshell/SKILL.md`, with its own row and switch and the same `SKILL.md` (6h2 decisions 1 to 6). Copilot also reads `~/.agents/skills`, where Codex's copy goes, and takes the first skill it finds by name, so the live check installs both and records what Copilot lists.
     7. **History: full parity** with Claude Code, Codex and Qwen Code. Copilot conversations show in History with the transcript view and search, and the importer copies the ones run outside Marshell from `<Copilot home>/session-state/<id>/events.jsonl`, under the same import switch. Only `events.jsonl` is copied: `workspace.yaml`, `plan.md`, `checkpoints` and `files` are left. A conversation's title is its first prompt, because the name `/rename` gives is kept in `workspace.yaml`.
     8. **The company tab is editable** like the other four. Copilot loads its settings as JSONC, with comments and trailing commas, so raw editing stays on for such a file, the raw text is saved as typed once it parses that way with an object at the top, and form changes keep the comments. A repeated key still makes raw read-only. The plan's first task checks what Copilot does with a byte-order mark and whether Copilot's own writes (`/model`, `copilot config`) keep comments. If the live check shows that a running Copilot undoes a change made in Marshell, Review gets the warning 6g decision 3 gave Antigravity.
     9. **Copilot's tab masks leaves only,** as Qwen's does (6l2 decision 9). 6e decision 15's name rule would hide `includeCoAuthoredBy` (its name holds "auth"), which is one of the form's six keys, and `storeTokenPlaintext`, which is a switch. Hidden with no reveal: everything under a key named `env` or `headers`, everything under `sandbox.credentials`, a `telemetry` key whose name holds "headers", `proxyUrl`, and any string whose own key looks secret (`sandbox.userPolicy.network.proxy.password`, for one). An object with a secret-looking name is walked into, and numbers and booleans are never hidden.
     10. **Copilot's managed settings are not read.** An organization can set them in three places (`%ProgramFiles%\GitHubCopilot\managed-settings.json`, the registry under `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Policies\GitHubCopilot` and a cache of settings its server sends), and the research could not confirm which wins over the user file. So a row an organization sets isn't marked read-only, as Claude Code's and Qwen's are. The config guard still refuses to write a managed file.
     11. **WSL.** Copilot in a distro gets "Set up in <distro>" like the other CLIs (6j1 decisions 5 to 9), and the launcher offers it for a distro whose PATH has it (6o decision 5). The config guard admits `<distro home>\.copilot`, the hooks file is `<distro home>/.copilot/hooks/marshell.json` with the bridge by its Linux path, and the status line there has no chain. A `COPILOT_HOME` set inside the distro isn't followed, as for the other CLIs' overrides there. A conversation run in a Marshell WSL tab shows in History (6j1 decision 7); one run in a distro outside Marshell isn't imported, as for Qwen (6m decision 10). The live check found that the one run in a Marshell WSL tab is listed without its turns, as Codex's and Qwen's are: for these three CLIs only the importer copies a conversation, and it reads no distro. The same holds on Windows while "Copy conversations run outside Marshell" is off. Slice 6s fixes both, the missing turns in a WSL tab and with the import switch off (its decisions 1 to 4). The plan's first task and the live check install `@github/copilot` with npm into a scratch prefix under `/tmp`, with `HOME` and `COPILOT_HOME` there, and run it against the fake model. If that task shows Copilot's hooks can't reach the bridge from a distro, the UI says so (6j1 decision 2) and the plan changes at its decision gate.
     12. **The guard's rule for decision 4.** `COPILOT_HOME` opens the folder it names when it is set, absolute, free of `..` and not a drive root. Nothing beside that folder opens. The other refusals stay in front of it: Marshell's own home, the organization folders and a managed file name are refused there as anywhere. At the plan's decision gate the user gave `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME` and `QWEN_HOME` the same rule (2026-11-20), so each CLI's own home folder opens when its variable names one, read the way that CLI's code already reads the variable (Qwen's `~` included). Antigravity has no such variable. Task 8 kept the rule narrower than it was asked (the plan's own call): on Windows the folder must be on one of the PC's drives, so a home on a network share adds nothing, and neither does one in a WSL distro's share, where decision 11's five folders are the rule. The final review's fix pass made that hold for where the folder really is as well as for how it is written, so a drive letter mapped to a share and a link that leads into one add nothing. Slice 6s decision 6 adds one refusal: a folder that holds the user profile or Marshell's home.
     13. **`elicitation_dialog`** reads "Has a question", the phrase Claude Code's question state has. It clears on the next prompt, tool result or end of turn, as Copilot's permission prompt does. Esc in the dialog fires no hook (a 6n1 known limit), so the row then keeps the state until the next event.
     14. **Accounts says nothing about a custom provider.** With `COPILOT_PROVIDER_BASE_URL` set, Copilot needs no GitHub sign-in, and the section doesn't say so.
     15. **A Copilot conversation run outside Marshell is labelled Terminal** (the user, at the plan's decision gate, 2026-11-20). Copilot's event log says `producer: "copilot-agent"` for `copilot -p` and for an interactive run alike, so nothing is guessed and a `-p` run shows in History without the headless filter, as a Qwen chat does (6m).
     16. **What the plan's first task settled** (`docs/research/2026-11-06-copilot-cli.md`, "What 6n2's first task saw"). Copilot loads a `settings.json` with comments, a trailing comma or a byte-order mark, so the company tab's raw editor accepts all three. Copilot's own change of a setting writes the whole file again without them, which Marshell can't prevent, and a run that changes nothing doesn't write the file, so the tab carries no running warning (decision 8). The question dialog fires `notification` with `elicitation_dialog` as documented (decision 13). In a distro, Copilot's hooks and status line run a Windows exe by its `/mnt/c/...` path and hand it the body, so decision 11 stands as written. The fixtures cut the text of Copilot's own system prompt from each recorded log (mine).
     17. **The last account's shape is unconfirmed.** Nobody is signed in to Copilot CLI on the development PC, so `lastLoggedInUser` in `config.json` is known only from strings in Copilot's runtime. The reader takes a `login` and a `host` where it finds them and shows no account line otherwise (decision 2).
   - **Slice 6n3 decisions (2026-11-24).** The third Copilot CLI slice (6n1 decision 1): Copilot CLI in every Doctor check from 7a to 7c2. The facts are in `docs/research/2026-11-06-copilot-cli.md`, part 3, for Copilot CLI 1.0.106. Decisions 1 to 4 were asked of the user on 2026-11-24, and each time the user took the recommended option. Decisions 5 to 13 follow the Doctor's own decisions (7a, 7b, 7c) under AGENTS.md's parity rule. Decisions 14 to 18 are what the plan settled without asking. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-24-phase-6n3-copilot-doctor.md`.
     1. **One slice,** with one plan, one live check and one pull request, as 6n1 decision 1 said.
     2. **Copilot's own toasts are Broken, with a fix.** Copilot's setting `notifications: true` raises Windows toasts under its own name (app id `GitHub.Copilot.CLI`), beside Marshell's banners. The item is pre-checked, and its fix sets `notifications` to false in `<Copilot home>/settings.json`, with preview, backup and Undo. This is what 7b decision 4 first set for Antigravity, whose toasts turned out not to exist. Like every `notify.*` item, it shows only while Marshell's banners are on. The live check first confirms that the toast shows from a Marshell tab; if it doesn't, there is no item.
     3. **Copilot's built-in GitHub MCP server is treated like any server.** It is listed and named as built in, it counts in the 30-day usage check, and an unused one is a Waste item whose fix adds `github-mcp-server` to `disabledMcpServers` in the user's `settings.json`. Measure says "Not measured: remote server." If the plan's fact check shows that the list doesn't turn a built-in off, the item is report-only (7c decision 3).
     4. **Issue #154 goes in, and the `disableAllHooks` check doesn't.** The live check runs Copilot in a distro, types `/clear` in both kinds of tab and, if the row reads Ended or loses its agent, gives the WSL branch the 500 ms hold. A Doctor item for a CLI's `disableAllHooks` is new for every CLI, so it is issue #188.
     5. **Hooks** follow 7a decision 5. Marshell's `hooks/marshell.json` that is gone, incomplete or pointing at a moved bridge is Broken and pre-checked, and the fix sets the hooks up again through Copilot's own setup plan. A Copilot whose hooks were never set up gets "Hooks aren't set up for Copilot CLI." Copilot has no hook trust step.
     6. **Transcript retention** follows 7a decision 6. Copilot has no setting that deletes sessions by age (they go only with `/session prune` or `/session delete`), so it gets no item, and Claude Code's item names Copilot CLI with Codex and Qwen Code.
     7. **The CLI doctor line** follows 7b decision 2: "Copilot CLI has no doctor command." `/diagnose` needs a model and the network, and the `list --json` subcommands are not a doctor.
     8. **A notification hook the person added** follows 7b decision 4: an Info item that names the command and is never edited. Copilot has the `notification` event. The entries read are those of every `*.json` in `<Copilot home>/hooks` except Marshell's own file, and of the `hooks` key in the user's `settings.json`.
     9. **Which configs are read** follows 7c decision 7:
        - Servers: `<Copilot home>/mcp-config.json`, and `.mcp.json` in each of Marshell's projects. A project's `.github/mcp.json` is not read: Copilot 1.0.106 doesn't load it (the first task's check).
        - Skills: `<Copilot home>/skills` and `~/.agents/skills` (which Copilot skips when `COPILOT_HOME` is set), and `.github/skills`, `.agents/skills` and `.claude/skills` in each project.
        - Plugins: the servers and skills of each installed plugin that is on, each item named with its plugin and given no fix (7c decision 6).
        - In a WSL distro, user level only.
        - Copilot's own bundled skills are not listed, as Qwen Code's aren't. `.claude/commands` files are not skills and are not listed.
     10. **The turn-off fix** follows 7c decision 8 and writes only the user's `settings.json`: a server's name goes in `disabledMcpServers` and a skill's in `disabledSkills`, for user and project items alike. Both lists match by name, so 7c decision 12's rule applies: an item that shares its name with another in use is report-only.
     11. **Broken and long skills** follow 7c decisions 3 and 9 by what Copilot does with the file: a skill Copilot refuses to load is Broken, and a description Copilot cuts is Waste and report-only. The first task's check found: Copilot refuses a skill whose name is over 64 characters or has a character outside its rule, whose description is over 1,024 characters, or whose frontmatter block doesn't close, and all four are Broken. It cuts nothing. A skill with no `name` loads under its folder's name and one with no `description` loads with its body's first line, so neither is Broken.
     12. **Usage** follows 7c decisions 4, 13 to 15. Copilot's history is `<Copilot home>/session-state/<id>/events.jsonl`: a `tool.execution_start` with `mcpServerName` is a server's use, and a `skill.invoked` is a skill's, matched by the `SKILL.md` path it names (both recorded by the first task, the skill's for a model's call and for a typed `/name`). Copilot never deletes sessions by itself, so its history covers the 30 days whenever the oldest session is that old.
     13. **Measure and WSL** follow 7c decisions 17 and 20, 7a decision 7 and 7b decision 5. Measure starts a user-level server, and a project's server only when Copilot's `config.json` lists the folder, or a folder above it, under `trustedFolders` (Copilot compares without case and with either slash); any other gets "Not measured: Copilot CLI hasn't approved it." A distro's servers are not measured and its history is not scanned, as for the other CLIs.
     14. **The toast item needs Marshell's hooks, and is Windows only.** Without Marshell's hooks set up for Copilot, Marshell raises no banner for a Copilot session, so nothing arrives twice and there is no item. In a distro the setting isn't checked: whether a toast reaches the Windows desktop from a distro is not known.
     15. **The built-in server is listed whenever Copilot loads it.** The first task's check found it loaded and its tools sent to the model with nobody signed in, so the first reading (only for a signed-in account) was wrong. It is not listed with `COPILOT_OFFLINE` set or when the person's own config has a server of that name. `computer-use`, which `copilot mcp list` shows and whose tools never reach the model, is not listed.
     16. **The servers' cost counts the tools' full definitions.** That is what Copilot sent in the first task's check, with `toolSearch` on and off. Copilot may load tools on demand for some models, by a server-side flag Marshell can't read, so the cost line says the count is in full and may be less. (First written the other way round, before the check.)
     17. **A fix of Copilot's hooks keeps its status line.** The status line is in `settings.json`, beside the hooks file, and a distro install writes one too (6n2 decision 11). The fix asks for the status line whenever it is Marshell's now or `managed.json` says Marshell set it, on Windows and in a distro.
     18. **Formats seen and not seen** (7c decision 16). The plan's first task records an MCP call and a skill use with a real Copilot against the fake model. What can't be recorded there (a signed-in `config.json`, a plugin's items) is judged from Copilot's schema and source, and the research note says which.
     19. **The skill listing's budget is reported** (7c decision 9's listing line, in Copilot's form). Copilot describes skills up to 15,000 characters of listing and only names the rest. When a person's skills pass that, a Waste item says how many are listed by name only, with no fix, and the cost counts those by name.
     20. **A hooks file that isn't Marshell's any more is kept or replaced, as the person says** (the user's choices, 2026-11-24, in three rounds). `hooks/marshell.json` is Marshell's whole file. When Marshell set the hooks up and the file now holds anything Marshell didn't write, the Doctor says so in a Broken item with two ways out:
         - **Keep my file** (a button on the item). Marshell remembers the person's part of the file (everything but Marshell's own entries) as accepted. From then on Marshell changes only its own entries in that file: a hooks fix, "Set up" and "Turn off" put back or take out Marshell's entries and leave the rest. If the person's part changes again, the item comes back.
         - **The fix** replaces the file with Marshell's hooks, after the usual preview, diff and backup, and Undo brings the old file back. It is not pre-checked, because it overwrites what a person wrote.
         - A kept file shows an Info row, "Copilot CLI's hooks file has your changes", with **Stop keeping**, which brings the item back.
         - A kept file that lacks some of Marshell's hooks still gets the Broken hooks item, not pre-checked, whose fix repairs only Marshell's entries.
         - A file that doesn't parse or isn't text is reported with neither; a read-only one can be kept and not replaced. Until a file is kept, "Set up" on the Agents page refuses it, as before. Other picked fixes go through either way.
         - The record is in Marshell's own folder (`agents/copilot/kept-hooks.json`), so keeping writes nothing of Copilot's. The same goes for a distro's file.
     21. **The first task's check confirmed decisions 2, 3 and 10:** Copilot's toast shows from a Marshell tab whenever the tab's terminal doesn't have the focus, `disabledMcpServers` turns the built-in server off, and both lists match a name exactly, case included.
     22. **Asked again after the first task** (2026-11-24): the user kept decision 3 knowing the built-in server is there for everyone, and chose decision 16's full definitions, decision 19's listing item and decision 20's fix. Asked twice more the same day, the user added "Keep my file", "Stop keeping" and the hooks-only fix of a kept file to decision 20.
   - **Slice 6o decisions (2026-11-07).** The shell-first launcher: the shell is picked first, Tools are removed, a session is named before it starts, and the launcher gets a new focus look. Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, after 6n1 merged.
     1. One slice, with its own plan, live check and pull request. It runs now, before 6n2, 6n3 and 7d.
     2. **Tool profiles (slice 6c) are removed.** Gone:
        - the shipped profiles (`crates/core/src/tools/profiles.json`) and own ones (`~/.marshell/tools.json`);
        - launch presets: the launcher's Tools group and its fill-in fields, `CreateSessionRequest.tool` and the `/v1/tools` routes;
        - Needs you from matched prompt text (Status limited), and port chips from a tool's announced URLs;
        - the Agents dialog's Tools list and its own-tools editor;
        - `SessionInfo.tool`, the profile a running command matched.

        A `tools.json` already on disk is left there and no longer read. This replaces 6c decisions 1 and 5 to 9, and the announced-URL half of decision 3.
     3. **Three parts of 6c work without a profile and stay**, moved out of the tools module:
        - port chips for the ports a tab's own processes listen on (6c decisions 3 and 4, the listener half), and auth-callback ports such as a `login` command's still get no chip;
        - not recording the output of a command that prints secrets, with the marker saying why (decision 2; there are no prompts or announced ports left to skip);
        - scrubbing secret flags from the tab title, the foreground phrase, the recording marker and the logs (decision 10).

        Decision 11's OS reads stay for these.
     4. **The launcher picks the shell first.** The sentence starts with two chips, for example `[PowerShell 7] running [Claude] in [my-app · main] · [Manual] · [Administrator ☐]`.
        - The shell chip lists the detected shell profiles: PowerShell 7, Windows PowerShell, Command Prompt, Git Bash and each WSL distro.
        - The agent chip lists "Just the shell" first, then the agents available in that shell. Changing the shell filters the list again.
        - The default shell is Settings' default profile. The last shell and agent are remembered, as the program is today.
     5. **An agent is offered in a shell when it is installed there and Marshell drives it there.**
        - Windows shells (PowerShell 7, Windows PowerShell, Command Prompt, Git Bash) offer the agents found on the Windows PATH.
        - A WSL distro offers the CLIs Marshell drives in WSL (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity and Qwen Code; Copilot CLI with 6n2) and own agents, when that distro's PATH has them. The check runs when the distro is picked and can start a stopped distro.
        - Command Prompt offers agents without the first-prompt line, because it can't pass a prompt safely.
        - An agent preset (DeepSeek, which starts Claude Code) follows its agent.
     6. **An agent that isn't available in the chosen shell is not a row.** One line under the agent list names each one with the reason, for example "Not in Ubuntu: Antigravity (not installed), GitHub Copilot (Marshell runs it in WSL later)".
     7. **The session's name can be set in the launcher.**
        - The footer's "Session N" becomes a Name field beside Start. It comes after the prompt line in Tab order, and Alt+N moves to it from anywhere in the sheet.
        - Empty keeps the default name, which is shown as the placeholder.
        - The name follows the sidebar's rename rules: trimmed, control characters dropped, at most 120 characters. It goes in `CreateSessionRequest.name`, which already exists.
     8. **With the worktree chip on, Task name and Name stay separate fields.** While Name is untouched, the session takes the task name and Name shows it as the default; typing in Name stops that. The branch comes only from Task name, as today.
     9. **The launcher's focus look: no box and no rule.**
        - A focused field or chip changes its fill and tint, and a text field shows an accent caret. In forced colors, focus is a system-colour Highlight outline.
        - frontend-design and good-css settle the exact look.
        - Every focusable thing in the launcher gets it: the prompt line, the Name, Task name and folder fields, the chips, Back and Start.
        - The visual system records it as the app's focus style. A later slice carries it to the other stylesheets once the user has judged it in this slice's live check, and the app's ring stays everywhere else until then.
   - **Slice 6p decisions (2026-11-07).** A menu at the bottom left of the window, and a cleaner command palette. Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, while 6o ran. Today the palette has about 60 rows. "History" (Ctrl+Shift+Y) and "Search all sessions" (Ctrl+Shift+Alt+F) open the same view, seven rows open the same Settings page, and Tools holds 25 unrelated commands.
     1. Its own slice, with its own plan, live check and pull request. It runs right after 6o, before 6n2, 6n3 and 7d. It is planned once 6o merges.
     2. **A menu at the bottom left takes four destinations out of the palette's list:**
        - Settings, as one item that lists its sections. It replaces Settings…, Appearance…, Agents…, Shell profiles…, Notifications…, History settings… and Diagnostics….
        - History, with its search. "History" and "Search all sessions" become one command.
        - Doctor.
        - Departures.

        Keyboard shortcuts and Pause or resume notifications stay in the palette.
     3. **A moved item is found by typing, not listed.** With an empty box the palette doesn't list it, but typing finds it: "appear" finds Settings: Appearance. Every action is still in the palette, as the review checklist asks.
     4. **The palette gets four fixes:**
        1. The five Mode rows become one "Change mode…" command that opens a second list with the modes.
        2. With an empty box, the palette starts with the last few commands run.
        3. Sessions, View and Tools become the groups Session, Terminal, Window and App.
        4. A command that can't run now is left out, for example one that needs a focused session, a split, a task or a run script, when that is missing.
     Decisions 5 to 10 were asked when the slice was planned, on 2026-11-07, after 6o merged.

     5. **The menu button** is a three-line icon button named "Menu", at the left end of the sidebar footer. The plan ring, the 5h % and the Doctor count sit to its right.
     6. **On the rail** the menu icon sits at the bottom edge. **Focus mode** shows no button: the menu opens from its shortcut or the palette, like everything else there.
     7. **The menu's shortcut is Ctrl+Shift+M** on Windows and Linux, and it can be rebound. ⌘M is Minimize on macOS, so the plan picks the Mac key.
     8. **Ctrl+Shift+Alt+F goes.** Ctrl+Shift+Y opens History with its search box focused, and pressing it again goes back to the sessions.
     9. **The footer's Doctor glyph stays as it is.** The menu's Doctor item shows the same count.
     10. **Settings is a submenu.** Its item shows ›. Right arrow, Enter or a click opens the section list beside the menu, and Left arrow or Esc goes back to the menu.
   - **Slice 6q decisions (2026-11-07, first round).** Split view is removed, and a tabbed pane on the right takes its place. Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, while 6o ran. A planning workflow researches the options first; the user decides the follow-up questions, and then the slices are planned.
     1. **The order:** it comes after 6o and 6p, before 6n2. The research runs now.
     2. **Split view is removed completely.** Gone:
        - the layout menu, split (Ctrl+Shift+D) and zoom pane (Ctrl+Shift+P);
        - pane headers and the compact prompt lines;
        - the row menu's "Split into a pane", and Ctrl+click or Ctrl+Space on a row.

        A saved split opens as its focused session. This replaces the split parts of slice 2e and the row-menu decisions that name a split.
     3. **The Preview (slice 2.5) grows into the tabbed pane** and keeps all its kinds. The three main kinds are a web browser, PDF and text files; Markdown and images stay as extra kinds. Ctrl+Shift+Q shows and hides the pane.
     4. **Tabs belong to their session.** Switching sessions swaps the pane's tabs, and a restart restores them.
     5. **The pane is resizable,** by a divider you can drag or move with the keyboard, and its width is remembered. It can be shown and hidden, and the terminal keeps at least 80 columns where the window allows.
     6. **The browser is a real one:** it opens any website, with an address bar, Back, Forward and Reload, and a port chip opens its localhost page. It runs isolated, with no access to Marshell's own API. It also:
        - stays signed in across restarts, in a profile kept apart from the person's own browser;
        - opens DevTools with F12;
        - offers region to prompt on a page;
        - opens pages an agent asks for, through the marshell CLI or the local API. Every CLI gets this equally.
     7. **Text tabs edit and save.** They have syntax colours for the file types Sublime Text knows, line numbers, find and replace, undo, and Ctrl+S to save. A file an agent changes reloads; with unsaved edits, the tab offers a merge.
     8. **Syntax colours follow Marshell's theme:** one light and one dark scheme, built from its tokens and tuned for contrast.
     9. **A PDF tab adds three things** to the slice 2.5b viewer:
        - find (Ctrl+F);
        - an outline and page thumbnails;
        - highlights and notes, saved back into the file.
     10. **A tab opens:**
         - with Ctrl+click on a path or URL in terminal output;
         - from a port chip;
         - by dropping a file on the pane, from quick open, or with Open file… or Open URL…;
         - from a + menu on the tab strip: New browser tab, Open file… and recent files.
   - **Slice 6q decisions (2026-11-07, second round).** Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, after the research in `docs/research/2026-11-07-tabs-pane-options.md`. They settle what the first round left open.
     1. **The order is risk first.** Each part has its own plan, live check and pull request:
        1. 6q-1 removes split view;
        2. the 6q-0 browser spike runs, with no merge;
        3. 6q-2 adds the pane and the tabs foundation;
        4. 6q-3 adds the browser;
        5. 6q-4 adds the text editor;
        6. 6q-5 adds PDF find, outline and thumbnails;
        7. 6q-6 adds PDF notes.

        The spike's answer shapes the foundation's geometry.
     2. **The browser starts as a spike, and the user decides after it.** A throwaway prototype on the real frameless window tests:
        - keys;
        - focus after Alt+Tab;
        - resize at 125% and 150%;
        - file drop;
        - F12.

        The browser kind is built only if the spike passes and the user approves it. If it fails, links and port chips open the system browser instead. Tauri's `unstable` feature and the capability change from `windows` to `webviews` still need the user's approval under SCOPE.md.
     3. **An overlay over a browser tab freezes the page as a picture.** While the palette, a menu, a dialog or the + menu is open, the page is hidden and a screenshot stands in, with a short flash. Region to prompt works on that frozen picture.
     4. **The page keeps the browser's keys.** While a page has focus:
        - F5, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+F and Ctrl+P work in the page;
        - Marshell's chords (Ctrl+Shift+K, Ctrl+Shift+Q and the like), Ctrl+L for the address bar, and Esc go to the app.
     5. **A popup opens as a new tab.** Sign-in flows that need a link back to their opener fail; the person signs in on the site's own page instead.
     6. **A page an agent asks for opens in the background.** The request comes through the marshell CLI or the local API.
        - Only http and https are opened.
        - Tabs per session are capped.
        - Focus is never taken.
        - The tab is marked with the agent's name.

        It uses the signed-in profile.
     7. **Ctrl+S saves at once, anywhere the person can write.**
        - A backup snapshot and Undo come first.
        - Marshell's own folder, managed settings files, UNC paths and device paths stay refused.

        This lifts `.claude/rules/local-api.md`'s "No route takes a file path to write" for the user-file save route alone. Config writes keep their preview.
     8. **In a text tab, Tab indents, and Esc then Tab leaves.** The way out is in the tab's help text, and Ctrl+M toggles it, as in CodeMirror.
     9. **The first PDF notes are highlights, comments and free text.**
        - A highlight can carry a comment, which works in any script.
        - Free text warns when its text is non-Latin, since Chrome and Edge may show nothing for it.
        - Ink, stamps and signatures stay out.
   - **Slice 6q-1 decisions (2026-11-07).** Split view's removal. Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, when the slice was planned. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-phase-6q-1-split-removal.md`.
     1. **Ctrl+click on a session row selects it like a click,** and Cmd+click does the same on macOS. **Ctrl+Space selects it like Enter.** Neither toggles a pane any more.
     2. **Ctrl+Shift+Tab stays, as "Select the terminal".** It selects the one terminal and starts the navigation cursor, which is still the only way to start the cursor from inside a terminal, since terminal programs keep Ctrl+Shift+arrows for word selection. This replaces research §3, which removed the key. 6q-2 may extend it to the tabbed pane. (The label, and what the key does with the pane on screen: replaced by slice 6q-2 decision 5.)
     3. **`docs/BRIEF.md` stays as it is.** This list records the removal.
     4. **Decided from the research (§2.1 and §3), not asked:**
        - `layout.json` keeps its shape. The core folds a saved split on load to its focused session: the zoomed pane's tab, else the active pane's, else the first open one, else none.
        - The core accepts one pane only, so the frontend doesn't fold on its own. The research proposed folding on both sides; one rule in one place can't disagree with itself.
        - `arrangement`, `ratios` and `zoomed` stay in the file as dead fields until 6q-2.
        - Ctrl+Shift+D and Ctrl+Shift+P stay unbound.
        - The shell panel's "Open in a pane" becomes "Open as a session", and it selects the new session.
   - **Slice 6q-0 decisions (2026-11-07).** The browser spike. Asked of the user on 2026-11-07, when the slice was planned. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-phase-6q-0-browser-spike.md`, and the findings go in `docs/spikes/S12-browser-pane.md`.
     1. **The spike branch may turn on Tauri's `unstable` feature and scope the default capability by `webviews` instead of `windows`.** Both stay on `phase-6q-0`, which is never merged. 6q-3 asks again.
     2. **Real input is mixed.**
        - The agent sends the keys with SendInput. Before each one it checks that the dev window is in front, and it repeats each key 20 times to count misses.
        - The user does Alt+Tab, the window-edge drag, the drop from Explorer and the display-scale changes (125% and 150%) by hand.
     3. **The spike also runs the research's four extra checks:**
        - the overlay freeze, with CapturePreview, timed;
        - Back, Forward and Reload through WebView2's COM API, and a cookie that survives a restart;
        - isolation: the page can't call Marshell's IPC, and the navigation allow-list holds;
        - Tab into and out of the page, with the page's UI Automation tree reachable.

        There is no screen-reader pass; that stays at release sign-off.
     4. **The agent writes the spike's code in the main thread.** Sonnet workers take only mechanical pieces.
     5. **Decided, not asked:**
        - the spike's code stays on the pushed branch `phase-6q-0`;
        - the plan, this list and the findings reach `main` through a docs-only pull request;
        - the go or no-go is added here once the user gives it.
     6. **Asked during the spike (2026-11-09),** after the live check's resize findings (S12 findings 20 to 22):
        - **6q-2 places the page by rule.** The front sends its layout as straight pieces over the window's width, and Rust places the page from them inside the window's own resize, so the page is never behind the window's edge. During an edge drag the app's own UI (the URL bar, the window buttons) catches up about a frame later, the lag it has with no page open. Inside the window (the divider, the sidebar) the page waits for the app to paint, so it moves with the app's own picture (S12 finding 21).
        - **The layout's jumps get motion in 6q-2.** The sidebar folding to the rail and the page switching between full and side by side animate instead of jumping. The page follows the animation frame by frame, with Rust running the same curve as the app.
        - **The app's own resize drawing is a separate slice after 6q.** The terminal text flickering, the small shell resizing unevenly, the white strip at the bottom left and the window buttons moving late all happen with no page open, so the spike only records them.
     7. **Asked during the spike (2026-11-09),** after a research round on WebView2's late redraw (S12 finding 23). The redraw itself can't be removed: on Windows, Chromium shows the old frame until the page draws at the new size, by design. The spike's Task 10 tests what can hide it or has its own fix:
        - **The page tries oversize and clip.** While the pane shrinks, the page keeps its larger size and the pane crops it; while it grows, the page is set a little wider than the pane, so the stale strip and the scrollbar stay outside the crop. The page takes the pane's exact size once the resizing stops. Overlay scrollbars, hiding the scrollbars during a drag, and freezing the page during an edge drag are not tested.
        - **The terminal's text flicker is tested now and ships right after the spike, before 6q-2,** as its own small front-end change. xterm 6.0 clears its canvas on every resize and redraws a frame later; xterm fixed it upstream (PR #5529). This part moves out of decision 6's resize slice.
        - **The spike may set the window's and the app webview's background colour at runtime,** first with a bright test colour to find which layer paints the white strip. `tauri.conf.json` doesn't change.
     8. **The go or no-go (2026-11-09): go, with limits.** Asked after Task 10's hand checks (S12 finding 23). The pane's page is a WebView2 child webview on the main window, and 6q-2 and 6q-3 carry the limits in S12's "Go or no-go".
        - **The page stays the pane's exact size.** Oversize and clip made the divider drag worse both ways the spike built it. Pinned to the pane's top-left corner, the page's scrollbar disappeared while dragging. Pinned to the edge that stays still, which the final review found missing and the user asked to test before the docs merged, the page popped on release and still jumped (S12 finding 23, checks 10c and 10d). So 6q-2 doesn't use it. The page's scrollbar and content still jump while the divider or the sidebar changes the page's width, and that is accepted as a limit.
        - **The window's background fix ships with the terminal's fix,** as one small change before 6q-2. The main window's background follows the app's theme, set from Rust at runtime, which made the white strip invisible in the spike; and the terminal fits and draws inside its resize observer. So decision 7's front-end change gains a Rust part, and the white strip leaves decision 6's resize slice.
     9. **Keys in the page follow the terminal's rule (2026-11-09).** The user's words: "treat the web page as we treated multiple shells: a selected pane reads the keybinds". Of the chords, the app gets only the shortcuts its keymap claims, as with the terminal (the session shortcuts, and the navigation chords once navigation has started), and the page gets the rest. The app sends Rust the claimed list, and the page's key route forwards only those. This replaces the spike's route, which gave the app every Ctrl+Shift chord. Esc and Ctrl+L still go to the app, and F12 still opens DevTools (6q decisions, second round 4 and first round 6).
     10. **The resize fixes (2026-11-09).** Asked when decision 8's small change was planned. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-09-resize-fixes.md`.
         - **The terminal stays on xterm 6.0.0 and calls xterm's private redraw, guarded.** xterm's own fix (PR #5529) is still only in the 6.1.0 betas: on 2026-11-09 `latest` is 6.0.0 and `beta` is 6.1.0-beta.337. The private call is what the user judged in the spike. If the function is missing, the app logs an error and falls back to the next-frame fit, and a test fails when an xterm upgrade renames it or leaves 6.0.x. The private call goes once a stable release carries #5529. Pinning the beta was the other option.
         - **The window's background is set at runtime only.** The UI reads its own computed background and sends it to Rust on load and on every theme change. The window stays white for the moment before the UI has loaded, as today. `tauri.conf.json` and the capabilities don't change.
         - **The process is light.** One branch `resize-fixes`, a short plan, the code written in the main thread, a live check, one `/code-review` pass, then the pull request.
         - **The live check is measured and judged by hand.** The agent measures blank frames over CDP with Claude Code's and Codex's full-screen TUIs open, and the user drags the window's edges and the dividers and judges by eye, as in the spike.
         - **Asked during the live check: the terminal draws mid-update.** While a program holds synchronized output (DEC mode 2026), xterm's private redraw only notes the rows, so a resize left the cleared canvas empty: 1 blank frame in 160 resize steps with Codex's TUI, and 63 in 80 under a loop that holds the block open half the time. The terminal now asks xterm's renderer itself then, one private level deeper, and both runs gave 0. For one frame the screen may show a half-finished update. Waiting, as xterm's own fix does, was the other option.
         - **Decided, not asked: a second draw at fractional display scales.** At an emulated 125% scale the WebGL addon's own device-pixel observer set the canvas's size again after the draw, and 86 frames in 80 resize steps were blank. A second observer on the canvas, made after the addon's, draws again in the same frame, and every fit goes through the same draw path. xterm's fix draws there too. The numbers are in `docs/research/2026-11-09-resize-fixes-live-check.md`.
         - **Asked after the user's hand check (2026-11-10): a full-screen program's text stays at the top when rows go.** The user saw Codex's text jump up and down while the top or the bottom edge moved. When rows go and the cursor is on the last row, xterm drops rows from the top, and Codex repaints about three frames later. `main` has it too (16 jumped and 22 blank frames in 80 resize steps); with the same-frame draw the blank frames showed the shifted text instead (35 jumped, 0 blank). Before a fit that loses rows, the terminal now moves the alternate screen's cursor onto the new last row, where xterm puts it anyway, so the rows go from the bottom: 0 jumped and 0 blank frames in 240 steps. It writes a third private xterm name, guarded like the other two, and it stays after xterm 6.1 (`topAnchor.ts`). A shell's screen isn't touched. Leaving it for the resize-polish slice, and going back to a blank frame for full-screen programs, were the other options.
   - **Slice 6q-2 decisions (2026-11-10).** The side pane and its tabs: the foundation the browser, the editor and the PDF features build on. Asked of the user on 2026-11-10, when the slice was planned. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-phase-6q-2-side-pane-tabs.md`.
     1. **The pane is called the side pane.** The command is "Side pane" (Ctrl+Shift+Q), the divider is "Resize the side pane", and an open is announced as "Opened README.md in the side pane." Nothing in the app says "Preview" any more. Keeping "Preview" until the browser lands, and "Tabs", were the other options.
     2. **An open makes a new tab, or selects the file's tab.** Ctrl+click on a path, quick open, a drop and Browse… all open a new tab in the session on screen; a file that is already open there gets its tab selected instead. A link inside a tab (a Markdown link to another file) moves that tab, and each tab's Back and Forward walk its own history. A reusable preview tab as in VS Code, and replacing the selected tab, were the other options.
     3. **Web addresses go to the system browser until 6q-3.** 6q-2 adds Ctrl+click on an http or https address in terminal output, and it opens the system browser, as a port chip does. Port chips and Markdown's web links stay as they are. The + menu has no Open URL… and no New browser tab until 6q-3, which moves all of these into a browser tab.
     4. **One switch shows and hides the pane for the whole window.** Moving between sessions swaps the tabs and never changes the terminal's width. A session with no tabs shows an empty state with Open file…, Browse…, the project's recent files and "Drop a file here"; closing the last tab leaves that empty state. Opening a tab from anywhere shows the pane. Ctrl+Shift+Q with no tab open shows the empty pane; this replaces slice 2.5's rule that it opens the file palette. A switch per session, and hiding the pane when a session has no tabs, were the other options.
     5. **The keys.**
        - Ctrl+Shift+Tab moves the keyboard from the terminal to the side pane and back, and still starts the navigation cursor. Its command reads "Switch between the terminal and the side pane". With the pane hidden it selects the terminal, as before.
        - In the tab strip, Left, Right, Home and End move between tabs, Delete closes the focused tab, and Alt+Shift+Left and Right move it, the way Alt+Shift+Up and Down move a session.
        - Ctrl+Tab shows the next tab from anywhere, wrapping, on every OS. A classic terminal has no code for Ctrl+Tab, so no terminal program loses it.
        - "Previous tab" and "Close tab" are palette commands with no default key.

        Ctrl+Shift+D and Ctrl+Shift+P for next and previous, and no global tab key at all, were the other options.
     6. **Every layout change moves, with one motion.** Showing and hiding the pane, folding and opening the sidebar, focus mode, and the two automatic jumps (the sidebar folding to the rail under 960 px, the pane covering the terminal under 720 px) all take 200 ms on the app's ease-out curve (`--dur-drawer`, `--ease-out`). The terminal keeps its grid while the motion runs and fits once at the end, so a program sees one resize. Dragging the window's edge, the divider or the sidebar's handle is never animated. With reduced motion every change is instant. The user judges the feel in the live check. Animating only the automatic jumps, and 120 ms, were the other options.
     7. **The pane's width is saved in pixels, and the pane gives way before the terminal.** The divider stops where the terminal would drop under 80 columns. As the window narrows the pane shrinks to its minimum of 320 px, then the terminal goes under 80 columns, and with under 720 px of room the pane covers the terminal, as the Preview did. Widening the window gives the pane its saved width back. Until the divider is first moved the pane takes half the room, and a double-click on the divider goes back to that. Folding the sidebar first, and covering the terminal as soon as 80 columns don't fit, were the other options.
     8. **Recent files are the project's.** The + menu and the empty pane list the last 10 files opened in any session of this project, newest first, without the ones open in this session. A list per session, and one list for the whole app, were the other options.
     9. **"Open file…" is quick open, and "Browse…" is the system's file dialog.** Quick open searches the session's project, as before. Browse… opens any file on disk; the dialog is opened from Rust, like the launcher's folder picker, so no capability and no dependency changes. Quick open alone, and the dialog alone, were the other options.
     10. **A drop while the launcher or a dialog covers the window opens nothing.** The announcer says "Close the launcher to open a file." (or names the dialog). This settles the lead slice 6o left. Opening the file behind the launcher, and closing the launcher to open it, were the other options.
     11. **The browser's Back, Forward and Reload are icon buttons (the user, 2026-11-10).** "In the url bar, instead of back, forward and reload lets put icons there." Each has an `aria-label` and a tooltip, like the pane's own Back and Forward. 6q-3 builds the bar.
     12. **Sonnet workers implement the plan's larger tasks,** as in 6q-1, each checked in the main thread.
     13. **Decided, not asked:**
         - **`layout.json`.** `view.pane` holds the switch, the width, each session's tabs and the recent files. `arrangement`, `ratios` and `zoomed` leave the API and the types (6q-1 decision 4), but the file keeps them, written empty by the file layer alone, so a build from before 6q-2 still loads its sessions, sidebar and launcher folders from a new file and ignores `view.pane` (the user, 2026-11-10, after both reviews found that such a build read a new file as the defaults and then overwrote it). When that older build saves, the pane's tabs are gone from the file. The version stays 1: an old file loads as before with an empty pane.
         - **A tab's kind is a word, `file` for now.** Markdown, code, image and PDF are still told apart by the file's extension. A kind this build doesn't know is dropped when the file loads, so 6q-3's `browser` tabs don't break an older build.
         - **Limits.** A session holds at most 32 tabs; the 33rd open is refused and announced ("This session has 32 tabs. Close one to open another."). A tab's history keeps its last 50 files, and the file keeps 200 recent files in all.
         - **Tabs stay with an ended session** and come back when it resumes. They go when the session is closed and the app next starts.
         - **A tab's title is the file's name.** Two tabs with the same name each add their folder's name; the tooltip is the full path.
         - **A full strip** shrinks its tabs to a minimum, then scrolls sideways with no scrollbar, keeping the selected tab in view.
         - **A tab closes** from its × button, with a middle click, with Delete in the strip, or with "Close tab". The tab to its right is selected next, else the one to its left.
         - **A tab moves** with Alt+Shift+Left and Right or by dragging it, built on pointer events because native file drop turns HTML5 drag and drop off on Windows.
         - **The four tabs shown most recently stay loaded,** so coming back to one keeps its place. Others load again from the top, as does every tab after a session switch. Only the tab on screen is polled for changes; a hidden one checks when it is shown.
         - **A drop of several files opens each one,** and the last is selected. Before, only the first opened.
         - **The pane's page and the Rust half of the layout rule arrive with the browser (6q-3).** 6q-2 has no page to place. It builds the two things 6q-0 decision 6 needs from the app: the layout as one pure function of the window's width, the sidebar and the pane, and the motion with its curve and length as constants Rust can run too.
     14. **A button in the title bar shows and hides the side pane (the user, 2026-11-13).** Once the pane was hidden, only Ctrl+Shift+Q and the palette brought it back; the user found no button for it in a hand check. Asked of the user the same day:
         - **Where:** at the end of the title bar, just before Minimize, mirroring the Sidebar button at the start. (Not a handle on the terminal's edge, and not the session's bottom bar.)
         - **What it does:** one toggle, always visible, with `aria-expanded` and the shortcut in its tooltip ("Show the side pane (Ctrl+Shift+Q)", "Hide the side pane (Ctrl+Shift+Q)"). The pane's own "Hide the side pane" button stays.
         - **Decided, not asked:** it is there in every sidebar mode, focus mode included, and on every system. A press does what Ctrl+Shift+Q does, so the keyboard goes into the pane when it shows and to the terminal when it hides. With no session, or while a view replaces the terminal, it is `aria-disabled` and reads "The side pane shows beside a session". The navigation cursor reaches it, named "Side pane".
     15. **Every side-pane tab shows its type (the user, 2026-11-13).** Asked after the hand checks, for the fix PR before 6q-3b:
         - **A long file name shortens in the middle and keeps its extension:** `sample-long-name.txt` shows as `sam….txt`. A web page's title shortens at its end, as before.
         - **Four icons:** a globe for every web tab, always (the site's own icon leaves the strip, "Slice 6q-3 decisions" 14); one text-file icon for every text type (`.ts`, `.txt`, `.md`, `.js`, `.ps1` and the rest); a PDF icon; an image icon.
     16. **Rendered and Source are icons (the user, 2026-11-13).** The text buttons of a Markdown or HTML file's toggle became an icon toggle, from a picture the user sent: an eye for Rendered and `</>` for Source, side by side on one filled track, the pressed one with a stronger fill. Each keeps its name as its `aria-label` and tooltip ("Rendered", "Source") and its `aria-pressed`. HTML files get the same toggle (decided, not asked: it is one control).
   - **Slice 6q-3 decisions (2026-11-10).** The browser tab. Asked of the user on 2026-11-10, when the slice was planned. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-phase-6q-3a-browser-tab.md`.
     1. **6q-3 runs as two slices.** 6q-3a builds the browser tab itself: the page, the URL bar, keys and focus, the overlay freeze, isolation, port chips and links, F12 and a clean quit. 6q-3b adds `marshell open` with its API route (6q decisions, second round 6) and region to prompt on a page. Each has its own plan, live check and pull request. One slice, and region to prompt in 6q-3a, were the other options.
     2. **The user approved three security-surface changes (SCOPE.md).**
        - Tauri's `unstable` Cargo feature, which child webviews need. No crate is added.
        - The default capability scoped by `webviews: ["main"]` instead of `windows`, with an app manifest that puts Marshell's own commands under it. A page then gets no plugin permission and none of Marshell's commands (S12 finding 14).
        - `frame-ancestors` in the CSP, only if the live check shows that a frame at the app's own origin inside a page reaches Tauri's IPC. It tightens the CSP. If the check shows no reach, the CSP stays as it is.
     3. **Web addresses open a browser tab, with a way out.** Ctrl+click on an address in terminal output, a port chip and a web link in a Markdown tab all open a browser tab in the side pane. The URL bar has an "Open in the system browser" icon button for the page on screen. This ends 6q-2 decision 3. A setting that chooses, and a tab for localhost only, were the other options.
     4. **Up to 8 pages stay loaded in the whole app, across session switches.** Coming back to a session finds its page as it was left: its scroll position, a half-filled form, a sign-in step. Past 8, the page shown longest ago unloads, and it loads again at its address when it is next shown. The file tabs' rule (the 4 most recent in the session on screen, loaded again after a switch) and never unloading were the other options.
     5. **A download goes to the Downloads folder,** and WebView2's own download flyout shows it, as in Edge. Asking where to save each time, and blocking downloads, were the other options.
     6. **A page's permission request gets WebView2's own prompt.** That covers the camera, the microphone, location, notifications and clipboard reading. The answer is remembered per site in Marshell's browser profile, and a page an agent opened still needs the person's click. Refusing every request, and a prompt for the clipboard only, were the other options.
     7. **Words in the URL bar search Google.** Text that looks like an address opens instead, with https for a site and http for localhost and IP addresses. A choice of engine can come later. DuckDuckGo, and addresses only, were the other options.
     8. **Pages follow Marshell's theme.** A site with a light and a dark design gets the one that matches the app, and it changes when the app's theme does. Following Windows' setting was the other option.
     9. **"New browser tab" shows a blank page with the keyboard in the URL bar.** "Open URL…" in the palette does the same. A start page with the session's ports and the project's recent pages was the other option.
     10. **Two extras are in 6q-3a.** A browser tab shows its site's icon, with a globe until one arrives. A palette command, "Clear the browser's data…", asks first and then clears the cookies, the cache and the site permissions in Marshell's browser profile. Saving passwords was offered and stays off.
     11. **The main thread writes the page's Rust:** the WebView2 COM calls, the key route, focus and isolation. Sonnet workers take the frontend and the mechanical tasks, each checked in the main thread. Workers for every larger task, and Codex CLI, were the other options.
     12. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these; the user can change any of them.
         - **A browser tab is a pane tab of kind `browser`.** Its one history entry holds the page's address, and the tab also saves the page's last title, so the strip can name it before the page loads. The page keeps its own back and forward history, which a restart doesn't restore. An address already open in the session selects its tab, and a blank page always gets a new one.
         - **A page may open `http`, `https` and `about:blank`, and nothing else.** Refused: the shell's own names, the dev server's origin, the local API's port, an address with a user name in it, and every other scheme. Every other name for this computer opens, with a port or without (decision 13).
         - **A page can't send notifications,** which decision 6 said it could ask for. The notification plugin replaces `window.Notification` in every webview, and its commands are refused from a page. Giving pages the browser's own back needs a change the slice wasn't allowed to make (how the plugin is loaded, or a script of ours in every page). Found in the final review; the user keeps it as a limit (decision 13).
         - **Text in the URL bar that is a path or another scheme is refused,** with a line under the bar. It is never sent to Google: a path can name a person or a project.
         - **The layout rule's mechanism changes from 6q-0 decision 6.** Rust places the page inside the window's resize, by rule, as decided. While the app's own motion runs, or when a resize crosses a layout breakpoint, Rust holds for 250 ms and follows the app's per-frame reports instead of running the curve itself. The live check judges it, and the curve in Rust comes back if the page trails.
         - **Anything drawn over the pane freezes the page:** the palette and the other overlays, every menu and dialog, the launcher, the navigation cursor, and a file dragged over the window. A file dropped on a page counts as a drop on the pane and opens a tab.
         - **The app's shortcuts reach Rust as a list of chords,** built by trying every key with every modifier against the terminal's own rule, so the page and a terminal give up the same keys.
         - **Reload doubles as Stop** while a page loads. A popup opens as a new tab in the popup's own session, selected only when that session is on screen.
         - **A page whose process died** keeps its tab, and loads again at its address when shown or reloaded.
         - **Browser tabs are Windows only for now.** Elsewhere "New browser tab" is disabled and says "Not available on this system yet", and web addresses keep opening the system browser.
         - **A test holds the isolation:** every command the shell registers is granted to the app's own webview and to no other, and no capability names a page.
     13. **The user's answers after the final review (2026-11-10).** 6q-3a merged with two places where the build differed from decisions 6 and 12, and one open question.
         - **A local web app opens under any name.** The user: seeing the web app you work on, from inside Marshell, is one of the features most needed. 6q-3a refused every name under `.localhost` on the scheme's default port, so a dev server behind a proxy at `http://app.localhost/` didn't open. Now only the shell's own names are refused: `tauri` and `ipc`, the two schemes this build serves. wry answers every `http://<name>.…` address itself inside a page, at any port and under any ending, so those are refused whole (`http://tauri.localhost:3000/`, `http://ipc.example.com/`), and `<name>.localhost` with every name under it is refused on `https` too. That also closes a gap in 6q-3a, which let `http://tauri.localhost:3000/` through; a page there got no command, because no capability names a page. `https://tauri.app/` opens. The list was read from wry 0.57.0 and Tauri 2.12.2. A test fails, until the list is read again, on another version of either, on a new package that builds on Tauri, on the `protocol-asset` or `isolation` feature in the shell's manifest, on a scheme the shell registers, and on `useHttpsScheme`.
         - **Page notifications stay a limit.** The user doesn't need them. Decision 6 holds for the camera, the microphone, location and the clipboard.
         - **The app's shortcuts work from the Address box,** as they do from a page. The user asked for the better experience; the box is where the keyboard is while a person browses, and no shortcut is a key that edits a line of text. A key pressed with AltGr stays the box's, because Windows reports AltGr as Ctrl+Alt and it types a character on some layouts. Ctrl+Shift+arrows still select words there. The other text boxes (rename, the launcher's fields, search) keep their keys.
     14. **The user's answers after the hand checks (2026-11-13).** The checks found three bugs, and tracing them found a fourth (`docs/research/2026-11-10-browser-tab-live-check.md`, findings 25 to 28). They are fixed in one small PR before 6q-3b; the plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-13-pane-fixes.md`.
         - **Ctrl+Shift+Tab into a page that Tab had left puts the keyboard on the page's first stop,** as Tab into it would, whichever end Tab left by. Otherwise it puts the keyboard back where the page last had it.
         - **A ring just outside the page shows that the page has the keys.** It is drawn in a 2 px gutter between the slot's edge and the page.
         - **Hiding the pane folds the page as a picture.** The hide first freezes the page (about 50 ms), then the fold starts with the picture inside it. Hiding the page at the fold's start, with the pane folding empty, was the other option.
         - **The page placer's motion bug is fixed in the same PR,** each part with a test that fails first.
         - **The strip's site icon gives way to a globe** for every web tab ("Slice 6q-2 decisions" 15), which replaces decision 10's site icon in the strip.
         - **Decided, not asked:** the ring shows whenever the page has the keys, after a click too, the way the terminal shows where the keyboard is. A hide waits at most 150 ms for the picture, then folds anyway. Esc returns focus to whatever opened an overlay, as `useDialogFocus` does; the terminal takes the keyboard after an overlay closes only when nothing else has it.
         - **A rolled Ctrl+Shift+Tab is a follow-up (the user, 2026-11-13).** The live check found Tab going down a few milliseconds before Shift, which runs Ctrl+Tab ("Next tab") instead (finding 30). Asked of the user: the fix goes in its own small change after this PR. Ctrl+Tab will wait for Tab's release, or about 50 ms, before switching tabs, and run Ctrl+Shift+Tab instead if Shift goes down meanwhile, in the app and in the shell for a page.
         - **Fixed in `pane-fixes`:** finding 25 in 0547677a and 2846c19f, 26 in 91406993 and 931781a5, 27 in 193ea341 and 2846c19f, 28 in 97caad97; the tab types ("Slice 6q-2 decisions" 15) in 50231090. The user judged the fold "fine" and kept the 2 px frame.
     15. **The rolled Ctrl+Shift+Tab fix (2026-11-13).** Decision 14's follow-up, in its own small PR on branch `ctrl-tab-rollover`; the plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-13-ctrl-tab-rollover.md`. Asked of the user: I build it inline, with no workers.
         - **Decided, not asked:** the hold covers a fresh Ctrl+Tab only, with no Shift, Alt or Meta, and not a repeat. A repeat while it is held is dropped, as the app already drops a repeated Ctrl+Tab. Shift counts only with Ctrl still held and no Alt. Any other key pressed during the hold ends it first: the held chord goes, then the key. A hold ends unsent when the window or the page loses the keyboard, since the person has moved on. When the app is about to give a page the keyboard back (after a key it took from the page, after a cover, after the window comes back), a Ctrl+Tab held meanwhile goes first, since giving the keyboard away would drop it. In the app the hold sits in front of every handler and sees only real key presses: a page's forwarded key, already settled by the shell, isn't held a second time. The shell holds only a Ctrl+Tab the app claims.
         - **WebView2 never reports Shift to the shell** ("A few specific keys are never considered accelerators, such as Shift", the `AcceleratorKeyPressed` reference), so the shell looks at the keys every 2 ms while it holds, and ends the hold as Ctrl+Shift+Tab when it sees Ctrl and Shift down, as the app does at Shift's key-down. A Shift let go again before Tab still counts. Otherwise it reads the keys when the hold ends, at Tab's release, at 50 ms or at another key. Both holds run the same cases from `src-tauri/src/browser/tab-hold.json` in their tests. A key the page keeps, pressed during the hold, acts in the page at once; the held chord reaches the app just after it (asked of the user after the final review, 2026-11-14: holding that key back too would lose it, since the shell can't replay a page's key into the app).
         - **Live check (2026-11-14): finding 30 is fixed.** By real keys with Tab 10 ms before Shift, 30 of 30 rolled presses switched between the terminal and the side pane, 15 from the terminal and 15 from a page, and the tab never stepped. WebView2 reported no Shift to the shell, as expected. The note is `docs/research/2026-11-13-ctrl-tab-rollover-live-check.md`.
         - **After the final review (2026-11-14), the user: "fix the minors".** Besides the two above: a navigation hold whose modifier release went to a page (the page took the keyboard as Ctrl and Shift came up) ends at the next key that goes down without them, so it can't take a later Ctrl+Shift+arrow from a terminal.
         - **Re-check after the fix pass (2026-11-14): pass.** By real keys on the branch's last code, 10 of 10 rolled presses switched panes (5 from the terminal, 5 from a page), 4 of 4 presses with Shift let go again before Tab did too, and 2 plain Ctrl+Tabs from a page each stepped one tab. The note's "Re-check after the fix pass" section has the timings.
   - **Slice 6q-3b decisions (2026-11-13 and 2026-11-17).** `marshell open`, the mark on a tab an agent opened, and region to prompt on a page. Asked of the user on 2026-11-13 (1 to 8) and when the slice was planned on 2026-11-17 (9 and 10). The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-phase-6q-3b-marshell-open.md`. Built on `phase-6q-3b` and live-checked on 2026-11-18 (`docs/research/2026-11-17-marshell-open-live-check.md`).
     1. **`marshell open` takes web addresses and files.** An address opens a browser tab; a path opens a file tab, which is read-only like every file tab until 6q-4. This widens "Slice 6q decisions", second round 6, which named pages only.
     2. **The tab opens in the background.** It is not selected, and a hidden side pane stays hidden. The title-bar pane button shows a dot until the pane has been shown.
     3. **The opener's mark sits on the tab until the tab is first selected.** It is the agent's own icon (`AgentMark`). After that the tab's tooltip keeps "Opened by <agent>".
     4. **Region to prompt on a page sends a crop of the page as it is on screen,** labelled with the page's title and address. It follows the slice 2.5b flow, and no script runs in the page.
     5. **The tab goes to the caller's own session** (`MARSHELL_TAB_ID`), or to the one `--session <n>` names. Outside Marshell `--session` is required.
     6. **A region starts from a "Select region" icon button in the URL bar and from a palette command,** and then uses the keys a PDF's region uses.
     7. **The five leads 6q-3a left are fixed in this slice,** each with a test where a rule can be tested without a page, and by the live check otherwise.
     8. **Builders are as in 6q-3a.** The main thread writes the page's Rust and the route with its checks. Sonnet workers take the frontend and the mechanical tasks, each checked in the main thread.
     9. **The built-in `marshell` skill tells agents about `marshell open`** (2026-11-17). One command line and one rule join its text. Installed copies update through the usual preview. Leaving the skill as it was, with only `marshell --help` naming the command, was the other option.
     10. **A plain shell's `marshell open` is a background tab too** (2026-11-17), for a person and for a script. Its tab shows a plain dot where an agent's icon would be. Showing the tab at once in a session with no agent, and a `--show` flag, were the other options.
     11. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these; the user can change any of them.
         - **The request waits in the core until the app takes it.** `POST /v1/sessions/{tab}/open` checks the request and puts it in a list. The app hears the list on the event stream it already has, adds each tab to its own copy of the layout, and reports each one done. The app stays the only writer of the pane's tabs, and a request made while the window is reloading isn't lost. A Tauri event was the other way; those are for what the operating system reports (`.claude/rules/tauri-shell.md`). The command waits up to 5 s for the app's answer, so it can say when the app refused; after that it says "Marshell hasn't answered yet. The tab opens when its window does." and exits 0. At most 64 requests wait.
         - **An address starts with `http://` or `https://`.** Anything else is a path, made absolute from the folder the command runs in. When no such file exists and the text looks like a host (`localhost:3000`), the error names the whole address to type.
         - **A file must exist and be a regular file,** and it passes the pane's own file checks: no UNC path and no device path.
         - **One command may name several targets.** Each is asked for in turn. The exit code is 0 when every one was taken, 1 when any was refused, and 2 for a usage error.
         - **Something already open isn't opened twice.** Its tab gets the mark again, unless it is the tab on screen.
         - **A session with no tabs:** the new tab becomes its selected one, since a session's tabs always have one selected. The pane still isn't shown. A session's selected tab loses its mark as soon as it is on screen (the pane shown, the session in view), not only when it is clicked, so a tab that was selected from the start doesn't stay marked while the person looks at it (built in Task 5, 2026-11-17).
         - **A background page loads when its tab is first selected,** as every page loads when it is shown. The one case with no click is a session whose pane is open and empty: the page loads there at once. An agent can already start the system browser, so this adds no reach.
         - **A session with 32 tabs refuses the request:** "Session 3 already has 32 tabs in its side pane."
         - **With the app closed the command says "Marshell isn't running."** and exits 1. It has no `--start`.
         - **Who opened it** comes from the caller's own `MARSHELL_TAB_ID`, which the command sends along: the core reads that session's agent. A caller outside Marshell, and a session with no agent, give the plain dot, and the tooltip then reads "Opened with marshell open".
         - **The pane button's dot** shows while any session has a tab nobody has seen since it was opened this way, also when the pane is open on another session. Showing the pane with that session in view clears that session's part. It isn't saved across a restart; the mark on the tab is. The user kept this after the final review (2026-11-18), over a dot for the session on screen only and over any showing of the pane clearing it; the dot doesn't say which session has the tab.
         - **An agent typed at a shell's prompt is the opener** (after the final review, 2026-11-18): a session launched as a shell whose foreground program is an agent marks its tabs with that agent, as the sidebar names the row.
         - **A screen reader hears it:** "Claude Code opened example.com in the side pane." through the announcer. The announcer speaks at most once per 1.5 s and keeps only the newest waiting message, so of several tabs opened within that time only the first and the last are heard, and a status line that comes next (a shell's command ending) can replace the last; the tabs' names still end ", new" (live check, 2026-11-18).
         - **"Select region" is one palette command for every kind of tab that has it:** a PDF, an image and a page. R still starts it in a PDF or an image; in a page R belongs to the page.
         - **A region's label on a page** is `<title> (<address>)`, with the address cut at 500 characters.
         - **Browser tabs are Windows only** (6q-3 decision 12), so elsewhere `marshell open <address>` answers "Browser tabs aren't available on this system yet." Files open on every system.
         - **The leads.** A page that opens a tab with no click behind it is refused, with "This page tried to open a tab." under the bar: a popup opens only when WebView2 recorded a person's request for that address, and one asked for before the page's handler is in place is refused too. A title is cut at 200 characters before it leaves the shell, and an address over 4096 characters is reported as its site's root. An address the shell would refuse (the app's own origins, the local API's port) is refused before a tab is made for it, by the same cases file the shell's rule is tested with. Making a page no longer holds one of the async runtime's workers. A page's first navigation is checked by the address it commits: one that doesn't parse sends the page to `about:blank` with the refusal line. Making the page blank and then sending it to its address was the plan; it put `about:blank` in every page's history (Task 8, 2026-11-17).
   - **Slice 6q-4 decisions (2026-11-18).** The text editor in a side-pane file tab. Asked of the user on 2026-11-18, when the slice was planned (1 to 8); 9 is what the plan settled without asking. They build on "Slice 6q decisions", first round 7 and 8 and second round 7 and 8. The plan for the first half is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-18-phase-6q-4a-text-editor.md`.
