     27. **"Open terminal links here" covers Ctrl+click in terminal output only.** Switched off, that click opens the system browser. A port chip, a link in a Markdown file, `marshell open` and the address bar keep opening in the Browser panel. Port chips too was the other option.
     28. **Settled by the plan, not asked:**
         - The settings are `browser.terminal_links` (on), `browser.open_agent_html` (on, 11) and `browser.cookies` (`always`, 18) in `settings.json`. The Cookies submenu and the Settings choice read "Keep always", "Keep until quit" and "Never keep".
         - A checked row or a radio row closes the menu when chosen, and the change is announced ("Open terminal links here: off", "Viewport: Phone", "Cookies: Until quit.", in the words of 22's line).
         - A screenshot is of the page on screen: with the Browser panel hidden, a tab not loaded, or an HTML file in front, the two rows are off with the reason, and the palette commands say it. A picture's name takes the page's host with ":" written "-" ("localhost-5173 2026-11-21 134501.png"); a name already in Downloads gets " (2)". The core makes the name from the host and the time the app sends, so no route takes a file name; a host that can't name a file is written "page". A page that stays covered for 5 seconds gets "The page is covered. Close what covers it and try again."
         - A written file opens when it exists after the write and its name ends in `.html` or `.htm`; a path a CLI gives relative is read from the folder its hook names, and a WSL session's path is turned into its Windows one (29 says which of those open). It doesn't depend on the CLI's live-status switch. Codex reports writes made by `apply_patch` only. An event of another agent than the tab runs opens nothing, and at most 32 such opens wait for the app at once, so a `marshell open` always has room.
         - Where pages aren't available (macOS and Linux today), the viewport still works for an HTML file, and the screenshot rows, the cookie choice and "Open terminal links here" are off with "Not available on this system yet" (a link can only open in the system browser there).
         - Until the settings are read, the menu and the Settings page show no switch as on and no cookie rule as chosen, with "Your settings couldn't be read" when the read failed. A run that can't read them makes private pages, so nothing is kept against a rule it can't see; when a later read works, the pages load again under the saved rule, and that is announced as any change of rule is.
         - "Clear browsing data…" under "Never keep" clears what is on disk and loads the tabs again, because a private page's cookies live in memory until the last private page closes.
      29. **From a WSL session, only an HTML file on a Windows drive opens by itself** (a path under `/mnt/<drive>`). A file inside the distro is `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` to Windows, and the file guard refuses every network path, for `marshell open` and the viewer as well; this slice leaves the guard as it is. The Settings page says so in one line ("In a WSL session, only files on a Windows drive (under /mnt) open."), and reading a session's own distro files is left to a slice of its own, with an issue. Admitting the session's distro now, and no auto-open in a WSL session at all, were the other options.
   - **Slice 6r-2 decisions (2026-11-21).** The file tree, the path bar and the Files menu ("Slice 6r decisions" 1, 12 and 13). Asked of the user on 2026-11-21, when the slice was planned, in five rounds, after a read of the pane's, the editor's and the core's file code and of the user's screenshots of another app's Files panel; the user took the recommended option each time. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-21-phase-6r-2-file-tree.md`.
     1. **The tree lists everything in the project folder but `.git`, and what git ignores is dimmed.** An ignored file or folder (`node_modules`, build output, logs, an agent's screenshots) is drawn dimmed and read as "ignored", since agents often write there. Git's view alone (the list "Open file…" uses), and everything drawn alike, were the other options.
     2. **The tree's root is the session's git checkout; outside one, it follows the shell.** In a checkout it is the checkout's root (a worktree's own), as "Open file…" uses. Outside one it is the folder the shell last reported, so a `cd` moves the tree, or the launch folder when the shell reports none. The launch folder, fixed, was the other option.
     3. **"?" in the filter searches file contents, as plain text.** Case is ignored unless the text has a capital letter. The search skips ignored files, binary files and files over 1 MB, and stops at 1,000 matching lines. It is new in the core; in a checkout it uses `git grep`. A regular expression, names only, and a later slice were the other options.
     4. **The tree is read-only, with a menu.** A row opens its file; its menu (right-click, Shift+F10 or the Menu key) has Open, "Paste path into <session>" (what dropping the file on the terminal does), Copy path, Copy relative path and Show in File Explorer. Nothing is created, renamed, moved or deleted from the tree. File operations with a confirmation, and no menu, were the other options.
     5. **"File tree position: Automatic" puts the tree at the window's edge,** so the open file sits next to the terminal (sidebar, terminal, file, tree), mirrored in a right-to-left layout. Left and Right fix the side. Next to the terminal, and a choice by width, were the other options.
     6. **Under 480 px of Files panel width the tree covers the file.** It is drawn over the file, opening a file from it hides it, and the tree button brings it back. The tree above the file, and the two squeezed side by side, were the other options.
     7. **The path bar's folders show themselves in the tree.** Each folder in the path is a button that reveals that folder in the tree, showing the tree if it is hidden, and the bar's menu copies the path. The path of a file outside the project folder starts at `~` or at the drive. A menu of each folder's contents, and plain text, were the other options.
     8. **"Go to file or search" gets Ctrl+Shift+P (⌘P on macOS).** It is today's "Open file…" (the palette's file list), which had no key. "Show file tree" gets none: it has its button, its menu row and its palette command. P is the only letter still free on every system: E is IBus's emoji key on Linux, J opens the webview's developer tools, and H is kept for Changes. P for the tree, P and E, and no new key were the other options.
     9. **Preview tabs are on by default.** A click on a file in the tree, or Space on its row, opens it in a preview tab, whose name is in italics and is read with ", preview"; the next preview replaces it. Space keeps the keyboard in the tree. Enter or a double-click opens a kept tab and moves the keyboard to it, and editing the file or double-clicking its tab keeps a preview tab. Keeping by editing only, and off by default, were the other options.
     10. **Auto save is off by default.** Agents read and run these files, and a half-typed edit on disk can break a dev server or be read mid-change. When it is on, it saves 1 second after the last key and on leaving the tab, the session or the window, with the same safe save as Ctrl+S, and it stops at a changed-on-disk conflict. On by default, with or without the timer, were the other options.
     11. **"Word wrap" on wraps every file; off is today's start.** Off, the default, starts Markdown and plain text wrapped and code unwrapped, as now; on wraps code too. Alt+Z still flips the tab in front only. All or none, and Alt+Z flipping the setting, were the other options.
     12. **The Settings page gains a Files section with the Files menu's settings,** as Browser's are in Settings → Browser; the menu and the page change the same saved values. A section named Editor, and the menu alone, were the other options.
     13. **The tree's icons are Marshell's own line glyphs in the text colour,** about twelve, drawn like the tab icons (folder, open folder, code, Markdown, text, image, PDF, web page, data, config, archive, other); in forced colours they take the system's text colour. Tinted glyphs, and an icon pack, were the other options.
     14. **The tree follows the open file.** When the Files panel's selected tab changes, the tree opens the folders down to its file, marks it and scrolls it into view; a file outside the project folder leaves the tree as it is. Marking it only when its folder is open was the other option.
     15. **The tree shows no git status in 6r-2.** Slice 6r-3, which reads git's status for Changes, decides whether the tree marks changed files too. Marks now, and never, were the other options.
     16. **A file the pane can't show says so and offers "Show in File Explorer".** "report.docx can't be shown here." replaces "This file isn't text.", and nothing is run. Opening it with its app (programs excepted), and not opening it at all, were the other options.
     17. **The filter matches names, shown in the tree.** Its letters match anywhere in a file's or a folder's name, ignoring case; the matches stay in their folders, which open, and the matched letters are bold. It searches git's list, as "Open file…" does, so an ignored file isn't found by name. The ranking "Open file…" uses, in a flat list, was the other option.
     18. **A letter typed on a tree row moves to the next row whose name starts with it,** the standard tree pattern; Ctrl+F in the tree moves the keyboard to the filter box. Typing into the filter was the other option.
     19. **A content search's results take the tree's place** under the filter box: each file with its matching lines and their numbers. Enter on a line opens the file at that line in a preview tab; clearing the box brings the tree back. A tab of their own was the other option.
     20. **The tree is shown the first time the Files panel opens.** "Show file tree" starts on, and hiding it is remembered for the window. Hidden at first was the other option.
     21. **"Show in File Explorer" uses the opener plugin's reveal,** with one capability line, `opener:allow-reveal-item-in-dir` (the user's word on the security surface, as `SCOPE.md` asks). It selects the item in Explorer through the system's API and runs nothing. A core route that starts `explorer.exe /select`, and leaving it out, were the other options. Asked after the first plan draft, with 22.
     22. **The Files menu's first row reads "Go to file…"** and opens the palette's file list, as decision 8 has it, so the row promises no search it doesn't do; content search is the filter's "?". Keeping "Go to file or search" with a "?" search in the palette, and a row that puts the keyboard in the tree's filter, were the other options.
     23. **Settled by the plan, not asked:**
         - The settings are `files.preview_tabs` (on, 9), `files.auto_save` (off, 10), `files.tab_indents` (on), `files.word_wrap` (off, 11), `files.indent_guides` (on), `files.whitespace` (off), `files.scroll_past_end` (off), `files.tab_size` (4) and `files.tree_position` (`automatic`) in `settings.json`, as in the reference. "Tab size" is how wide a tab is drawn and the indent of a file whose own can't be told; a file indented with 2 spaces keeps 2.
         - Whether the tree shows and its width belong to the window (`layout.json`): 240 px at first, 160 px at least, and the file keeps 240 px. The tree's root and its open folders (at most 500) belong to the session and are saved with its tabs, so they come back after a restart; a new root (a `cd` outside git) starts with every folder closed.
         - The tree reads the disk by itself: while it is on screen and the window has focus, the folders it shows are read again every 2 seconds, and one whose modified time hasn't changed costs nothing more; every 30 seconds, and when the window gets the focus back, all are read afresh. A new file shows within about 2 seconds.
         - Folders come first, then files, in natural order ignoring case ("file2" before "file10"). `.git` is never listed. A folder of more than 5,000 entries shows the first 5,000 and "And 1,234 more". A link to a folder (a symlink or a junction) is listed and read as a link and never opened, so the tree never leaves the project folder; a link to a file opens as the file.
         - Only the tree and its search results open preview tabs. Go to file…, Browse…, a link, a drop, `marshell open` and an agent's HTML file open kept tabs, and opening by any of them a file shown in a preview tab keeps it. A link followed inside a preview tab keeps it a preview. Each panel has at most one preview tab, and an edit keeps it before anything can replace it.
         - A search's text is 2 to 200 characters, runs 300 ms after the last key, and cancels the one before. A line over 240 characters is cut around its first match. The results say "Showing the first 1,000 matching lines." when cut and "No file has “text”." when empty, and the count is announced: "12 matching lines in 4 files."
         - The name filter shows at most 500 matches ("Showing 500 matches. Type more to narrow them.") and says "No file or folder name has “text”." A folder that matches is shown closed; opening it lists every file under it.
         - Esc in the filter box clears it, and in an empty box returns the keyboard to the terminal, as Esc in a tab does; Down moves to the first row. Esc in the tree returns the keyboard to the terminal; in the narrow layout (6) it first closes the tree and the keyboard goes to the file.
         - The command "Open file…" is called "Go to file…" everywhere it appears (the palette, the + menu, the Files empty state, the Files menu, Keyboard shortcuts), since it is one command (22). The palette gains "Show file tree" and "Collapse all folders", with no keys.
         - The Files ⋯ menu sits in the tab strip, as Browser's does. The path bar's row ends with two buttons, "Collapse all folders" and "Show file tree" (pressed while the tree shows).
         - "Paste path into <session>" does what a drop on that terminal does: the path is pasted, Enter is never sent, the keyboard moves to that terminal, and it says "Pasted main.ts into api-server."
         - "Show in File Explorer" reads "Show in Finder" on macOS and "Show in file manager" on Linux.
         - With the keyboard sent to the Files panel and no tab open, it goes to the tree.
         - A session whose folder can't be listed (none, or a network path such as a WSL distro's own files: 6r-1b decision 29) shows "This session has no folder the tree can show." in the tree.
         - Auto save says nothing when it works; a failure or a conflict is said as Ctrl+S's would be. Closing a tab with unsaved edits still asks, and leaving a session auto-saves its text tabs first.
         - Indent guides are Marshell's own small editor extension; no package is added.
         - Settled after the plan's review: a session's tree is kept when its last tab closes. A file too large to preview keeps its sentence and gets the same "Show in File Explorer" button (16). Inside the Files panel the file's header leaves out its name and folder, which the path bar shows; the Browser panel's viewer keeps its header as it is. In the narrow layout (6) a click, Enter or a double-click on a file hides the tree, and Space leaves it over the file, since Space keeps the keyboard in the tree (9). With the keyboard alone, a preview tab is kept by Enter on its row in the tree. A search stops after 3 seconds as it does at 1,000 lines, and says "The search stopped after 3 seconds. Showing what it found." "Show in File Explorer" refuses a network path ("Couldn't show the file: it's on a network share."). Auto save never writes text that still holds conflict markers and never runs while the changed-on-disk bar is up; Ctrl+S still can.
     24. **An auto save keeps a copy of the file's last version only when the newest copy is at least 5 minutes old;** Ctrl+S keeps one every time, as today. File history keeps 20 copies a file, so a save a second after each pause would push the older ones out within minutes; this way the 20 reach back at least 100 minutes of auto saves. An auto save says nothing and offers no "Undo save". A copy at Ctrl+S only, and a copy at every save, were the other options. Asked after the plan's review found it. Amended by Slice 6t decision 3.
   - **Slice 6s decisions (2026-11-21).** A small slice after 6n2 for issues #152 and #155, both found in 6n2's live check (`docs/research/2026-11-20-copilot-surfaces-live-check.md`, findings 1 and 2). Asked of the user on 2026-11-21, when the slice was planned, in two rounds: 1 to 5 and 7 first, then, after a read of the code the slice touches, the second round that changed 5 and 6 and added 8 to 11. The user took the recommended option each time but one (decision 11's Codex part is the user's own answer); decision 6 was given on 2026-11-20 with issue #155. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-21-phase-6s-history-tab-copies.md`.
     1. **A conversation run in a Marshell tab is copied at each turn end, for every CLI that has a transcript.** Until now only Claude Code's Stop hook asked History for a copy, and the importer was the only thing that copied a Codex, Qwen Code or Copilot CLI conversation. Now each of the three asks for one when a turn ends (Codex's Stop; Qwen Code's Stop and StopFailure; Copilot CLI's agentStop and errorOccurred) and when the session ends. Antigravity stays Resume-only: Marshell reads no transcript of it. One copy at the session end only was the other option.
     2. **"Copy conversations run outside Marshell" doesn't decide tab conversations,** as it never did for Claude Code. The label stays, and the hint under it gains one sentence: "Conversations run in a Marshell tab are kept whether this is on or off." Leaving the hint as it is, and having the switch decide both kinds of conversation, were the other options.
     3. **The rows this bug left are filled in once.** A conversation that is linked to a Marshell tab and has no copy (listed as "Untitled conversation · 0 turns") is copied at the next start, in the background, when its transcript file is still there. One from a WSL tab is copied only when its distro is already running: Marshell never starts a distro for it. Leaving those rows as they are was the other option.
     4. **WSL: only conversations run in a Marshell WSL tab,** copied from the transcript path the session's own record holds (`\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…`). A conversation run in a distro outside Marshell stays unimported (6m decision 10, 6n2 decision 11). Importing from every distro was the other option, and would be a slice of its own.
     5. **The guard's refusal names the variable** where the refused preview shows today's general sentence: "CODEX_HOME names C:\Users, a folder that holds your user profile, so Marshell doesn't write there." or "…a folder that holds Marshell's own folder, so Marshell doesn't write there.", with the variable and the folder it names. Keeping today's sentence was the other option. The second round shortened it: the app already starts every refusal with "Marshell will not edit <file>: ", so the sentence ends at the reason, as in "Marshell will not edit C:\Users\hooks.json: CODEX_HOME names C:\Users, a folder that holds your user profile." and "…a folder that holds Marshell's own folder." Keeping both parts, and dropping the app's start for this refusal, were the other options.
     6. **The guard's new rule (issue #155, the user's OK for the change to `guard.rs` on 2026-11-20).** A home variable (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `QWEN_HOME`, `COPILOT_HOME`) that names a folder containing the user profile or Marshell's home opens nothing: `C:\Users` and the profile itself are refused. 6n2 decision 12's other refusals stay. A folder such as `D:\work` can't be told from a real CLI home and stays admitted, and no rule is added for system folders, which need elevation that Marshell never runs with. The second round narrowed it for the profile itself: the guard opens the whole profile whatever the variables say, so a variable that names the profile (`CODEX_HOME=<profile>`, `QWEN_HOME=~`) adds nothing and its files are written like any other file in the profile, with the usual preview. Only a folder that holds the profile from above, or holds Marshell's home, is refused. Refusing the files such a variable would put in the profile was the other option: the guard would have to know each CLI's file list, and Marshell could neither set up nor remove its hooks for that person.
     7. **Codex's transcript check is tightened.** Only a file named `rollout-<time>-<session id>.jsonl` (or `.jsonl.zst`) for the session at hand is linked and copied. Until now any absolute local `.jsonl` or `.jsonl.zst` path a Codex hook named was accepted, and keeping that was the other option.
     8. **Codex's Interrupt copies too** (second round). Codex fires no Stop when a turn is interrupted or hits a usage limit, only Interrupt, which Marshell already installs for the status and which carries the transcript path. Stop and the session end only was the other option.
     9. **An open History view isn't refreshed by this slice** (second round). It re-reads its list when History is reopened, a filter changes, a session is renamed or an import finishes, as it always has for Claude Code. Issue #157 holds the live refresh. Adding it to this slice was the other option.
     10. **The start-up pass of decision 3 runs at every start** (second round). A turn that ends while its tab closes or Marshell quits gets no copy, and a WSL row's distro may be stopped at one start and running at the next. A row is still copied once, because it then has its copy. Once after the update, with a marker file, was the other option.
     11. **The live check** (second round). Codex runs real turns on the user's free account, with the script's copy of the sign-in file that it deletes at stop (the user: "codex is logged into my free account"). Qwen Code and Copilot CLI run against the fake model. The WSL part repeats 6n2's setup in the user's Ubuntu for Copilot CLI and adds Qwen Code the same way, with `HOME` and each CLI's home under `/tmp`. Codex in a WSL tab rests on tests. Copilot only, and no WSL part, were the other options.
     12. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these on what the code check found (2026-11-21); the user can change any of them.
        - **The events, per CLI.** Claude Code: Stop, StopFailure, SessionEnd (as before). Codex: Stop, Interrupt, SessionEnd. Qwen Code: Stop, StopFailure, SessionEnd. Copilot CLI: agentStop, errorOccurred (recoverable or not: a copy replaces the whole file, so an early one costs nothing), sessionEnd. Antigravity: none. A Copilot status line never asks for a copy.
        - **A Copilot event that names no transcript uses the path the session already has.** Only agentStop carries one. For sessionEnd and errorOccurred the path comes from the tab's record of that session, in a WSL tab and in a Windows tab alike, and only a session with no record yet falls back to the path built from Marshell's own `COPILOT_HOME`.
        - **The copy waits 1.5 s after the hook for all four CLIs,** the time measured for Claude Code. When Codex, Qwen Code and Copilot CLI write their last lines is unmeasured, so the live check compares each copy's last line with the source, and the session end's copy is the full one either way.
        - **The start-up pass covers all four CLIs that have a transcript,** Claude Code included, and goes through the same one-copy-at-a-time rule as a hook's copy. It asks Windows once for the running distros (`wsl.exe -l --running`, which starts nothing) before it touches any file on a distro's share. For Codex it also tries the path with `.zst` added, because Codex compresses old rollouts where they lie.
        - **Codex's file name rule** is: the name starts with `rollout-`, ends with `-<session id>.jsonl` or `-<session id>.jsonl.zst`, and has a time between them. The time's exact form is not checked, so a new Codex that writes it differently still shows in History. The path may hold no `..`. The importer takes a rollout only when its name ends with the id its first line gives, so nothing it copies is dropped when the index is rebuilt.
        - **When a folder holds both the profile and Marshell's home, the sentence names the profile.** The folder is shown as the variable spells it. A variable that names Marshell's home or a folder inside it keeps today's "This is Marshell's own data folder."
        - **The guard compares against both spellings of the profile:** the one Windows reports and `USERPROFILE`, which the CLI adapters use.
        - **Known limits.** The uninstaller uses the same guard, so it can't remove Marshell's hooks from a home the new rule refuses, and while such a file is in its plan it changes nothing on Windows ("Nothing was changed.") until the variable is unset; 6n2's wider rule was never in a published build. A final turn that ends after the tab has closed is copied at the next start only when the conversation had no copy yet (decision 3's pass fills rows that have none); one that had a copy keeps it without that turn until the conversation runs in a tab again, or, with the import switch on, until the importer sees the file change. The Codex name rule accepts one wrong tail, because the time isn't format-checked: an id of digits and `-` only that ends another id whose earlier groups are digits too. A drive root that holds the profile (`CODEX_HOME=C:\`) is refused by name like any other wide folder. Found by the plan's review.
   - **Slice 6t decisions (2026-11-22).** A small slice after 6r-2 for what it left open: issues #167 (git walks a folder link in a checkout), #168 (a shell that moves into a repo gets no ignore marks) and #169 (the deferred minors and a hand check). Asked of the user on 2026-11-22, when the slice was planned, in three rounds and a fourth after the plan review (decisions 11 and 12), after a read of the core's file, tree and search code and a probe of git 2.55 for Windows in a scratch repo (it walked both test junctions, 2,000 files each; `ls-files --directory` stops at an untracked folder; an `:(exclude)` pathspec keeps git out of a link). The user took the recommended option each time but one: decision 5 is the user's own design.
     1. **A folder the shell moved into gets git's ignore rules without git.** When the shell's reported folder lies inside a checkout that isn't the launch folder's, the core reads that checkout's `.gitignore` files, `.git/info/exclude` and the global excludes file itself, with the `ignore` crate (ripgrep's matcher, MIT or Unlicense), and never runs git there (#168; the reason is in 6r-2's PR #170: a folder learned from terminal output can hold a hostile `.git/config`). The tree dims what is ignored and the content search skips it. Known limit: there, a file git tracks despite a pattern shows as ignored. Running a hardened git, asking once per folder, and leaving it as it was were the other options.
     2. **In the launch folder's checkout, git lists only tracked files and the core walks the rest.** `git ls-files --cached` reads the index and walks nothing; the core walks the untracked files itself with decision 1's matcher and never enters a folder link, so a junction to a big folder costs nothing (#167). The content search runs `git grep` over the tracked files and the core's own search over the untracked ones. Every git run the Files panel starts has a deadline. Git with an `:(exclude)` for each link (no new crate, but a project with thousands of links overflows Windows' command-line length), and deadlines alone, were the other options.
     3. **An auto save also keeps a history copy when the file on disk isn't what Marshell last wrote there** (an agent or another program changed it); otherwise 6r-2 decision 24's 5-minute rule stands. So an agent's version is never overwritten by an auto save without a copy. Keeping the 5-minute rule alone was the other option.
     4. **The tree's folder-name rule stays as it is until the Linux and macOS work.** A name with `:` or `\` is refused on every platform (`tree.rs`, `layout.rs`, `paneRules.ts`), which keeps Windows' drive-relative and backslash forms out; on Linux and macOS such a folder can't be opened in the tree. That work makes the rule Windows-only. Doing it now, untested on those platforms, was the other option.
     5. **While Files waits, a 40 px round loading ring stands in the middle of the tree area, with a line under it:** "Listing files…" while the name filter's file list loads, "Searching…" while the next `?` search is due or running. The rows that were there are hidden until the answer lands, so old rows are never read as the answer; while a search is typed the ring stays up and the results come at the pause. The ring is the size of the empty panels' mark and the line sits under it as their title does. It shows only after 150 ms, so a fast answer never flashes it; it stays still under reduced motion and the app's own motion setting, and pauses with every loop while the window is hidden; in forced colours it takes the text colour. The old note under the box clears at once. A list that failed to load says "Can't list the files in <folder>.", the palette's words. This is the user's own design, given over three answers: a round ring, then the middle of the tree area and bigger, then the words under it. Inside the filter box at its end edge, and a ring over the dimmed old rows, were the other options.
     6. **A search for more than 200 characters says "Search for 200 characters or fewer." and sends nothing,** as "Type at least 2 characters to search." does below 2. Both limits count characters, as the core does, so "?😀" asks for more text instead of failing. Stopping the box at 200 was the other option.
     7. **Collapse all folders says "Collapsed all folders."** through the announcer, from the Files menu and the palette. Staying silent (6r-2's ruling) was the other option.
     8. **The hand check of Ctrl+Shift+P with real focus inside a browser page runs with the user in this slice's live check** (#169). Leaving it for release sign-off was the other option.
     9. **"Empty" (the empty-folder row) and "Automatic: At the window's edge." (the File tree position help) stay,** 6r-2's wording, now the user's.
     10. **Decided, not asked.** The plan (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-22-phase-6t-files-leftovers.md`) settles these on what the code check found (2026-11-22); the user can change any of them.
         - One rules engine in the core serves the tree's marks, the file list and the search, so `git check-ignore` no longer runs. Its missing deadline and its one-run-per-folder retry go with it.
         - In the launch folder's checkout a file git tracks is never marked ignored, which is `check-ignore`'s answer today. Git's tracked list is read again only when the index changes.
         - Where git can't run, a checkout is listed and searched by its rules alone.
         - No walk enters a link, `.git` or a nested checkout. Dotfiles are listed, as git lists them.
         - The rules are read from files only. A `.gitignore` that is a link is skipped, as git 2.32 and later skip it. A `gitdir:` naming a network or device path is not followed. A pattern file over 1 MiB is skipped.
         - A folder the shell moved into is never itself ignored. Inside `node_modules/pkg` the tree, the list and the search still show it, as ripgrep started there does; the folders above it still apply their lines below it.
         - The search lets tracked files' lines fill the 1,000-line cap first, and skips file links everywhere, outside git too.
         - The deadlines are 2 s for the list, 3 s for the search and 2 s for the tree's tracked list.
         - The record of Marshell's last write lives in memory, so after a restart the first auto save of each file takes a copy. An Undo counts as Marshell's write.
         - A name list that failed keeps the unfiltered tree under its note.
         - While Files waits, the rows hide at once and the ring follows at 150 ms. Focus in the rows moves to the filter box.
         - The ring itself isn't spoken. Its line is plain text, nothing is announced, and the tree area is `aria-busy` while it waits.
         - The "And N more" row is the last item of its folder's set.
         - A saved tree root longer than 4,096 characters is refused, and dropped at load.
         - At 32 tabs, an open from the tree that replaces the panel's preview tab goes through, as the reducer already allows.
         - A tree save flushed by a session that has just closed is dropped.
         - Matching ignores case on Windows and macOS, where git sets `core.ignorecase` when it makes a repo. A repo that turns it off there is still matched without case.
         - The global excludes file is the one the `ignore` crate finds in the user's git config. A quoted path with a space falls back to git's default file, `~/.config/git/ignore`, and `include` lines and the system config aren't read; these are known limits. A global file that is a local link is read, as dotfile managers make it.
         - A nested repo's files are never judged by the outer repo's rules, as git doesn't judge them. A folder link counts as a folder for a `dir/` pattern.
         - A file link is listed when its target is a local file, as today, and never read by the search.
         - Git's tracked list is kept for at most 8 checkouts. A list cut by its deadline is kept until the index changes, so a huge repo doesn't run git for it on every request.
         - When the shell moves, the name filter's list is read again, and the ring shows "Listing files…" in place of the old folder's rows.
         - Settled while building (2026-11-22):
           - A folder the shell moved into inside a checkout is listed by the checkout's rules alone. The fixed skip list for `node_modules`, `target`, `dist` and `build` now applies only outside git, so in a repo whose `.gitignore` doesn't name them they are listed, as git lists them.
           - A `.git` file over 4 KiB, or one whose `gitdir:` or `commondir` names something that isn't a folder, means no checkout. A `.git` that is neither a file, a folder nor a link is passed over on the walk up.
           - A tracked file inside an ignored folder isn't marked, as `check-ignore` never reports a tracked file; the folder itself stays marked.
           - A path in git's index that isn't UTF-8 is left out of the list, as it was before.
           - A session with no folder shows no "Searching…" ring.
           - A tree root over 4,096 characters is not saved, and the session's earlier saved tree stays.
           - On Windows and macOS a file renamed only in case, which git's index still holds under its old spelling, is listed, searched and marked once, under the index's spelling (the final review).
           - A cut tracked list is kept only when it was read with a request's whole budget (the list's or the tree's 2 s). The search reads it with what `git grep` left of its 3 s, so a cut list from the search is used once and not kept. A checkout whose git folder can't be followed (a `gitdir:` naming a network path) has no index to watch, so its list is read on every request (the PR review).
     11. **A repo in the home folder counts only for the home folder itself** (asked 2026-11-22, after the plan review). Below home, a folder with no repo of its own is a plain folder, for the launch folder and a folder the shell moved into alike. So a dotfiles repo whose ignore file holds `*` doesn't empty Files for every project under home, and the launch folder no longer turns the whole home folder into the tree. Using the home repo's rules, as git does, was the other option.
     12. **The `ignore` crate's notice goes in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`** (asked 2026-11-22): one "ignore and globset" entry with the MIT text, in the regex entry's format. The lockfile gains the crates it brings, all MIT or Apache-2.0. Seeing the entry before it was written was the other option.
   - **Slice 6r-3 decisions (2026-11-23).** The Changes panel: the session's git diff with line comments handed to the agent ("Slice 6r decisions" 1, 4, 7 and 9), and the tree's change marks 6r-2 decision 15 left to this slice. Asked of the user on 2026-11-23, when the slice was planned, in six rounds, after a read of the pane's, the task Review sheet's, the core's git and paste code, a web check of the five CLIs' review commands and paste handling, and spike S15 (`docs/spikes/S15-changes-git-sandbox.md`), run between the third and fourth rounds. The user took the recommended option each time but one: decision 4, where they chose to follow the shell with a hardened git, which S15 then made concrete (5). 22 is what the plan settles without asking. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-23-phase-6r-3-changes.md`.
     1. **One scroll, a section per file.** A summary on top (files, lines added and removed, commits ahead of the base, uncommitted work), then every changed file as a section: its path, its status and its counts, then its diff. A section folds from its header. A file list beside one file's diff (the task Review sheet's layout), and a tab per file, were the other options.
     2. **Changes reads and comments; it changes nothing in the repository.** No staging, committing, discarding or pushing: the agent and the terminal do those, and 6r-4 adds "Create pull request" with its push. A file's header can still open the file in Files. A per-file Discard, and staging with a commit box, were the other options.
     3. **"Main" is the remote's default branch, then the local one.** The base is origin's default branch (`origin/HEAD`, else `origin/main`, else `origin/master`), and with no remote the local `main`, else `master`. So a branch cut from a fetched `origin/main` shows only its own commits even when local `main` is behind, and on `main` itself Changes shows the unpushed commits and the uncommitted work. Marshell never fetches. The local branch alone, and a "Compare with…" menu, were the other options.
     4. **Changes follows the shell into any checkout.** It shows the checkout that holds the folder the shell last reported, else the launch folder's, so a `cd` into another repo moves Changes too; outside a checkout it says so. The Files tree keeps its own rule and stays in a launch checkout (6r-2 decision 2), so after such a `cd` the two can show different repositories; the tree's change marks are those of the checkout the tree shows (corrected 2026-11-23 after the plan review, which found the tree doesn't follow the shell out of a launch checkout). This overturns, for Changes only, 6t's rule that git never runs in a folder learned from terminal output, on the condition of decision 5. The launch folder's checkout only (my recommendation), and following the shell after asking once per folder, were the other options.
     5. **Git runs in a sandbox for every checkout.** Spike S15 showed that `-c` flags don't harden git: a repository's own config still ran its clean filter and still read its include file, which can name a network path. So git never reads a repository's config: it runs with a git folder of Marshell's own, pointing at the repository's objects, index and work tree, with a config Marshell writes from a short list of settings that change how files compare (line endings, case, symlinks, renames), read from the system, user and repository files without following their includes. No program named in any config runs, the user's Git LFS filter included. The launch folder gets the same engine, so a repository reads the same however Changes reached it. Known limit: a file stored through a filter (Git LFS, git-crypt) can show as changed once it is touched. The sandbox only where the shell moved (plain git in the launch folder), and the launch folder only after all, were the other options.
     6. **The diff refreshes by itself while Changes is on screen.** Every 2 seconds while the panel shows and the window has focus, as the tree does: a cheap check first (git's raw file list and each changed file's size and time; about 36 ms on this repo), and the whole diff only when that moved. A comment being written is never moved or lost by a refresh. At each turn end with a Refresh button, and the button alone, were the other options.
     7. **"Ask <agent> to review" types each CLI's own review command, or a written request where it has none,** without Enter: Claude Code `/code-review`, Codex `/review` (which opens its picker), Copilot CLI `/review`, Qwen Code `/review` (which reviews uncommitted work only), and for Antigravity, which has no review command, a request to review the changes against the base by file and line; the menu says so. One written request for every CLI, and no review action, were the other options.
     8. **The Files tree marks changed files,** from Changes' data: a letter at the row's end (M modified, A new, R renamed) in the text colour, read as ", modified", ", new" or ", renamed", and a small dot on a folder that holds changes. While the tree shows, the check runs on its 2-second poll even with Changes hidden. No marks was the other option.
     9. **Comments reach the agent as a paste into its prompt, never with Enter,** as region to prompt and drops do (§2.5's delivery rule): the person reads it and presses Enter. Sending is refused, and the comments kept, while the agent waits on a permission or a question, so a paste never lands in an approval. Pasting and pressing Enter, and the clipboard, were the other options.
     10. **Comments are collected, then sent together.** Each waits under its line as a draft that can be edited or deleted; "Send 3 comments" hands them over as one message. This is the diff drawer's design recorded on 2026-10-15 (phase 8). Each comment sent on Add, and both, were the other options.
     11. **A comment covers a line or a range of lines** in one file: Shift+click or Shift+Up and Down widen it. One line only was the other option.
     12. **The message gives each comment's path and lines, the quoted code, then the comment,** under "Comments on the changes (compared with origin/main):". A removed line is named as one ("src/old.ts:7 (removed line)"). Paths are plain text: no CLI documents a line range in `@path`, and `@path` makes Claude Code read the whole file. Path, lines and comment only was the other option.
     13. **Where a multi-line paste isn't safe, Send writes the message to a file and pastes one line naming it.** Codex on Windows is known to get Marshell's pastes as typed keys (`docs/research/attachment-ref.md`), and no one had checked whether a multi-line paste stays in each CLI's input under ConPTY; the plan's first task measures it for all five CLIs. A CLI that fails gets `Read my review comments in "<path>"`, the file under the session's attachments folder, so no newline is ever typed into it. One line with the quoted code dropped, and the clipboard, were the other options.
     14. **Changes' key is Ctrl+Shift+H, and ⌘⇧H on macOS,** which keeps the letter as Browser and Files keep theirs. ⌘H is Hide and ⌘⌥H is Hide Others there. A macOS binding gains ⌘ with Shift for it, the first such key. ⌘J and ⌘U were the other options.
     15. **Draft comments are kept per session through restarts,** saved with the session's tabs. When the agent changes a commented line, the comment stays, marked "The line changed", and still quotes the text it was written on. Sending clears them. Memory only, and dropping a comment whose line changed, were the other options.
     16. **Changes has syntax colours, "Hide whitespace changes" and "Wrap long lines".** The code is coloured as in the editor and the added and removed fills stay under it. The two switches are in the Changes ⋯ menu and saved with the app's settings. "Copy diff as a patch" was offered and not taken.
     17. **The diff is one list for the keyboard.** One Tab stop; Up and Down move line by line through every file, headers included; Alt+Up and Down jump to the previous and next file; Left and Right on a header fold and unfold it. Enter on a line starts a comment there, and Shift+Up and Down first widen it to a range. Shift+F10 or the Menu key opens the line's menu. Esc returns the keyboard to the terminal. A screen reader hears "Added, line 42: …". File headers in the Tab order, with the arrows inside one file, was the other option.
     18. **The title-bar button is the icon alone,** pressed while Changes shows, as Browser's and Files' are; nothing runs git while Changes and the tree are hidden. A count of changed files was the other option.
     19. **A session with no prompt line copies the comments.** A plain shell or a CLI run manually can't take a paste safely (a newline runs a command), so Send reads "Copy 3 comments", puts them on the clipboard, says so and clears them, as region to prompt does. The review action is left out of a session with no agent. No comments there was the other option.
     20. **Big and generated files start folded:** a file of more than 400 diff lines, a lockfile, or a minified file shows its header and "Show 1,234 lines". Folding and unfolding are kept per session. Everything open was the other option.
     21. **In the comment box Enter adds the comment and Shift+Enter starts a new line.** Esc closes the box; text already typed is kept as a draft. Ctrl+Enter to add was the other option.
     22. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these on what the code check and S15 found (2026-11-23); the user can change any of them.
         - Panel and copy: the panel is "Changes"; its button reads "Show Changes (Ctrl+Shift+H)" and "Hide Changes (Ctrl+Shift+H)", its Fill button "Fill the side pane with Changes". The stack order is Browser, Files, Changes ("Slice 6r decisions" 7), with a divider between each two shown panels. Modifier+Tab visits terminal, Browser, Files, Changes.
         - Changes holds no tabs. Its strip shows the summary ("4 files · +120 −18") and then ⋯, Fill and Hide; the line under it names the branch and the base ("feat/login · 3 commits ahead of origin/main · uncommitted work") and, when the shell moved, the checkout's folder. Ctrl+Tab from Changes steps the tab panel that had the keyboard before it, as from the terminal.
         - The ⋯ menu: Refresh; "Ask <agent> to review (types /code-review)"; Fold all files and Unfold all files; "Hide whitespace changes" and "Wrap long lines" as checked rows. The palette gains "Changes panel", "Refresh changes", "Ask the agent to review" and "Send comments". "Review changes" stays the task sheet's command.
         - The empty states, each with an icon: "No git repository" ("<folder> isn't in a git checkout."), "No changes" ("Nothing differs from origin/main."), "Can't read the changes" (git's sentence, with Try again), and for a session with no folder Changes can read, the tree's sentence.
         - A task session compares with its task's base branch, as its Review sheet does. Detached HEAD compares with the base the same way; a repository with no commits shows every file as new; with no base branch at all, Changes compares with HEAD and says "No main branch found. Showing uncommitted work."
         - Limits, as the task Review sheet's: the diff is cut at 4 MiB on a file boundary and says so; an untracked file over 256 KiB, or one with a NUL in its first 8 KiB, is named and not shown. A file section starts folded once 10,000 diff lines are already open above it. Deadlines: 2 s for the check, 5 s for the diff.
         - The sandbox refuses a repository whose `objects/info/alternates` names a network or device path, copies its `shallow` file, and reads no reftable repository ("This repository stores its branches in a format Changes can't read yet."). A network folder (a WSL distro's own files: 6r-1b decision 29) shows "Can't read the changes" with "{folder} is on a network share, so Changes doesn't read it."
         - Comments: at most 50 per session and 2,000 characters each, with no control character but a line break and a tab (code pasted into a comment keeps its indent), since the drafts travel in every save of the window's layout; a quoted line is cut at 200 characters, and control characters in quoted code are shown as U+FFFD. The pasted message is at most 16,000 characters, or what spike S16 found each CLI keeps if less, past which Send uses decision 13's file for every CLI. "Send" pastes, announces "Pasted 3 comments into api-server." and moves the keyboard to that terminal, as a drop does; the file form says "Pasted a link to 3 comments into api-server."; the clipboard form "Copied 3 comments. api-server has no prompt line." "Discard all" asks first, in the bar.
         - A comment's quote is at most its first three lines, then "…". A range is cited by its new-file numbers, and by its old-file numbers with "(removed lines)" when it holds only removed lines.
         - The review action and Send are refused while the session is paused, has ended, is elevated, or waits on the person, with the reason, as `marshell send` refuses.
         - Settled while building and in the live check (2026-11-23, `docs/research/2026-11-23-changes-live-check.md`):
           - S16 measured all five CLIs keeping a 16,099-character paste unsent, so every CLI is pasted into and the 16,000 cap stands.
           - At most 200 new files get a section, and the line under the strip says "And 102 more new files aren't shown."; a new file past them gets no tree mark either. A cut diff says "The diff is cut at 4 MiB. 3 more files aren't shown."
           - The switches' help lines: "Leaves out lines that differ only in spaces and tabs." and "Wraps a long line in the diff instead of scrolling it sideways."
           - A range is announced as "3 lines selected". A right-click opens the header and line menus too; "Open in Files" is off on a deleted file, and "Copy path" copies the full path and says "Copied the path.", as Files does.
           - Refusals name the reason: "{name} runs no agent, so the comments are kept.", "{name} runs elevated, so nothing was typed."
           - Enter or Esc on an empty box only closes it; Delete on a comment moves the keyboard to the item before it; "Discard all" asks with the keyboard on Keep.
           - The tree's letter is in the text colour and a folder's dot in the secondary text colour, both the system's text colour in forced colours, where the diff's syntax colours give way to it too. The marks follow the tree's own root, while Changes follows the shell.
           - A comment whose lines moved is labelled and cited at the lines they are on now, and the keyboard stays on its line when lines above it come or go.
           - A failed read replaces the diff with "Can't read the changes" and Try again, even right after a good one; an open comment box keeps its text through it.
         - Settled in the final review's fix pass (2026-11-23):
           - Git is given the work tree only after the core has looked at every folder on the way to a tracked file, each poll. git opens a submodule's repository (and a folder now at a tracked file's path) even with `--ignore-submodules=all`, so one whose `.git` leads to a network or device path, through its gitdir, `commondir`, alternates, refs or a link on the way, refuses the whole repository: "A submodule of this repository points to a network folder, so Changes doesn't read it." A pointer file that isn't UTF-8, or whose path starts with white space, is refused the same way, and an index holding a path that isn't UTF-8 shows "A file name in this repository isn't UTF-8, so Changes doesn't read it."
           - Comments written on another checkout stay with it: the list doesn't show them against this checkout's files, the line under the strip says "Send or discard the 2 comments on repoA first.", folding keeps them where they are, and Send names that checkout and its base ("Comments on the changes in C:\work\repoA (compared with main):").
           - An open comment box whose file leaves the diff stays, with its text, under the file's "gone" note.
           - The drafts are held to the layout's rules before a save, so one of them never stops the window's layout saving; a file whose name holds a colon or a backslash gets "This file's name holds a colon or a backslash, so a comment or a fold on it can't be kept."
           - The comment box takes the keyboard only when it is opened, and a redraw puts the keyboard back on the item or in the box it was in. In forced colours the Send bar's buttons and the box draw an edge, and the rows' ring, hover and selection use system colours.
         - Settled in the pre-merge review (2026-11-23):
           - A look for new files that the deadline cuts isn't run again for 10 s when it was slow, and the line under the strip says "Looking for new files took too long, so some may not be shown."
           - A send clears only the comments it held: one added or edited while it went stays. A send from another checkout keeps each file's comments together.
           - Comments on another checkout keep their line and the Send bar beside an empty diff, so they can still be sent or discarded.
           - A double-click opens what the menu's Open opens, and a deleted file gets the menu's reason, "The file is deleted, so there is nothing to open."
           - Showing Changes with its key moves the keyboard into it, as Browser and Files do: `focusPane` waits up to about 3 s for the first reply (and gives up if the keyboard went elsewhere meanwhile), and an empty state with no button lets its title take the focus. Where the keyboard was is read at the first frame, and again once what asked has left the document, so a panel shown from the command palette still takes it after the palette closes and gives it back to the terminal.
           - A new comment beside earlier ones keeps the base those were written against, as a fold does, and a new comment stays on the checkout its box was opened on when the shell moves to another repository before it is added.
           - A checkout whose list of changed files passes 8 MiB gets "This checkout has too many changed files for Changes." rather than part of the list shown as all of it.
           - With whitespace hidden, only a modified file with no mode change reads "Only whitespace changed."; a rename, a new empty file and a mode change get no note.
           - A file past the 4 MiB cut that holds a comment keeps a section, "This file is past the 4 MiB cut, so its lines aren't shown.", so the comment can be edited or deleted.
           - Send and Review wait at most 5 s for Claude Code's settings, then answer "Claude isn't ready yet, so the comments are kept." (or "so nothing was typed.").
           - A comment box saved after the shell moved to another checkout keeps the lines it was opened at, and its label reads those; a file of the same name in the checkout shown now doesn't renumber it.
           - A fold or "Fold all" keeps fold choices only for files in the diff shown now, so the choices for files long gone never reach the layout's limit of 1,000. A file that leaves the diff and comes back opens at its default.
           - The sandbox passes `core.autocrlf` on in every spelling git reads as a boolean (yes, on, 1 and so on) as well as `input`, so a CRLF file doesn't show as wholly changed.
           - Asking for a comment box while another holds typed text keeps that text as a draft first, as Esc does ("Comment kept as a draft."), then opens the new one (asked 2026-11-23). Asking again for the box that is open leaves it as it is and gives it the keyboard.
           - An edit saved after its comment was deleted or sent goes in as a new comment, so the text isn't lost.
           - With no session the Changes strip reads "Changes", not the last session's counts.
           - Not changed: each checkout Changes reads leaves its sandbox folder, with a copy of the index, under the Marshell home, and nothing removes it. The sweep that would deletes folders under the home, so it gets its own design in issue #177.
           - Not changed: a request whose connection closes mid-check (a reload of the window; the panel itself never cancels one) can leave "Looking for new files took too long, so some may not be shown." for up to 10 s. The list of new files stays the earlier one.
           - Not changed: on a checkout with thousands of tracked folders the link safety check, which runs on every check, can use up the 2 s budget, and the panel says "Git took too long to read the changes." It is measured only at 2,193 files (about 9 ms); a cache loosens a safety check, so it waits for its own measurement in issue #176.
           - Not changed: a poll that gets no answer at all (the core restarting) leaves the last diff on screen until the next poll, 1 to 2 s later; a failure the core knows of comes as a reply the panel shows.
           - Not changed: a path whose patch header git quotes (a control character, `"` or `\` in its name; `core.quotepath=off` covers the rest) isn't matched to its section, so it shows no rows, and a binary one marks the file before it as binary. Windows can't hold such a name in a work tree; Linux and macOS aren't verified yet.
           - Not changed, from the fifth review (issue #178; none of the first two can happen on Windows): on Linux and macOS a named pipe, socket or device file in a checkout hangs Changes until Marshell restarts, because the look for new files reads it; a comment on a file whose name holds a control character can't be sent, and nothing says why; and a git setting that sits in a file the system or user config includes (`[include]`, `[includeIf]`) isn't read, because the sandbox reads those configs with `--no-includes`, so a `core.autocrlf` set there is missed and a CRLF file can show as wholly changed.
   - **Slice 6u decisions (2026-11-23).** A small slice after 6r-3 for what its pre-merge review left: issues #176 (the link safety check's cost on a big checkout) and #177 (the sandbox folders are never removed), a look at the 6r-3 review fixes in the running app, and the limits of #178 written down (above). Asked of the user on 2026-11-23 in one round, after the measurements in `docs/research/2026-11-23-changes-big-checkout.md` and a read of the sandbox, task-removal and uninstall code. The user took the recommended option each time. #178's own fixes wait for the Linux and macOS work.
     1. **A slow link safety check is reused for 10 s.** When the check that keeps git off a network or device path (`worktree::reach`) took over 250 ms, its "clear" answer stands for up to 10 s while the tracked paths are the ones it looked at (the index hasn't changed), the same rule the look for new files has. A checkout where it is faster keeps it on every check, and a refusal is never reused. Measured: 9 ms on this repository, 165 ms at 20,000 files, 1.0 s at 100,000, where a whole check drops from 1.3 s to 0.33 s. This replaces "each poll" in 6r-3 decision 22 for checkouts that slow. What it costs: there, a link put in between two checks can be followed by git for up to 10 s. Planting one needs a program already running as the user, which can reach the network itself; a repository that was cloned or checked out is still looked at before git sees it, because a new index is a new look. Listing the folders on four threads (0.93 s to 0.40 s, no loosening, four cores busy on every poll), both, and leaving it were the other options.
     2. **A sandbox goes when its checkout is gone or it wasn't used for 7 days.** Each sandbox holds a file, `marshell-checkout`, with its checkout's path, written again once an hour while the sandbox is in use. At every start, off the main threads, Marshell removes a sandbox whose checkout folder no longer exists or whose file is older than 7 days, and temp files a stopped write left that are older than a day; and it removes a task's sandbox when it removes the task's worktree. It removes only a folder under `<home>/git-sandbox` whose name is the 16 hex digits made from the path its own file names, so nothing a repository says picks what is deleted, and a path that is on the network or behind a refused link is never looked for. A sandbox with no such file (made by a build before this slice) gets one the next time it is used. A second Marshell on the same home is safe: its sandboxes are in use, so their files are fresh. "Gone checkout only", and emptying the folder at every start, were the other options.
     3. **Uninstall removes the sandboxes.** `marshell uninstall` removes every sandbox under `<home>/git-sandbox` (the same test of what is Marshell's), and the folder when it is then empty, beside `<home>/bin`; it says so before it asks: "The folder <path> (the Changes panel's copies of git's index) will be removed." They are a cache with nothing of the user's in them. The rest of the home stays, as before. Leaving the uninstaller alone was the other option.
     4. **The look for new files asks the disk once per folder.** It listed each folder and then asked three more times for what the listing had just said: twice for a `.gitignore` and once for a `.git`. The listing now answers those (names compared without case, as Windows and macOS open them), and the results are the same. Measured: 0.86 s to 0.22 s at 20,000 files, 2.56 s to 1.17 s at 100,000. The Files panel's list and search use the same walk. Found while measuring #176; the user chose a profile and a small fix over an issue.
     5. Settled while planning (2026-11-23):
        - Not changed: `tasks::task_at` reads the tasks file on every tree and Changes poll (issue #176's comment). Measured at 0.07 ms with no tasks and 3.2 ms with 50, so it stays as it is.
        - Not changed: asking each folder for its modified time, to skip the ones that didn't change, was measured and dropped. The time a parent's listing gives for a folder is updated late by NTFS (a new file in it did not move it), and asking each folder itself costs 0.6 s at 100,000 files against 0.9 s for listing them.
        - Known limit: a cold disk was not measured (it needs a restart or administrator rights to empty the file cache); every number here is with the files in memory.
        - Known limit: at 20,000 files both the link check (165 ms) and the look for new files (217 ms) are now under 250 ms, so both run on every check and a check takes about 0.46 s where it took 0.26 s plus 0.86 s every 10 s. New files show at once in return.
        - Known limit: a session that still polls a checkout after its worktree was removed makes a small sandbox again (a few small files); the next start removes it.
        - Known limit: a network checkout is told by its path's text (`\\server\share`), so a checkout on a mapped drive letter is looked for at start like a local one. A drive whose letter is gone counts as a gone checkout: its sandbox is removed and made again on the next use.
        - Settled in the plan review: a sandbox that is being written while the sweep reaches it is skipped until the next start, and a removal that fails partway puts the sandbox's file back, so a later sweep can finish it.
        - Found in the live check (`docs/research/2026-11-23-changes-leftovers-live-check.md`):
          - Fixed: a comment box asked for while another held text opened without the keyboard when the kept draft sat above its row.
          - Known limit: at 100,000 files the link check and the look for new files come due in the same check every 10 s, and together they pass the 2 s budget, so "Looking for new files took too long, so some may not be shown." is up for much of the time. Tracked files always show. Letting the two come due at different times is its own change (a GitHub issue).
        - Settled in the final review (2026-11-23):
          - `marshell uninstall` waits up to 10 s per folder for a sandbox that is being written; a task's removal waits up to 2 s (the pre-merge review: one try lost to a check of any other checkout), and the sweep still makes one try. Uninstall runs alone in its process, so its wait only shows in tests.
          - Only files named as Marshell names its own temp files (`.<name>.<pid>-<n>.tmp`) are removed as temp files (the pre-merge review); any other `.tmp` file under `git-sandbox` stays.
          - A task's removal leaves the sandbox when `git-sandbox` itself is a link, as the sweep and uninstall do.
          - Declining uninstall's question still prints why `bin` stays, as it did before the sandbox made it a question.
          - Uninstall's exit code is 0 when `git-sandbox` is kept for a file Marshell didn't make: nothing of Marshell's is left in it.
          - Known limit: the lock between a removal and a sandbox being written works inside one process. A second Marshell on the same home, or an uninstall while the app runs, can remove a sandbox in use; the next check rebuilds it, and one check may fail in between.
          - Known limit: a sandbox made by a development build from before this slice has no file naming its checkout, so nothing removes it until it is opened again. No release made such a sandbox.
          - Known limit: on Linux and macOS the 10 s a clear link check stands for doesn't count time asleep, so an answer from before a suspend can stand just after it. The look for new files already works this way. It waits for the Linux and macOS work (issue #178).
   - **Slice 6r-4a decisions (2026-11-23).** GitHub in the Changes panel, first half: the pull request's and CI's status, "Create pull request" with its push, and merge ("Slice 6r decisions" 5). Asked of the user on 2026-11-23, when the slice was planned, in five rounds, after a read of the Changes panel's and the sandbox's code and a check of `gh` 2.101.0 on this PC: from a folder outside any repository, `gh pr list --repo <owner>/<name> --head <branch>` and `gh pr view --repo …` answer with no git at all; one GraphQL call returns the pull request, its checks and the repository's merge settings for 1 of 5,000 points an hour; and `gh auth status --json hosts` lists the signed-in hosts in 0.4 s. The user took the recommended option each time. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-23-phase-6r-4a-github-sync.md`.
     1. **6r-4 runs as two slices.** 6r-4a: the pull request's status, the CI checks, "Create pull request" with its push, and merge. 6r-4b: GitHub's review comments on their diff lines, and handing them to the agent. Each has its own plan, live check and pull request. One slice, and three (read-only status first, then the actions, then the comments), were the other options.
     2. **A push is the user's own `git push`, after a confirm.** Marshell runs plain `git push -u origin <branch>` in the checkout once the user confirms a sheet that names the folder, the branch and the commits. It behaves as a push from the terminal: the user's credentials, the pre-push hook and Git LFS all run, and Marshell handles no token. This is the one place Marshell runs a repository's own config, and only on that click; everything Changes reads still goes through the sandbox (6r-3 decision 5), and `gh` never runs inside a checkout: it is given the repository by name, from a folder of Marshell's own. It amends 6r-3 decision 2: Changes now changes the remote, and the branch's upstream setting, when the user pushes. A sandboxed push with gh's token (no hook, no LFS), no push in Marshell, and a request typed to the agent for all of it, were the other options.
     3. **The pull request shows in a bar under the branch line,** above the diff, so it stays in view while the diff scrolls. It holds the state ("#182 Open · Checks: 5 passed, 1 failed", "Review required"), the main button, and opens to list the checks. With no pull request it reads "No pull request · 3 commits to push" with "Create pull request…". A section at the top of the diff's list, and a fourth panel "GitHub", were the other options.
     4. **"Create pull request…" opens a sheet, and the menu can ask the agent instead.** The sheet has the title, the description, the base branch and "Create as draft". The title starts from the only commit's subject, or from the branch name when there are several; the description starts from the repository's pull request template when it has one, else from the list of commit subjects. It says how many commits will be pushed and warns when files are uncommitted ("2 files aren't committed and stay out."). Its button is "Push and create". The ⋯ menu gets "Ask <agent> to open the pull request", which types a request without Enter, the same wording for all five CLIs. The sheet alone, and a sheet that starts empty, were the other options.
     5. **Merge is a button with a menu of methods.** It lists the methods the repository allows (merge commit, squash, rebase), GitHub's default first, and asks before merging, naming the pull request, the method and the base. When GitHub says the pull request can't merge (conflicts, a failing required check, a review still needed), the button is off and says why. Where the repository allows auto-merge and checks still run, it reads "Merge when checks pass". The default method only, and no merge in Marshell, were the other options.
     6. **A failed check can be handed to the agent.** "Ask <agent> to fix" types a request that names the check, its link and the `gh run view <id> --log-failed` command into the prompt, without Enter. The agent reads the log itself, so Marshell fetches no logs. A session with no agent gets the text copied. The check also opens on GitHub. Pasting the end of the failing log too (logs can hold secrets), and links only, were the other options.
     7. **Marshell asks GitHub only while Changes shows.** With the panel on screen and the window focused: every 30 s, every 10 s while checks run or an auto-merge waits, and at once after a push, a create, a merge or Refresh. Nothing while hidden, as 6r-3 decision 18 has it for git. Asking in the background so the title-bar button can show a mark, and only on demand, were the other options.
     8. **Later pushes are a Push button, confirmed each time.** Once a pull request exists and the branch has commits GitHub lacks, the bar reads "2 commits to push" with "Push 2 commits", which opens a short confirm naming the folder, the branch and the commits. Marshell never force-pushes: when GitHub's branch has commits the local one lacks, the bar says so and leaves it to the terminal. Confirming only the first push, and no Push button, were the other options.
     9. **After a merge the checkout stays as it is.** The bar reads "Merged" with its link, and the checkout stays on its branch: Marshell never fetches or switches branches. The ⋯ menu gets "Ask <agent> to switch to main and pull", typed without Enter; a plain shell gets the two commands copied. Nothing at all, and Marshell switching and pulling itself, were the other options.
     10. **A GitHub link opens in the system browser,** where the user is signed in, so a private repository's pages and the check logs load. The Browser panel, and both from a menu, were the other options.
     11. **The bar is on by default, with a switch.** It shows whenever the checkout's origin is on GitHub and gh is signed in. Settings → Changes gets "Show pull requests from GitHub", on by default; with it off Marshell never runs gh. Off until turned on, and no switch, were the other options.
     12. **Marshell pushes from any checkout, with a warning outside the launch one.** Push and Create work wherever Changes shows a GitHub checkout (6r-3 decision 4). The confirm always names the folder, and when it isn't the checkout the session started in it adds "This isn't the folder the session started in. A push runs this repository's hooks and settings." The click and the confirm are the consent, as typing `git push` there would be. The launch folder's checkout only, and no extra warning, were the other options.
     13. **Pull requests inside origin's own repository only.** For a fork the bar still shows and creates pull requests within the fork, and adds "A fork of owner/repo" with "Open a pull request to owner/repo on GitHub", which opens GitHub's compare page. Pull requests from a fork to its parent get their own issue. Building those too, and nothing for forks, were the other options.
     14. **Without gh, one quiet line says what to do.** On a GitHub checkout with gh missing: "Pull requests need the GitHub CLI." with "Get it", which opens cli.github.com in the system browser. Signed out: "Pull requests need a GitHub sign-in. Run `gh auth login` in a terminal." with Copy. The line has Hide, which turns decision 11's switch off. A checkout that isn't on GitHub shows no line. Nothing at all, and a line with no Hide, were the other options.
     15. **With an open pull request, the diff compares with that pull request's base.** When the base isn't origin's default branch (say `develop`), Changes compares with it as last fetched (`origin/develop`) and the branch line names it, so the panel shows what the reviewers see. If that branch was never fetched, it keeps the default branch and says "This pull request targets develop, which isn't fetched here." Draft comments keep the base they were written against. This adds a case to 6r-3 decision 3. Keeping the default branch with a note was the other option.
     16. **A running push keeps its sheet open, with git's output.** After "Push and create" or the Push confirm, the sheet shows git's and the hook's output as it arrives and has Cancel, which stops the push. There is no time limit, since a hook may be slow. On failure the output stays, with Close and Try again; on success the sheet closes and the bar updates. Closing the sheet at once and showing "Pushing…" in the bar was the other option.
     17. **The checks list puts failures first and counts the rest.** Failed checks, then running and waiting ones, each with its name, workflow and time; passed and skipped ones fold into "5 passed, 1 skipped", which opens to list them. A change is announced once ("Checks passed on #182."). Every check in GitHub's order was the other option.
     18. **A draft gets "Mark as ready for review".** The bar reads "#182 Draft" and that is its main button; once ready it becomes Merge. The ⋯ menu of an open pull request gets "Convert to draft". Both are single GitHub calls with no git. Ready only, and the state alone, were the other options.
     19. **The bar works with every host gh is signed in to:** github.com and any GitHub Enterprise host `gh auth status` lists. Marshell takes the host from origin's address and passes it to gh. Only github.com can be checked live on the development PC, so an Enterprise host is built but not verified. github.com only was the other option.
     20. **Marshell pushes only to the repository the bar shows** (asked 2026-11-23, after the plan review found that `git push origin` can go somewhere other than the address the bar reads: a `pushurl`, a `pushInsteadOf` rule, several addresses). Marshell reads origin's push address too. Push and Create work only when origin has one address and it is the bar's repository; otherwise the bar still shows the pull request and its checks, and says "origin pushes somewhere other than owner/repo, so push from the terminal." The push also runs with every transport but https and ssh switched off. An SSH host alias (`github-work:owner/repo`) still shows no bar and gets its own issue. Reading `~/.ssh/config` for aliases now, and showing the address without checking it, were the other options.
     21. **Quitting while a push runs asks first** (asked 2026-11-23): "A push is still running. Quit and stop it?" Quit ends the push's whole process tree, so nothing keeps running unseen. A push that ends on its own leaves alone what it started (a sign-in window from Git Credential Manager, for one). Letting it finish unseen, and stopping it without asking, were the other options.
     22. **The pull request's base is kept in memory only** (asked 2026-11-23). After a restart the diff opens against the default branch and moves to the pull request's base when GitHub first answers; offline it stays on the default branch. Only a pull request against another base than the default is affected, and nothing new is written to disk. Saving each branch's base in a file under the home was the other option.
     23. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these on what the code read, S17 and the plan review found (2026-11-23); the user can change any of them.
         - The status has its own route and its own poll; the diff's 2-second poll is untouched, and a slow or failed GitHub ask never holds the diff.
         - Marshell uses only `gh api` and `gh auth status`. gh must be a `gh.exe` in an absolute folder of the PATH a session gets; it runs in `<home>/gh` with a short list of environment variables, 15 s and 4 MiB at most.
         - `origin` is the only remote looked at, and the bar's repository is its fetch address with `insteadOf` applied. An SSH host alias (`github-work:owner/repo`) isn't resolved, so such a checkout shows no bar; that gets its own issue. A setting that only an included config file holds isn't seen: the push runs with every transport but https and ssh switched off, and says so afterwards if git pushed to another address than the bar's.
         - A host other than github.com counts only while gh is signed in to it; `gh auth status` runs only for such hosts, at most every 10 minutes.
         - A pull request counts only when its branch is in origin's own repository. An open or draft one comes first; else the newest merged or closed one, and only if its last commit is in the local branch, so a reused branch name doesn't read "Merged". A closed one reads "No pull request · #182 was closed".
         - On GitHub's default branch the bar is one quiet line, and Marshell doesn't push that branch. A detached HEAD shows no bar.
         - With commits still to push, Push is the main button and Merge is off until they are pushed.
         - The checks summary counts passed, failed, running, waiting and skipped; the list puts failed, cancelled, running and waiting first; past 100 checks it says "And N more on GitHub."
         - The bar's controls are Tab stops before the diff; the checks list is one Tab stop with arrow keys and a row menu.
         - "Cancel auto-merge", "Convert to draft", "Open on GitHub" and "Copy link" are in the ⋯ menu. Four palette commands, none with a default key.
         - An ask while GitHub is unreachable or signed out shows one line and no actions; a pull request seen earlier is not shown as current.
         - When GitHub's limit is used up, Marshell waits until the reset, and at least a minute.
         - A second ask within 3 seconds answers from the last one. A moved `HEAD` recounts the commits to push without asking GitHub.
         - No session state stops a push: it types nothing into the session. One push per checkout at a time. Its output keeps the last 2,000 lines. A push that outlives its sheet (the window reloaded) keeps running, and the bar finds it again.
         - The push uses the git a session would find, with Marshell's own environment (not the session shell's: a proxy or an SSH agent set only in a shell profile isn't there) and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`. A Git Credential Manager window can still appear, and what the push started is left running when it ends on its own.
