An SSH question (a passphrase, a new host key) has no terminal to ask in; Cancel ends it.
         - A branch whose name starts with `-` or `+` is never pushed, and every push names the full ref on both sides. A branch name from GitHub that Marshell can't use safely makes the bar say so, and a default branch name with shell characters is never written into a command.
         - Every action names the folder it was confirmed for and is refused if the session has moved; merge, ready, draft and auto-merge act only on the pull request Marshell itself last read for that branch.
         - A commit made in the instant between the click and git reading the branch is pushed with the rest; the bar shows the true state straight after.
         - With several commits the title starts as the branch name, unchanged. The template is read from the work tree (`.github/`, the root, `docs/`), not from GitHub; a folder of several templates isn't used. "Base branch" is a text field.
         - With nothing to push, Create's button reads "Create pull request" and no push runs. If the push works and the create fails, the form stays with GitHub's sentence.
         - A pull request's base comes before a task's base branch for the diff.
         - The three requests are written in the core, one line each, the same for every CLI, and the branch names in them come from the core, never from the webview. The fix request names the repository (`--repo`) so the agent's gh finds it. A session with no agent gets the text copied; "Ask … to open the pull request" is left out there. For a plain shell, switch-and-pull is copied as two commands.
         - A fork's link opens GitHub's compare page for the parent, against the parent's default branch.
         - Quitting asks only when it goes through Marshell's own quit; when the system shuts down, a running push is stopped without a question.
         - Not verified live (S17): an accepted auto-merge, a merge held by a required check or review, a GitHub Enterprise host, a credential prompt during the push.
   - **Slice 6r-4b decisions (2026-11-23).** GitHub in the Changes panel, second half: a pull request's comments on their diff lines, handed to the agent ("Slice 6r decisions" 5, "Slice 6r-4a decisions" 1). Asked of the user on 2026-11-23, when the slice was planned, in three rounds, after a read of the Changes panel's draft-comment and pull-request code and spike S18 (`docs/spikes/S18-review-comments-through-gh.md`), run on the scratch repository while the questions were asked: one GraphQL call returns every thread, review summary and conversation comment for 1 of 5,000 points an hour; a thread whose line a later commit changed has no line any more, only the code it was written on; and resolving and replying work with gh's own sign-in. The user took the recommended option each time but three: decision 2 (they added Reply), 4 (hidden behind a switch, where folding was recommended) and 5 (a Post button in the box, where a draft first was recommended). The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-23-phase-6r-4b-review-comments.md`.
     1. **Changes shows all three kinds of GitHub comment.** A comment on a line, a range or a whole file sits on the diff. Review summaries (a review's own text, with no line) and conversation comments (where some review bots post all their findings) get a short list above the diff, opened from the pull request bar. Each can be handed to the agent. Line and file comments only, and those plus review summaries, were the other options.
     2. **Marshell resolves threads and replies to them.** A thread gets Resolve and Unresolve, one GitHub call each, and Reply, which publishes a comment under the user's name. This amends 6r-3 decision 2 once more: Changes now writes to a pull request's threads on GitHub, and still changes nothing in the repository. Resolve only (the recommendation, with replies left to GitHub or to the agent's gh), and read only, were the other options.
     3. **A GitHub comment joins the Send batch.** A thread gets "Add to send", and the bar gets "Add all 5 unresolved". They wait beside the user's own drafts, and one Send pastes everything without Enter (6r-3 decisions 9 and 10): the file, the lines, the quoted code, who wrote it and the whole thread. The user can add a note of their own to a thread first. "Ask <agent> to fix" on each thread at once (one paste per comment), and both, were the other options.
     4. **Resolved and outdated threads are hidden, with a switch.** Only unresolved threads on current lines show. The ⋯ menu gets "Show resolved and outdated comments", off by default. An outdated thread is one whose line a later commit changed, so GitHub gives no line for it. Folding both to one line each (the recommendation), and showing every thread open, were the other options.
     5. **A reply is posted from its box.** The reply box has a "Reply on GitHub" button and Ctrl+Enter posts; Enter starts a new line there. One step, with keys that differ from the draft comment's box (6r-3 decision 21), because this box publishes. A draft first with a "Post reply" button on it (the recommendation), and Enter posting at once, were the other options.
     6. **A thread follows its code.** GitHub gives a comment's line in the code as last pushed, and Changes shows the checkout as it is now, with unpushed commits and uncommitted edits. A thread finds the commented code again wherever it moved, as a draft does (6r-3 decision 15). If that code changed locally it stays at the old place, marked "The line changed", and still quotes the code the reviewer saw. A handed-over comment names the line as it is now. On lines only in a file that matches what was pushed (a list under the file's header otherwise), and GitHub's line number as is, were the other options.
     7. **A comment's text is rendered Markdown,** drawn with the renderer the Markdown preview uses, cleaned of unsafe HTML. A picture shows as a link, since the webview loads nothing from the internet, and every link opens in the system browser (6r-4a decision 10). A suggested change shows as a code block; applying it stays with the agent, since Changes edits no files. Plain text was the other option.
     8. **Only what the user sees is shown and sent.** A comment's source can hold text nobody sees once it is rendered: HTML comments and invisible characters. On a public repository anyone can write a comment and could hide instructions for the agent there. Marshell removes both before the text is shown or sent, so the message holds what the user read. Each comment goes under a line naming its author and that it comes from GitHub, and the thread says "Hidden text removed" when something was. The source unchanged with a warning and a way to read it, and the source as is, were the other options.
     9. **Reply is for threads only.** Review summaries and conversation comments have no threads on GitHub: a reply there is a new comment at the end of the conversation. They get "Open on GitHub" and "Add to send". One "Comment on the pull request" box on the list was the other option.
     10. **Marshell starts no new thread on GitHub.** The user's own Changes comments stay drafts for the agent. Posting a draft to GitHub as a new review comment gets its own issue: GitHub takes a new comment only on a line of the pushed diff, so it needs local lines mapped back to pushed ones and gives drafts a second meaning. Building it in this slice was the other option.
     11. **A sent thread is marked.** After Send, a thread reads "Sent to Claude Code" (the session's agent), kept per session through restarts, until it is resolved or gets a new comment. "Add all unresolved" skips sent ones and says so ("Add 2 unresolved · 3 already sent"). No mark was the other option.
     12. **The bar counts the comments to resolve, and new ones are announced once.** The pull request bar reads "3 comments to resolve" and goes to the first one when pressed. New arrivals are announced once ("2 new comments on #182."), as a change in checks is (6r-4a decision 17). Nothing is asked while Changes is hidden (6r-4a decision 7). The count alone, and asking in the background so the title-bar button can show a dot, were the other options.
     13. **Decided, not asked.** The plan settles these; the user can change any of them.
         - The comments are read by a call of their own, `POST /v1/sessions/{tab}/github/threads`, after each of the bar's asks, and kept for 3 seconds as the bar's answer is. The bar's query and parser are untouched. A write (`…/github/thread`) is taken only for the pull request the core last reported and a thread the core itself last read; the reply's target comment comes from that read, never from the request.
         - What is removed from a comment's source (decision 8): HTML comments, also one that runs across a code fence or never closes; link reference definitions (`[//]: # (text)` and the like, which render as nothing); invisible and direction characters, the set the bar's texts already drop, widened by the variation selectors and filler characters that can carry bytes unseen, up to everything Unicode calls default-ignorable but the emoji selector (every tag character, the shorthand and musical format controls); and control characters. A zero-width joiner stays inside an emoji, so a joined emoji stays whole and isn't marked. A code fence's own text is kept as written. Blank characters that draw as a space (a no-break space, a Braille blank) stay: they carry a bit a position, not text. A link's title is drawn as text after the link, and a link that draws nothing shows its address as its text, so neither is sent unseen (added while building, after the security review of the cleaner). After the whole-branch review the rest of a link is drawn too: a link's address that isn't https follows its text, and an https one shows when the pointer rests on the link; a picture that isn't a link to an https address shows its address after its alt text; the address and title of a link or picture inside a picture's description follow its words; an address is drawn as written and a title's hidden characters as their source; and a thread that sits on no unchanged row of the diff draws the rows its message quotes. What stays unseen without the pointer is the address of an https link that has text.
         - What can't be removed safely is drawn instead, so the panel shows everything the agent gets: a definition the cleaner didn't catch, a character entity (`&amp;`, `&ZeroWidthSpace;`) and a table show as their source text, and a code fence shows its whole first line. The cost is that a table and `&amp;` read worse than on GitHub.
         - A comment GitHub hides (minimized) is left out and counted ("2 comments are hidden on GitHub."). The user's own comments in a review they haven't submitted are left out too: nobody else sees them yet.
         - Raw HTML in a comment (`<details>`, `<br>`) shows as text, as in the Markdown preview. A picture tag (how GitHub writes a pasted screenshot) becomes a Markdown picture in the core, with only its address and alt text kept, and is drawn as a link like any picture.
         - A link inside a comment is opened with the mouse. The keyboard opens the comment on GitHub from the thread's menu; the text of a thread's first ten comments, 200 characters each, is read as the tree item's label.
         - In the message, GitHub's part comes after the user's drafts under the line "Review comments from GitHub on pull request #N, each under its author's name:". Every line of a comment's text is indented four spaces, so none can read as one of Marshell's own lines (a file citation, "My note:").
         - A thread on a file the diff doesn't show (reverted locally, or past the diff's size cut) is listed above the diff. A folded file hides its threads; the bar's button unfolds the file.
         - Limits: 100 threads, 50 comments a thread, the last 50 reviews and the last 50 conversation comments, each with "more on GitHub" when there are more; a comment's text is cut at 10,000 characters; a send holds at most 200 GitHub comments; 500 sent marks are kept per session. One send holds about 1 MB (the send route's limit): GitHub comments that don't fit stay picked for the next send and the notice says how many, while the user's own drafts always go.
         - A pick and a sent mark carry their pull request's number. Drafts belong to the folder, so switching to another branch's pull request and back keeps what was picked and sent for the first. A pick goes only when GitHub's answer lists its thread as resolved, and a sent mark when the answer lists its thread or note resolved or with another count. One the answer doesn't list stays, neither counted nor sent: it may be past the answer's cut or hidden for now, or the shell moved to another repository whose pull request has the same number. The cost: a thread deleted on GitHub leaves its pick and note stored.
         - A reply that GitHub didn't answer in time may have been posted. Marshell says so ("GitHub took too long to answer. The reply may be posted: look at the thread before you send it again.") and reads the thread again. While a reply is out, its box can't be closed or posted again.
         - A reply with a review in progress is refused with its own sentence ("You have a review in progress on #N. Submit or discard it on GitHub, then reply."), because GitHub would otherwise hold the reply back unseen (S18 finding 7).
         - The setting is `changes.resolved_comments`, "Show resolved and outdated comments", in the ⋯ menu and in Settings. The palette gets "Go to the next GitHub comment" and "Add unresolved GitHub comments to send".
         - A thread's and a note's menu opens with Enter as well as Shift+F10, since a thread has no single main action.
         - From the pre-merge review: a line that begins like a definition (`[label]:`) stays only when it surely is none, which is when more text follows its address and opens no title, so "[nit]: use const here" stays as GitHub shows it; any other goes, also where GitHub would show it ("[question]:" alone, whose address may stand on the next line); a comment mark inside a code span of one line (`` `<!--` ``) stays, since GitHub shows it and the panel draws it, but only where GitHub surely reads a code span: not after an escaped backtick, not once a tag, an address in angle brackets or a comment began earlier in the comment, and not while an earlier line of the paragraph left a backtick open (one in an indented code block still goes, with the mark); the diff hunk is asked once a thread, not on each of its comments, so a pull request with long threads fits in gh's four megabytes; a zero-width joiner stays only between two parts of an emoji, no longer between letters of scripts above the arrows (Chinese, Japanese, Korean), and the emoji selector stays only after such a part or in a keycap; and a thread's rows are compared with the checkout's without the characters the core takes out of them, so a file committed with CRLF line ends or a byte order mark doesn't read "The line changed". Left as a limit: the zero-width non-joiner and joiner that Persian, Urdu and some Indic spellings need are removed and marked, so such a word reads joined.
         - From the live check (`docs/research/2026-11-23-review-comments-live-check.md`): a resolved thread isn't sent, so with the switch on its "Add to send" and "Add your note…" rows are off with "A resolved thread isn't sent. Unresolve it first." (they used to add it, and GitHub's next answer dropped it without a word). A thread's label, which is all a screen reader gets of it, ends with the user's own note ("Your note: …", 200 characters). After a Copy that leaves nothing to send, the keyboard goes back to the list.
    - **Slice 6v decisions (2026-11-25).** The side pane without a session: Browser, Files and Changes work with no session open, and the sidebar's shell panel owns a tab set of its own. Asked of the user on 2026-11-25 in three rounds: while the Doctor's "Open file" was planned (1 to 4), after slice 7d2 merged (5 to 8), and after a design pass over the pane's, the shell panel's and the core's code (9 to 12). The user took the recommended option each time but for decision 10. The slice is built in two halves (decision 9); the plan of the first is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-25-phase-6v-1-default-folder.md`. Decision 13 is what that plan settled without asking, for the user to judge; the plan was reviewed against the code before it ran, and the review's corrections are in the text. Decisions 14 to 21 were asked on 2026-11-25 while the second half was planned (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-25-phase-6v-2-shell-pane.md`), and decision 22 is what that plan settled without asking.
      1. **The shell panel owns a tab set of its own, and focus decides whose tabs show.** The user: "the focused shell/session decides which tabs are shown". With focus in the shell panel the pane shows the shell's tabs; with focus in a session it shows that session's. This holds while sessions are open too, not only with none.
      2. **All three panels work for the shell's folder,** Changes included. What only an agent can do (review, sending to the prompt) is off there, and a sentence says so.
      3. **Each session keeps its own tabs exactly as now** ("Slice 6q decisions", second round 3).
      4. **With no session the folder is Marshell's default folder.** The user: "Marshell creates a folder on install e.g. C:\Program Files\Marshell. users are suggested to put project folders in there but not a must".
      5. **The default folder is `%USERPROFILE%\Marshell`** (the home folder's `Marshell` on other systems). Marshell makes it when it is missing, uninstalling leaves it alone, and Settings can point it somewhere else. `C:\Program Files\Marshell` (decision 4's example) can't be it: the installer is per-user and puts the app under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, and a folder under Program Files can't be written without elevation. Asking for a folder at first run was the other option.
      6. **The shell panel, the pane with no session, new sessions and the folder picker all use it.** A new session with no folder chosen, whose shell profile sets none, starts there instead of the home folder, and the launcher's folder dialog opens there. Keeping the home folder for sessions was the other option.
      7. **With no session, an open pane fills the area right of the sidebar.** Closing it brings the empty state back. With a session open the pane sits at the right as now. A pane at the right with the empty state beside it was the other option.
      8. **"Open as a session" takes the shell's tabs along.** The new session gets the shell's pane tabs, and the shell panel starts again with none, as it starts a fresh shell (slice 2e+ decision 6). The same holds when an agent CLI started in the panel turns it into a session by itself.
      9. **Two slices.** 6v-1 is the default folder, its setting, and the shell panel starting with no session. 6v-2 is the pane itself: the shell's tab set, the layout of decision 7 and the handover of decision 8. Until 6v-2 the pane still needs a session.
      10. **A browser page stays live through the handover.** A page open in the shell's tabs keeps running when the tabs move to the new session: no reload, so a half-filled form survives. This needs a new Tauri command and a line in `src-tauri/capabilities/`, which `SCOPE.md` puts behind the user's word; this answer is that word, for that command only. Reloading the page at its saved address (the recommendation) was the other option.
      11. **A shell profile's own folder comes first for the shell panel too.** With no session, the panel's shell starts in the default profile's folder when the profile sets one, else in the default folder: the order sessions use (the folder asked for, the profile's, the default folder, the home folder). The default folder always winning there was the other option.
      12. **"Default folder" sits at the top of Settings, Shell profiles,** above the profiles list: the path, "Choose folder…" and a way back to Marshell's own folder. Files and Advanced were the other places.
      13. **Decided, not asked (6v-1).** The plan settles these; the user can change any of them.
          - The setting is `default_folder` in `settings.json`. Empty means `%USERPROFILE%\Marshell`, so a folder that was never chosen follows the home folder if that moves.
          - Marshell makes its own folder at every start when it is missing, not only at the first: without it the shell panel and new sessions would fall back to the home folder with no word. Someone who doesn't want the folder chooses another one in Settings, and then Marshell's own isn't made.
          - Marshell never makes a folder that was chosen. A chosen folder must be there when it is saved ("That folder isn't there." otherwise; the folder dialog only offers folders that exist), and one that is gone later is not made again: no path that arrives in a request is ever written (`.claude/rules/local-api.md`). "Use Marshell's folder" makes Marshell's own again when it is missing.
          - Nothing is made when a session or the shell panel starts. A default folder that isn't there then (a drive that is unplugged, a folder deleted while Marshell runs) gives way to the home folder, which is what every start did before this slice. Settings then reads "This folder isn't there now, so shells and sessions start in your home folder."
          - A folder chosen in Settings must be a full path on this PC. A network share (`\\server\share`, which includes a WSL distro's `\\wsl.localhost\…`) is refused with "Choose a folder on this PC.": every start would wait on the share, and the Files tree lists nothing there (6r-2 decision 23). A mapped drive letter isn't recognised as a share and is taken; a start waits at most two seconds for it, and then takes the home folder.
          - With a session in focus, the shell panel still starts in that session's folder and never moves (slice 2e+ decision 6). The default folder is only for the panel shown with no session. The panel's header names the folder the shell really started in, which the core now answers.
          - Only a new session from the launcher or the API, and the shell panel, get the default folder. A restart, a resume (from the tab, from History or in the system terminal), a repo script, and a CLI's sign-in or update tab start where they did before.
          - The shell panel starting in the default folder doesn't list it among the recent folders; a session started there does, as in any folder.
          - The launcher's "started in …" line names the folder the session really starts in: the profile's folder when it sets one (it said "your home folder" there before, which was wrong), else the default folder by name, else "your home folder".
          - Tests never touch the real `%USERPROFILE%\Marshell`: a core that isn't told its default folder has none. A live check points it into its scratch folder with `MARSHELL_DEFAULT_FOLDER`, as `MARSHELL_DOWNLOADS` does for Downloads, because the home folder Windows reports doesn't follow `USERPROFILE`.
          - Neither uninstaller (the installer's, `marshell uninstall`) is changed: the folder lies outside `~/.marshell` and may hold the user's projects.
          - The row's help line ends with "Marshell makes it when it's missing." only while the folder is Marshell's own: a chosen folder is never made, so the sentence would be false for it (final review, 2026-11-25).
          - The dev-app guard (`.claude/hooks/guard-shell.mjs`) refuses `tauri dev` without a scratch `MARSHELL_DEFAULT_FOLDER`, as it does for the scratch homes, so an agent can't make `Marshell` in the real home folder by starting the app by hand (final review, 2026-11-25).
      14. **Pages stay live through the handover with no new command** (fourth round, 2026-11-25, asked while 6v-2 was planned; it replaces the mechanism of decision 10, not its aim). "Open as a session" keeps the shell's id, so a page of the shell's tabs is keyed by that id and its key doesn't change when the tabs move: no Tauri command, nothing under `src-tauri/capabilities/`, nothing in `build.rs`. The cost: when the panel's own shell ends and a fresh one starts, the pages of the shell's tabs load again at their saved addresses. A `browser_rekey` command (decision 10's way) was the other option.
      15. **Nothing marks whose tabs the pane shows.** With sessions open and focus in the shell panel, the pane shows the shell's tabs and says nothing about it; the tabs change with focus. The strip naming "Shell panel" (the recommendation) and a mark on the panel's header were the other options.
      16. **Esc in a pane that fills the area goes back to the shell.** With no session, Esc in the pane moves the keyboard to the shell panel's terminal and the pane stays open, as Esc beside a session goes to the session's terminal. When the shell panel isn't on screen, Esc hides the pane. Esc hiding the pane was the other option.
      17. **`marshell open` typed in the shell panel opens in the shell's tabs,** the way it opens in a session's tabs when typed there. `--session` still sends it to that session. Opening in the focused session, and refusing as today, were the other options.
      18. **With the shell panel off screen and sessions open, the pane shows the focused session's tabs.** Hiding the panel or collapsing the sidebar brings the session's tabs back; the shell's tabs are kept and show again when the panel is back and focus returns to it. Staying on the shell's tabs was the other option.
      19. **The small shell is always there.** The user: "shell panel being hidden doesn't mean that the small shell isn't running, its rule that its always there no matter what. so tabs in the situation you described will be the small shell's". So with no session the pane shows the shell's tabs whether the panel is on screen or not, and the shell runs then too. (Asked as: no session, the panel hidden, the pane opened.)
      20. **When the panel's shell ends, another starts at once, and the shell's tabs stay.** The user: "if the user does this then another shell is started instantly. keeping the rule from the prev question true". That the tabs stay is how decision 19's rule was read; clearing them was the other option.
      21. **The Changes empty state speaks of the folder, not the session.** Asked where the "no agent" sentence of decision 2 should show, the user sent the empty state ("Changes need a Git repository", "None was found for this session, or its folder couldn't be reached.") and then: "maybe we phrase it like : No Git Repository for this directory... and so on". Changes has said "No git repository" and "<folder> isn't in a git checkout." since slice 6r-3 (the screenshot's wording is older than that). What 6v-2 changes is the "and so on": the pane's other lines that say "this session" where the owner can now be the shell.
      22. **Decided, not asked (6v-2).** The plan settles these; the user can change any of them.
          - **The saved key.** The shell's tab set is saved in `layout.json` under the reserved key `"shell"`.
            - The panel's shell gets a new tab id at every start, so its id can't be the key.
            - A real tab id is `t` and 12 hex digits (`auth::new_id`), so no session can be named `"shell"`.
          - **The "no agent" sentence shows for the shell panel only:** "The shell panel runs no agent, so Review is off and comments are copied, not sent." A plain-shell session's Changes is as before.
          - **The handover never merges two sets.**
            - Into a set that already has tabs, the handover does nothing. Pane tab ids are per set (`nextId`), so a merge would renumber tabs and reload their pages.
            - A target that holds only a tree (or only drafts) keeps them and takes the shell's tabs.
          - **The fill is its own mode, `"fill"`, not `"cover"`.** Cover's Esc hides the pane and cover suspends the terminals; a filling pane has no terminal under it.
          - **History.** With History on screen the pane stays off, as today. The blocked line becomes "Close History to show the side pane." The old "The side pane shows beside a session." is no longer true with no session.
          - **The shell panel's 32-tab limit reads** "The shell panel has 32 tabs. Close one to open another." in the app. Through `marshell open` it reads "The shell panel already has 32 tabs in its side pane."
          - **The pane's copy says "folder", never "session" or "directory",** where the owner can be the shell (decision 21; the copy is in Task 7).
          - **A panel folded for lack of room counts as on screen:** its header is drawn.
          - **A shell that ends while the sidebar is collapsed.** The window learns that the panel's shell ended from the panel's terminal, which is drawn only while the sidebar is expanded. A shell that ends while the sidebar is a rail or off screen is replaced once the sidebar is expanded again, and the pane goes on listing its folder meanwhile.
          - **Restart loop guard.** A panel shell that ends three times within 10 seconds is not started again. The panel shows "The shell didn't start." with Try again. Without this, a profile whose shell exits at once would start shells forever (decision 20 read with this bound).
          - **What decides the owner.** Only focus in a session's terminal or in the sidebar outside the panel ("session"), or in the shell panel ("shell"). Focus in the pane, a browser page, the title bar or an overlay leaves the preference as it was.
          - **The panel's row is not a session.** It has no number and no agent. Push and pull-request actions in Changes work for the shell's folder, since they are not agent-only (decision 2).
          - **Where pane deliveries go.** A region's Add and "Paste path into" from the shell's tabs go to the shell panel. A region goes through the clipboard, as for any terminal with no prompt line.
          - **`--session`.** `marshell open --session <the panel's id>` typed outside the panel is refused as today ("No session …"). Only the panel's own shell reaches its set; `--session` still names sessions (decision 17).
          - **When the shell is asked for.** As soon as the window has its endpoint and its saved view, whether the panel is shown or not (decision 19 read this way). Hiding and showing the panel no longer starts or retries anything.
          - **Settled while it was built** (the ledger's rulings, and the live check in `docs/research/2026-11-25-shell-pane-live-check.md`):
            - **A filling pane slides in its own track.** While it opens or hides, the terminal's slot is held empty, so the pane never squeezes into the narrow middle track (live-check finding 1).
            - **The arrow-key cursor doesn't reach a filling pane.** With no session there is no terminal slot to mark, so `data-nav-pane` stays off; Tab and the panels' own keys reach the pane.
            - **The "no agent" sentence uses the Changes notice's existing look,** with no style of its own.
            - **A region added while the panel's shell is still starting** says "The shell is still starting.", not "No session to add it to."
            - **The panel's terminal tells the window when its shell ends,** once the exit line is written; that is what starts the next shell (live-check finding 2).
    - **Slice 6w decisions (2026-11-25).** The Files panel's layout, and the look of the pane's tabs. The user, while slice 6v-2 was in its final review: "how the files pane is separated (tree and the opened file) looks visually bugged … make sure that it fits 1,2,3 panes mode and various window sizes". What was wrong, seen in the running app at one, two and three panels: under 480 px the tree lay over the path bar's buttons (its own "Show file tree" button among them), cut the status line and chopped the "No files open" state into pieces; in a wide panel the file had two header rows and the tree one box of another height, so nothing lined up; in a short panel the empty state scrolled beside the divider. Asked of the user on 2026-11-25 in three rounds (1 to 4, then 5 to 8, then 9 to 11 with a picture of another app's Files panel). The user took the recommended option for 1, 3, 4 and 8, and chose otherwise for 5, 6, 7 and 11. Decision 2 is the first round's answer as the picture then replaced it. The plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-25-phase-6w-files-layout.md`. It gets a branch and a pull request of its own (decision 4).
      1. **Under 480 px the tree takes the file's place.** It fills the whole area under the path bar, edge to edge. The path bar and its tree button stay in view, and with no file open the tree shows in full. This refines "Slice 6r-2 decisions" 6, where the tree was drawn over part of the file.
      2. **Always two rows above the file: the tab strip, then the path bar.** The user, with the picture: "always two rows". The path bar runs across the whole panel, over the file and the tree. The filter box is the first thing in the tree's column, set in from its edges. The divider is a hairline from the path bar's line to the bottom, with a small gutter before the code. The first round's answer had put the filter box in the header row; the picture replaced that.
      3. **A short panel's empty state gives up parts by height and never scrolls.** The mark and the help line go first, then the list. The heading and the two buttons stay.
      4. **Its own branch and pull request,** after slice 6v-2, which shipped as reviewed.
      5. **Files has no Back and Forward buttons.** The user: "no need for back and foward buttons in files at all".
      6. **Once the list doesn't fit whole, the whole list goes.** Showing the rows that fit was the other option, and the recommended one.
      7. **In the narrow layout, Space on a file shows the file, as a click does.** The keyboard stays out of the editor. Space then differs between a narrow panel and a wide one, where the tree stays beside the file. Leaving the tree up was the other option, and the recommended one.
      8. **The editor's status line stays under the file.** The tree keeps its full height beside it.
      9. **The tree button stays in the path bar,** at its end, above the tree. The user, with the picture: "notice that theres a toggle button for the tree".
      10. **The pane's tabs are rounded and told apart by fill, as in the picture.** The user: "look at the opened file tab", and then "Its look too". The strip is shared, so Browser's tabs change with Files'. This replaces the flat tabs of "Slice 6r decisions".
      11. **Decided, not asked (6w), for the user to judge:**
           - **A file's tools sit in the path bar.** Rendered and Source, Select region, an image's size and the "Updated" note go after the path, before "Collapse all folders" and the tree button. The question's two options were left unpicked; "always two rows" and decision 5 leave them no other place.
           - **Alt+Left and Alt+Right still go back and forward in a file's tab.** Only the buttons go. A link followed inside a Markdown file would have no way back without them.
           - **The strip stays 28 px tall.** A tab is 24 px inside it, the height of the strip's buttons, so no panel height changes.
           - **The filter box gets a search mark,** as in the picture.
           - **After Space in the narrow layout the keyboard is on the tree button.** The tree that held it is gone and decision 7 keeps it out of the editor; Enter there brings the tree back.
           - **Closing the last file in the narrow layout brings the tree back** (decision 1). The tree button still hides it, and the empty state is under it.
           - **Decisions 3 and 6 hold for every panel's empty state.** The component is shared: Browser's list of ports goes the same way.
           - **A file under the narrow tree can't take the keyboard.** It stays mounted and is inert, so Tab never lands on something that can't be seen.
           - **Settled while it was built** (the ledger's rulings, and the live check in `docs/research/2026-11-25-files-layout-live-check.md`):
             - **The selected tab keeps the selected mark's end bar, cut to the rounded shape.** The picture shows a plain filled tab; the bar stays as the shape cue the accessibility safety net asks for beside a fill (live-check finding 1).
             - **The filter box takes the keyboard when the narrow tree is opened over a file that held it:** from the tree button, a folder in the path bar, the Files menu, and "Show file tree" in the palette (live-check finding 3).
             - **In the narrow layout a file opened from anywhere takes the tree's place:** Go to file, a recent file, a terminal link or another tab, as an open from the tree does (decision 1). Closing a tab that leaves another one selected shows that one.
             - **A tree that holds the keyboard stays up** when the open file changes behind the narrow tree (a file an agent or `marshell open` shows in a panel that had none) and when the panel narrows, whether the tree was beside the file or over it before. An open from the tree, Go to file and a click on a tab still take its place, as each moves the keyboard first.
             - **A panel that becomes narrow shows its file,** and the tree only when it has none. A tree left up from an earlier narrow layout doesn't come back over the file.
             - **Nothing of the file is drawn over the narrow tree:** a PDF's toolbar, the editor's find bar and a region's box stay under it. The file's tools leave the path bar while the tree covers it, and return with the file.
             - **"Updated" shows in the path bar also when it is the file's only tool.**
             - **In the path bar the notes come first, then the buttons:** an image's size and "Updated", then Rendered and Source, the viewport chip and Select region.
             - **A Files tab drawn with no Files panel around it keeps its own header row** for the tools, without Back, Forward, the name or the folder.
             - **A path longer than its bar scrolls and opens at its end.** The folders' names keep their width (live-check finding 2). When the bar's width changes, the path returns to its end only if it was there; a path the person scrolled back stays.
             - **A panel shorter than the heading and the two buttons can still be scrolled,** with no scrollbar drawn. Cutting a button off out of reach was the other way.
             - **Once the list is gone, the mark and the help line stay hidden,** even at a height where the two alone would fit. The order of decision 3 is kept strict.
    - **Slice 6x decisions (2026-11-26).** A panel that expands over the terminal, word wrap for every file, and two faults. The user, looking at slice 6w in the running app: "The expand 'Fill the side pane with...' buttons that each pane has needs to expand that specific pane to fill the main shell screen as well and not just the right pane", and, of a TypeScript file in the editor: "the text needs to fill the screen and not 'spill' to the sides". The same look met a browser page that stayed blank with nothing over it. The slice's plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phase-6x-expand-panel.md`.
      1. **⤢ expands its panel over the pane and the terminal in one press.** The panel takes the whole side pane and the terminal's place. The sidebar and the status bar stay. A second press puts the terminal and the other panels back. It works with one panel shown too. Two steps (the pane, then the terminal) and hiding the sidebar as well were the other options. This replaces the fill of "Slice 6r decisions" 7 and "Slice 6r-1a decisions" 10.
      2. **Each session keeps its own expanded panel, saved with the layout.** Picking a session that has none shows its terminal and the panels; coming back shows the expanded panel again, and so does a restart. One state for the window, kept through a session switch or ended by one, were the other options. The first question said the fill was already kept per session; it was one for the window, and the user chose per session again once that was put right. Which panels are shown, and their sizes, still belong to the window ("Slice 6r decisions" 3); the expanded panel is the one part of the stack a session owns.
      3. **Word wrap starts on, for every file.** The "Word wrap" setting's default is on, so code wraps too. The setting and Alt+Z still turn it off. A settings file from before is switched to on once: it reads as on until it is next saved, and from then on holds what the person chose. Leaving the default as it was, and wrapping always with no setting, were the other options. This replaces the default of "Slice 6r-2 decisions" 11.
      4. **The button is "Expand Files"** ("Expand Browser", "Expand Changes"), and its tooltip reads "Show the terminal and the other panels" while it is on. "Maximize Files" and "Fill the window with Files" were the other options. This replaces the names of "Slice 6r-1b decisions" 3.
      - What follows from them, and the two faults (not asked):
        - **An expanded panel acts as the pane does in a window under 720 px:** the terminals stay mounted and hidden, and there is no divider.
        - **A key that goes to the terminal ends the expansion:** Esc in a tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab, and the cursor's release on the terminal. The announcer says "Files is beside the terminal again." (the panel's name). In a window under 720 px, where the pane covers the terminal anyway, the same key hides the pane as before.
        - **Showing or opening into another panel ends the session's expansion,** as it ended the fill. Hiding a panel ends its expansion in every session, so it doesn't come back expanded by itself. Hiding the whole pane keeps it: shown again, the panel is expanded again. Picking the session's own row in the sidebar leaves it too. Going to a session by its number while its panel is expanded puts the keyboard in that panel, since its terminal is under it.
        - **The tooltip names only what comes back:** "Show the terminal" with one panel shown, and "Show the other panels" where the pane covers the terminal anyway (a window under 720 px, or no session). Where a press would change nothing (one panel shown and no terminal beside it) the button is `aria-disabled`, reads "No other panel is shown" and is not drawn pressed; an expansion saved for the session takes effect again once the window is wide enough.
        - **A fill saved before this slice is dropped.** `pane.panels.filled` leaves `layout.json`; a session's `expanded_panel` takes its place.
        - **The blank browser page.** The layout's 200 ms motion read the clock while it rendered. React can run a render again and throw it away while keeping what its hooks compared, so a render that fell just after the motion's end could leave the window's "the pane is hiding" cover held for good, and a covered page is hidden. The motion now draws from the time of its last frame, which is kept as state, so a render run again draws what the last one did.
        - **A file opened by a drop or by "Browse…" while the keyboard is in the narrow tree takes the tree's place** (issue #205), as an open from the tree does ("Slice 6w decisions" 1).
      - Settled while it was built (not asked; the live check is `docs/research/2026-11-26-expand-panel-live-check.md`):
        - **A keyboard with nowhere to be goes into the expanded panel.** An expanded panel covers its session's terminal, and a covered terminal can't take the keyboard. So whenever the keyboard would be left on that terminal or on nothing, it goes into the panel: a session reached by its number, a sidebar click, the palette or the tray; a launcher, a sheet or Settings that closes; a restart. A keyboard that is somewhere drawn stays there. The window tells the app when the terminals are hidden, which is after the layout's 200 ms motion, because until then the terminal is still drawn and takes the keyboard.
        - **Going to a session never ends its expansion; going to its terminal does.** The cursor's release on the session's own row keeps the panel and puts the keyboard in it, like a click on the row. Its release on the terminal, Esc in a tab and Ctrl+Shift+Tab put the terminal back.
        - **The shell panel has its own expanded panel,** like a session. Going to a session while it is up (the session's number, the palette, a launcher that closes, the cursor) hands the pane to the session, whose terminal could not otherwise take the keyboard. The shell keeps its expansion, which is back when the shell panel's terminal takes the keyboard.
        - **A second press of the button puts the keyboard in the terminal,** which takes it when it is shown again, as it does when a covering pane hides.
        - **The button is disabled only where a press would change nothing:** one panel shown and no terminal beside it. With one panel shown beside a terminal it expands over the terminal.
        - **Word wrap's switch is a mark, not a new file version.** `settings.json` stays at version 1 and gains `wrap_default_on`. A file without the mark reads as wrap on and is not written; every save writes the mark, so a later "off" holds. An older build that saves the file drops the mark, and the next start of this build reads wrap as on again.
        - **A web page follows Marshell's theme as it changes** (the user, of a Google results page: "the web page stayed dark mode after i changed to light from Marshell's settings"). A page with `prefers-color-scheme` rules or a `matchMedia` listener switched with no reload. A page that picks its theme once, when it loads, stays as it was until it is reloaded; Marshell doesn't reload pages for it.
        - **Left for later** (issue #208, the review's minor points): three tests that can't fail and one that pins a string it builds itself, "Fill" in two CSS comments and a test's name, and two focus notes from the live check.
    - **Slice 6y decisions (2026-11-26).** Faults a person meets, from the open issues. Asked which slice comes next, the user said: "for the next slice i'll let you pick the most recommended one to go for first". The lead picked this one: it asks the least of the user, who was away, and the people on the alpha meet these faults. Each report was checked against the code first, since some were weeks old. The slice's plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phase-6y-maintenance.md`.
      1. **A project inside a WSL distro that is not running stays listed, and nothing is read from it** (issue #158). It shows without its git branch, and its folder stays among the folders the config guard allows, matched by the path's text. Listing projects never starts a distro; a distro starts when a tab opens in it or a file in it is written. Leaving the project out of the list and out of the guard until its distro runs, and leaving things as they were, were the other options.
      2. **Hooks off gets Undo** (issue #93): after "Hooks off" in Settings → Agents, the same Undo the other config changes offer, for every CLI, for as long as the files still match what Hooks off wrote. Leaving it for a later slice was the other option.
      3. **An ended session's view puts the keyboard on Resume.** Going to a session that reads "Ended last time", by its number or a click, leaves the keyboard on the bar's Resume button, since the session has no terminal. Putting it on the view itself, where Enter does nothing and Tab reaches Resume, was the other option.
      4. **alpha.2 is tagged once the slice is merged** and CI is green on `main`: `v0.1.0-alpha.2`, published by the release workflow. The alpha from 2026-11-16 has none of what merged since. Waiting for the user's word was the other option.
      - The other faults, and what follows from the answers (not asked):
        - **Issue #88 was already fixed.** The launcher sits above xterm's layers (`z-index: 20`, pinned by `overlayStacking.test.ts`), so a click on it reaches it. The issue is closed with that evidence.
        - **"Open as a session" says when it fails** (the rest of issue #89; a panel shell that exits already gets a new one). The announcer says "Couldn't open the shell as a session.", and where the core has no panel shell any more the panel asks for a new one.
        - **The Terminal settings preview follows the scheme as it changes** (issue #150). With Appearance on Follow system it read the scheme while rendering, before the root element had it. It now subscribes to the root's theme and to the system's scheme.
        - **A hooks change refreshes the Company pages** (issue #90): set-up, Hooks off and its Undo. All five pages read their file again, since the change doesn't say which CLI it was for. A page with an edit in progress keeps what it shows, and its save is still refused as changed: a draft written against the old file is never planned against the new one unseen.
        - **A slow WSL at start is asked again** (issue #92). When the first `wsl -l -q` brings nothing within its 2 s and `wsl.exe` is there, the core asks once more in the background with a longer limit and, when distros come back, adds their profiles. The launcher reads the profiles each time it opens, so they show without a restart.
        - **"author" in a key is not a secret** (issue #91). A key that holds `key`, `token`, `secret` or `password` is masked as before. `auth` still masks a key (`auth`, `oauth`, `authToken`, `Authorization`, `authorisation`), except where it is only the start of "author" (`includeCoAuthoredBy`, `authorName`, `certificateAuthority`): such a key is shown, and its file can be edited in Raw. Splitting a key into words, as the issue proposed, would stop masking a run-together key such as `oauthtoken`, so the rule stays a match on letters and only "author" is let through.
        - **A session doesn't inherit Windows Terminal's marks** (issue #100): `WT_SESSION` and `WT_PROFILE_ID` are taken out of the environment of every session Marshell starts (tabs, the shell panel, administrator sessions, the outside console).
      - Settled while it was built (not asked; the live check is `docs/research/2026-11-26-maintenance-6y-live-check.md`):
        - **An unread project is known to the config guard as a distro and a Linux path,** never as a folder on disk. The guard resolves every folder it allows each time it checks a path, and resolving `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\…` starts the distro. So a project in a distro that isn't running is kept in a list of its own and matched by its text, `\\wsl$` and `\\wsl.localhost` alike. `guard.rs` changed for this, under decision 1. Which distros run is asked at most once in five seconds. A drive letter mapped to a WSL share is still read like any Windows folder.
        - **The second look at WSL is one try, 30 seconds long, on a thread of its own.** It runs only when the first detection found no WSL profile, and nothing waits for it: the app's start and its exit are as fast as before. A profiles save that lands in the same moment as the new detection is on disk, but the app shows the profiles from before it until the next start.
        - **Undo is its own route, for any earlier change:** `POST /v1/snapshots/{id}/undo` puts every file of one snapshot back in a single change, only while each file is still exactly as that change left it; otherwise it answers with the files that differ and writes nothing. The Doctor's "Undo last fix" runs the same code. A snapshot now also keeps what `managed.json` recorded for each of its files, and putting a file back puts those records back, so hooks that an Undo restores are Marshell's again for the Doctor and for uninstall (the final review's finding; an older snapshot has no records and is restored as before).
        - **The window holds one Hooks off to undo,** so the offer outlives closing Settings and ends with the app. A Hooks off for another CLI, or for the same CLI in another distro, replaces it; a Set up for the same CLI and distro withdraws it. Its block sits under that CLI's row, or under that distro's row, and takes the keyboard when the list comes back.
        - **A refused Undo stays on the page as a sentence with no button,** is announced, and keeps the keyboard on the block, since the button that was pressed is gone. A CLI's section stays drawn while it holds an offer, even when the CLI isn't found any more.
        - **Codex and trust after an Undo were not seen live:** this PC has no Codex account, and Codex stops at its sign-in before it lists hooks. The undo puts back the same bytes, and Codex trusts hook definitions by their hash.
        - **The ended view's Resume takes the keyboard even where it can't be pressed:** the button stays focusable there, so its reason can be read and the keyboard is never left on nothing.
        - **A test pinned the #91 fault and was changed with it:** `claude_masking_is_unchanged` expected `gitCoAuthor` to be hidden.
        - **"author" is let through only as that word** (the final review): the `or` must be written in the case of the `h` before it, so `authOrg`, `authOrigin` and `oauthOrganization`, where `Or` begins another word, stay masked as they were before the slice.
        - **The running-distro list is remembered from when the answer arrived,** not from when it was asked for (the final review): a `wsl.exe` that takes 5 seconds or more to answer is waited for once, not by every request.
        - **`guard.rs` got a second entry point for its tests** (the final review): `Guard::for_home_with` is `for_home` with the list of running distros passed in. What the guard allows and refuses is unchanged.
        - Left for later (issue #210): an Undo that answers 404 keeps its button; the busy Undo button in forced colours; no test for a project being read again once its distro runs; a refused Undo can't be dismissed; a deleted or symlinked project folder in a stopped distro; Hooks off leaving `{}` in a Codex home that had no `hooks.json`.
7. **Doctor**: the fix actions, on top of the phase-3 config core: "Fix X issues" and one-click undo; folds in each CLI's own doctor; detects duplicate notifications and offers to turn off each CLI's own desktop notifications (brief §5.5, moved here by slice 4e decision 4); reports the tokens injected by project references; checks Codex hook trust; waste checks (unused MCP servers and skills, long descriptions, broad scopes, a token-cost estimate); offers to raise `cleanupPeriodDays`.
   - Added 2026-10-16 (research in `docs/research/2026-10-16-accounts-cloud-extensions.md`):
     - **One-click doctor per CLI.** It runs the CLI's own doctor (`claude doctor` text, `codex doctor --json`) and opens a summary window of suggested fixes. Every fix goes through preview, backup and undo; nothing is applied silently. Antigravity has no doctor command, so it gets none.
     - **Instruction-file check, beside the doctor.** For CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md and the like, it checks per CLI for size, stale paths, contradictions and that CLI's conventions (for example Codex's 32 KiB limit), and shows suggested edits with a preview. Nothing is written until the user accepts; backup and undo apply. It is a check on files that exist; the phase-8 instruction library is where files are authored and wired.
   - Exit: a seeded broken config is fully repaired and fully undone.
   - **Slice 7a decisions (2026-11-05).** The Doctor's engine and its first checks. The slice's plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-phase-7a-doctor-engine.md`.
     1. Phase 7 is four slices:
        - **7a:** the check engine, the live Doctor page, "Fix X issues", the fix sheet and Undo. It covers the checks Marshell's code already detects, listed in decisions 5 to 8, and meets the phase exit on its own.
        - **7b:** each CLI's own doctor, and duplicate notifications.
        - **7c:** MCP servers and skills: broken, unused and token cost.
        - **7d:** instruction files, and the tokens injected by project references.

        Each later slice asks its own design questions when it starts.
     2. The report has BRIEF §5.4's three groups: Broken, Waste and Info. The group headings replace the mock's Error, Warning and Information words.
        - Each fixable item has a checkbox. Broken items are pre-checked; Waste and Info items are unchecked.
        - "Fix X issues" counts only the checked fixable items.
        - An item that needs the person, such as Codex hook trust, says what to do and isn't counted.

        This settles batch 3's open question on per-item fixing.
     3. Marshell's own checks are local, use zero tokens and touch no network.
        - They run in the background at startup, when the Doctor page opens, after a Doctor fix or undo, and on "Run again".
        - The sidebar count and the tray's Doctor item show the last result.
        - The CLI doctors (7b) run only when the person clicks.
     4. The last fix's snapshot id is kept under `MARSHELL_HOME`. "Undo last fix" therefore survives an app restart, for as long as every fixed file is unchanged since the fix.
     5. Hooks, for all four CLIs:
        - A CLI whose hooks Marshell set up (`managed.json` says so) and that are now missing, partial, or pointing at a moved bridge is Broken. The item is pre-checked, and the fix reinstalls the hooks through that CLI's own setup plan.
        - A CLI whose hooks were never set up, or were turned off with Hooks off, gets the Info line "Hooks aren't set up for <CLI>.". It opens the Agents dialog and isn't counted.
        - Codex hook trust is reported with what to do in Codex's `/hooks`. Marshell never trusts a hook for the person.
     6. Transcript retention:
        - Claude Code's `cleanupPeriodDays` and Qwen Code's `general.cleanupPeriodDays` are offered only while the key is unset (the 30-day default), never when the person chose a value. The item is in the Info group, unchecked, and its fix raises the value to 365.
        - The plan first checks whether Codex and Antigravity have such a setting. Where one has none, the item says so.
        - Amended after the check (`docs/research/2026-11-05-transcript-retention.md`): Qwen Code's `general.cleanupPeriodDays` clears rewind backups, subagent transcripts and debug logs, not chat recordings. Codex has no setting that deletes sessions by age, and Antigravity documents none. So only Claude Code gets the fix, and its item says what the other three have.
     7. WSL: the checks cover the Windows-side configs. They also cover each distro that `managed.json` says Marshell set up, but only while that distro is running. A stopped distro gets "Not checked: <distro> isn't running." The Doctor never starts a distro in the background.
     8. #94: each "changed" refusal in `ConfigService::apply_with` logs which check refused, and names the targets when they differ. #94 stays open until a run shows the cause.
   - **Slice 7b decisions (2026-11-05).** Each CLI's own doctor, and duplicate notifications. Facts checked on this PC on 2026-11-05 (claude 2.1.318, codex-cli 0.164.0, agy 1.4.12, qwen 0.26.5):
     - `claude doctor` prints plain text in about 1.3 s, needs no TTY, and has no `--json`.
     - `codex doctor --json` returns 25 checks keyed by id, each with a status, summary, details and remediation. Two of them (`network.provider_reachability`, `network.websocket_reachability`) contact OpenAI, and no flag skips them.
     - Antigravity and Qwen Code have no doctor command. `qwen doctor` would be read as a prompt.
     - Inside Marshell, a CLI's bell or OSC 9 never doubles a banner, because terminal alerts on a hooked row are already ignored.

     The decisions:
     1. A "Run CLI doctors" button on the Doctor page runs them, and only on that click (7a decision 3). The button says that Codex's doctor contacts OpenAI.
     2. Each problem a CLI doctor reports becomes an item in the groups, tagged with its CLI. An error goes in Broken and a warning in Info. The item shows the CLI's own summary and remediation. Codex's `details` are never shown, because they hold personal paths. Claude Code's text gives one item for each warning it lists, from its `- <issue>` line and the `Fix:` line after it. This was amended at plan writing, when the binary showed that the result line is followed by each warning. Antigravity and Qwen Code each get the Info line "<CLI> has no doctor command."
     3. These items are report-only: they are never counted in "Fix X issues", and Marshell acts on no other tool's diagnosis.
     4. Duplicate notifications cover only those sent outside the terminal:
        - Antigravity's `notifications: true` was to be Broken and pre-checked, with a fix that sets it to false. Amended 2026-11-05, with the user's OK, after reading the agy 1.4.12 binary:
          - It has no toast code: no `Windows.UI.Notifications`, no toast library and no AppUserModelID.
          - Its "Bell + desktop notification" is a bell and an OSC 9 escape in the terminal, which Marshell ignores on a hooked row.
          - So it gets no item and no fix, like the terminal-sequence settings below.
        - Every `notify.*` item appears only while Marshell's own banners are on.
        - Codex's `notify` command, and Notification hooks the person added, are Info items that name the command. They are never edited.
          - Amended 2026-11-05 after the final review: Codex has no Notification hook event (`docs/research/codex-cli.md`), so a group under it never runs and gets no item. Claude Code and Qwen Code have the event.
        - Terminal-sequence settings get no item: Claude Code's `preferredNotifChannel`, Qwen Code's `general.terminalBell` and Codex's `tui.notifications`.
     5. WSL follows 7a decision 7. The CLI doctors and the duplicate checks run in each distro that `managed.json` names, only while it is running, and only for the CLIs installed there. A stopped distro gets "Not checked: <distro> isn't running."
   - **Slice 7c decisions (2026-11-06).** MCP servers and skills. The facts, checked on this PC and in each CLI's docs or source (claude 2.1.318, codex-cli 0.164.0, agy 1.4.12, qwen 0.26.5), are in `docs/research/2026-11-06-mcp-and-skills.md`. All four CLIs load MCP tool definitions on demand by default.

     The decisions:
     1. 7c is two slices:
        - **7c1:** each CLI's servers and skills are listed. It covers the Broken checks (decision 3), long descriptions (decision 9), the skills' token cost (decision 10) and the turn-off fixes.
        - **7c2:** the usage scan (unused in 30 days, broad scope), and "Measure MCP servers" with the servers' token cost.
     2. **Measuring MCP servers (7c2)** is click-only and local, like "Run CLI doctors".
        - A "Measure MCP servers" button starts each enabled local (stdio) server with a timeout, reads its tool list and ends its whole process tree.
        - A remote server gets "Not measured: remote server." Measuring uses no network and no tokens.
        - The cost is the definition characters divided by 4, cut down to what the CLI loads up front: a deferred server costs its tool names and its instructions.
     3. **Broken items** are pre-checked (7a decision 2):
        - a server whose command can't be found, or that fails to start when measured;
        - a skill whose `SKILL.md` doesn't parse, or lacks a name or a description.

        The fix turns the server or skill off through the CLI's own switch and never deletes it, with preview, backup and Undo. Where there is no switch, the item is report-only and says so.
     4. **Unused items (7c2)** are Waste and unchecked, and the fix turns them off the same way. An item is called unused only when that CLI's history covers the whole 30 days, and not when it was added more recently.
     5. **Broad scope (7c2)** is report-only: a user-level server or skill used in one project only. An Info item names the project and says how to move it. The Doctor doesn't move it, because a project's own files are shared with the team.
     6. **Plugins and extensions.** Their servers and skills count toward cost and the usage check, and each item names its plugin. They get no fix, because a plugin update would undo it. Turning off a whole plugin waits for phase 8's Extensions.
     7. **Which configs are read:** user level, plus project level for the up to 30 folders in Marshell's `projects.json`. In a WSL distro (7a decision 7), user level only.
     8. **The turn-off fix writes only per-user files:**
        - **Claude Code:**
          - A user skill gets `skillOverrides.<name>: "off"` in `~/.claude/settings.json`, and a project skill gets it in the project's `.claude/settings.local.json`.
          - A `.mcp.json` server goes in `disabledMcpjsonServers` in the project's `.claude/settings.local.json`.
          - A local-scope server goes in `disabledMcpServers` in that project's entry in `~/.claude.json`, but only if the plan's check shows that a running Claude Code doesn't write over it. Otherwise it is report-only and points to `/mcp`. The 7c1 live check kept the switch: claude 2.1.318 rewrote the file and kept the key, and `claude mcp list` showed the server as "Disabled for this project" (`docs/research/2026-11-06-doctor-7c1-live-check.md`). A session already running when the fix applies wasn't checked; the user chose to ship and list that under the PR's Known limits (2026-11-06), since the item would only come back on the next check.
          - A user-level server is report-only, because Claude Code can turn it off only per project. Its item gives `claude mcp remove <name> -s user`.
        - **Codex:**
          - A server in `~/.codex/config.toml` gets `enabled = false`.
          - A skill, user or project, gets a `[[skills.config]]` entry with its `path` and `enabled = false` in `~/.codex/config.toml`.
          - A server in a project's `.codex/config.toml` is report-only.
        - **Antigravity:**
          - A server in `~/.gemini/config/mcp_config.json` gets `"disabled": true`.
          - A project server is report-only.
          - Skills are report-only, because Antigravity has no switch for one skill.
        - **Qwen Code:** a server's name goes in `mcp.excluded` and a skill's in `skills.disabled`, in the user's `settings.json`. This covers user and project items alike.
     9. **Long descriptions** are Waste and report-only: BRIEF §5.4 says to suggest shortening. A description is long when the CLI cuts it:
        - over 1,536 characters for Claude Code (`description` plus `when_to_use`);
        - over 1,024 for Codex.

        Qwen Code cuts the non-bundled descriptions to their first line once the whole listing passes 8,000 characters, so that gets one item for the listing. Antigravity documents no cut.
     10. **The skills' token cost (7c1)** is one Info line per CLI. It estimates what the skill listing loads into each session: name and description characters divided by 4, each capped where the CLI cuts it. A skill that is off, or kept out of the listing (`disable-model-invocation`, or Claude Code's `skillOverrides` value `"user-invocable-only"`), costs nothing.
     11. **A bare command missing from the PATH starts unchecked** (decided after the 7c1 review, 2026-11-06). The Doctor checks a bare command (`npx`, `node`, `uvx`) on the PATH Marshell's tabs get from the registry, but a CLI started from a shell gets that shell's PATH, which fnm or mise can extend in the PowerShell profile. So such a server is still Broken and fixable, but the page leaves it unchecked, and it says "Its command, npx, isn't on the PATH Marshell's tabs get." A missing absolute path stays pre-checked, and a command written with `${VAR}` or `%VAR%` isn't checked.
     12. **A switch that can't be applied makes the item report-only** (7c1 review). This covers a file that doesn't parse, a list that isn't one, a path the config guard refuses, and a file over 4 MiB. So does Qwen Code's switch by name when another server or skill in use has the same name, because the name would turn that one off too.

     7c2's questions, answered 2026-11-06 at the start of 7c2:

     13. **The usage scan runs with the background checks** (7a decision 3). It keeps, under `MARSHELL_HOME`, how far it has read each history file. So only the first run reads everything, and later runs read only new lines.
     14. **Marshell remembers usage.** The scan keeps each item's last use, and the oldest history it has seen for each CLI. So the 30 days of decision 4 can be covered past a CLI's own cleanup. Claude Code deletes transcripts after 30 days by default, which on this PC left 28 days of history.
     15. **When an item was added** is the earliest sign that it was there:
         - a skill folder's creation date;
         - the CLI's history listing it as available (Claude Code records each session's deferred tools and skill listing);
         - Marshell's own first sight.

         An item with no sign 30 days old isn't called unused.
     16. **Formats not yet seen.** Claude Code and Codex are judged in full, Antigravity's skills as reads of their `SKILL.md`, and Qwen Code from its source, since it has no chats on this PC. Antigravity's servers are never called unused, because no Antigravity MCP call has been seen. An Info line says that Marshell can't yet tell when Antigravity uses one.
     17. **Measure starts only what the CLI itself would start:** user-level and local-scope servers, and the project servers that the CLI approved or trusts. A project file can come from a cloned repo, so any other server gets "Not measured: <CLI> hasn't approved it."
     18. **A server that fails to start when measured starts unchecked**, as in decision 11. A CLI started from a shell can have an environment that Marshell's doesn't have. The item is Broken and fixable, and it names what failed.
     19. **The servers' token cost is one Info line per CLI**, like the skills' line (decision 10). It names the costliest servers and says how many weren't measured.
     20. **Measuring is Windows only.** A distro's servers get "Not measured: in <distro>." Measuring through `wsl.exe` waits for a later slice.
     21. **Measurements stay in memory until the app restarts**, as the CLI doctors' findings do (7b). This was the lead's default, which the user didn't change.
   - **Slice 7d decisions (2026-11-24).** Instruction files, and the tokens injected by project references. The facts are in `docs/research/2026-11-24-instruction-files.md`, checked on this PC and in each CLI's docs or source (claude 2.1.331, codex-cli 0.171.0, agy 1.5.14, qwen 0.26.5, copilot 1.0.106). Three of them correct earlier notes:
     - Claude Code reads `AGENTS.md` since 2.1.277, by default only when no `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md` or `CLAUDE.local.md` is in the folder or above it. The mode is `pluginConfigs["cc-plugin-agents-md@builtin"].options.instructionFiles`.
     - Codex cuts the file that crosses `project_doc_max_bytes` and skips the files after it, with a warning for each; it doesn't refuse them.
     - Copilot CLI expands `@path` only in `copilot-instructions.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, and only for targets inside the repo.

     The decisions:
     1. **7d is two slices** (amends 7a decision 1):
        - **7d1:** which files each CLI loads, per project, with their sizes, token estimates and each CLI's limits. It fixes nothing (decision 9).
        - **7d2:** missing imports and stale paths (decision 3), duplicates and clashes (decision 2), the suggested edits, and the tokens the reference notes inject (BRIEF §5.11). It asks its own questions when it starts.
     2. **Contradictions (7d2)** are duplicates plus a short fixed list of clear clashes, all report-only Info items that quote both lines with `file:line`:
        - the same text reaching one CLI twice, for example Copilot reading both `CLAUDE.md` and the `AGENTS.md` it imports;
        - npm, pnpm, yarn or bun named as the package manager; tabs against spaces; two indent widths.

        A real contradiction needs a model, which the Doctor doesn't run; phase 8's opt-in "Improve this file" is that.
     3. **Stale paths (7d2):** an `@import` that doesn't resolve is Broken, because the CLI loads less than the file says (Claude Code skips it without a word, Qwen Code leaves the literal `@path` in the text). A backticked relative path that doesn't exist in the project is Info and report-only. Plain words that only look like paths are never checked.
     4. **Size:**
        - Text a CLI cuts or skips is Broken and report-only. The item says which part never reaches the agent: Codex past 32 KiB of project files, an Antigravity rule over 24,000 bytes or past its 20,000-token budget, a Claude Code file over 4 MiB, and any limit the live check finds (decision 10).
        - More than 4k tokens loaded at every session start, for one CLI in one project, is Waste and report-only.
        - Every token figure is characters divided by 4 and says that it is an estimate.
     5. **One Info line per project** gives each installed CLI's start-up estimate, for example "Airport: at start, Claude Code loads about 2.4k tokens of instructions, Codex 1.9k, Copilot 4.1k." Expanding it lists each CLI's files in load order with their estimates, imports included. Files that load later are marked "loads when used" and aren't counted.
     6. **Which folders are checked:** user level, plus each project in `projects.json` as if a session started at its root (7c decision 7), including the folders above the root that a CLI walks up to. Nested files below the root (an `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` in a subfolder, path-scoped rules) are listed as "loads when used" with their size. In a running WSL distro, user level only (7a decision 7).
     7. **The settings that change what loads are followed, and the detail says why a file is in or out.** These are Codex's `project_doc_fallback_filenames` and `project_doc_max_bytes`, Qwen Code's `context.fileName` (which replaces the default list), Claude Code's `claudeMdExcludes` and its AGENTS.md mode, and folder trust: Codex and Qwen Code load no project files in an untrusted folder. Examples: "AGENTS.md: not loaded, CLAUDE.md is here." and "Not loaded: Codex doesn't trust this folder." Nothing is changed.
     8. **Auto memory counts,** marked "auto memory" and only up to what the CLI loads: Claude Code's `MEMORY.md` loads its first 200 lines or 25 KB, and any other CLI's memory file the plan finds is treated the same way. Memory gets no size items, because the CLI writes it itself.
     9. **7d1 fixes nothing.** A Codex item past 32 KiB says what gets cut, and that `project_doc_max_bytes` in `~/.codex/config.toml` raises the limit or a shorter file helps. Raising it was the other option; it makes every Codex session there cost more.
     10. **Facts not yet settled are checked in the 7d1 live check,** with real sessions in a scratch home (a few short prompts): when Antigravity loads rules and what its `@[label](path)` import resolves from, Copilot CLI's size limit, and the numbers behind Claude Code's warning at about 200 lines. Until a fact is settled the item says what is unknown, and one that stays unknown reads "<CLI> documents no limit."
     11. **The 4k Waste item suggests that CLI's own way to load text only when needed,** and names the largest files:
         - Claude Code: `.claude/rules/` with `paths:`, or a skill.
         - Copilot CLI: `.github/instructions/` with `applyTo`.
         - Antigravity: a rule with `trigger: model_decision`.
         - Qwen Code: `.qwen/rules/` with `paths:`.
         - Codex: a nested `AGENTS.md` in the folder it is about.
     12. **`globset` becomes a direct dependency of `marshell-core`** (the user's OK, 2026-11-24). It matches Claude Code's `claudeMdExcludes` globs against absolute paths. It already ships inside the app through `ignore`, and its line in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` now names the Doctor too. A hand-written matcher was the other option.
     13. **Claude Code's open facts are settled in a scratch `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` that the user signs in to themselves** (2026-11-24). No credential file is copied. Codex and Antigravity use the live check's own sign-in flags, and Qwen Code and Copilot CLI run on its fake models, whose request logs show the exact text each CLI sends.
     14. **A project's folder trust is read the way each CLI reads it** (from the plan's review, 2026-11-24). Codex leaves a project's files out only for a folder marked `untrusted`; 7c's stricter rule, which asks for `trusted`, stays for project configs. What Codex and Qwen Code do with a folder that has no entry is checked in the 7d1 live check (decision 10).
   - **Slice 7d2 decisions (2026-11-25).** What is wrong inside the instruction files 7d1 lists, two fixes, and the tokens the reference notes add. The slice's plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-25-phase-7d2-instruction-checks.md`. Decisions 1 to 9 are the user's answers; 10 to 17 are the plan's own rulings, for the user to judge. The plan was reviewed before it ran, and the review's corrections are in the text.
     1. **Two edits are offered, each through the preview, the backup and Undo, and each unticked at first** (amends 7d decision 9):
        - removing a dead `@import` (decision 2);
        - raising Codex's `project_doc_max_bytes` (decision 6).

        Everything else in this slice is report-only.
     2. **A dead import is Broken** (7d decision 3). One item for each import whose file doesn't exist, in a file some CLI loads at start or when used:
        - Title: "CLAUDE.md in Airport imports a file that doesn't exist", or "~/.claude/CLAUDE.md imports a file that doesn't exist" at user level.
        - Detail: "Line 12: @docs/setup.md." and then what each CLI that reads the file does with it: "Claude Code loads nothing for it." and "Qwen Code, Copilot CLI and Antigravity leave it in as text." (only the CLIs that read this file are named).
        - The fix removes the import from the file: the whole line when the line holds nothing else (a list marker aside), otherwise the import and one space beside it. The sheet shows the file's diff. Several fixes in one file are one edit of that file.
        - Not fixable, with the reason in the row: a file the config guard doesn't open ("Marshell doesn't change this file.") and a file in a WSL distro ("Edit the file in Ubuntu.").
        - The same file imported under two spellings (`@gone.md` and `@./gone.md`) is one item, and the fix removes both. A user-level file's dead import is one item, not one for each project.
     3. **Only an `@` that looks like a path counts as an import that failed** (issue #197). An `@` word whose file exists is an import, as before. One whose file doesn't exist is a dead import only when it starts with `./`, `../`, `/`, `~/` or a drive letter, or when it has a slash and its last part has a file extension with a letter in it (`@docs/setup.md`), or when it is a bare name with one of the extensions md, markdown, mdc, mdx, txt, json, jsonc, yaml, yml, toml, xml, ini, cfg, conf, js, jsx, ts, tsx, py, rs, go, sh, ps1, css, html, sql or lock (`@AGENTS.md`). Punctuation at its end (`.,;:!?)]}'"`) is left out of that test. Anything else (`@someone`, `@john.doe`, `@types/node`, `@v2`) is prose: it gets no item and no row under "Show the files". The same dead import written twice in one file is one row and one item, with the first line's number.
     4. **A backticked path that doesn't exist is Info and report-only** (7d decision 3). One item for each file with at least one:
