        - Title: "AGENTS.md in Airport names 3 paths that don't exist" ("names a path that doesn't exist" for one).
        - Detail: up to five, as "Line 14: src/old/api.ts." each, then "And 2 more." when there are more.
        - What is checked: an inline code span, outside fenced blocks, that is one relative path with a slash in it and none of whitespace, `*`, `?`, `[`, `{`, `<`, `$`, `%`, `://` or `...`. A `:line`, `:line:col` or `#anchor` at the end is dropped first. A path that starts with `path/to/`, `/`, `~`, `-`, `@` or a drive letter is skipped. So is one whose first folder isn't in the project and whose last part has no file extension (`origin/main`, `text/html`, `owner/repo`): it is most likely not a path.
        - It exists when it is there from the project's root or from the file's own folder.
        - **A path `.gitignore` covers is never reported** (the user's answer): build output and installed packages are expected to be missing.
        - Only files inside a project are checked, once each however many CLIs read them. User-level files, files above the project and files in a distro have no project to resolve from.
     5. **Duplicates are Info and report-only** (7d decision 2), in two forms (the user chose both):
        - **A file one CLI loads twice at start:** "Copilot CLI loads AGENTS.md twice in Airport", with "Once by its own name and once as an import of CLAUDE.md. About 250 tokens are sent twice."
        - **A repeated block:** a paragraph (or a fenced block) of 200 characters or more, compared with its spaces and line ends folded, that is in two files one CLI loads at start, or twice in one. One item for each pair of files: "CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md in Airport repeat 3 paragraphs" ("repeat a paragraph" for one), with "Claude Code and Copilot CLI load both. The first is at CLAUDE.md:12 and AGENTS.md:30: “Use pnpm for the frontend and Cargo for…”. About 420 tokens are sent twice." The quote is the paragraph's first line, cut at 80 characters. A block that is twice in one file reads "CLAUDE.md in Airport repeats a paragraph", with "Claude Code loads it. The first is at lines 12 and 40: “…”. About 60 tokens are sent twice."
     6. **Raising Codex's limit** is the fix of the item "Codex cuts its project instructions in <project>". It sets `project_doc_max_bytes` in the Codex home's `config.toml` to the smallest multiple of 8,192 that holds what the project's files need now. The item's detail gains "The fix sets project_doc_max_bytes to 49152, for every project. Codex then loads about 1.8k more tokens at each start here." With several such items ticked, the largest value is set once. Over 1 MiB it is not offered, and the row keeps 7d1's sentence.
     7. **Clashes are Info and report-only** (7d decision 2), looked for in **everything one CLI loads at start in a project, the user-level files included** (the user's answer), because the agent gets both lines and can't tell which wins. Three kinds, one item for each kind in a project:
        - **Package manager:** two of npm, pnpm, yarn and bun named for use. "Two package managers are named in Airport", with "AGENTS.md:12 says pnpm: “Use pnpm for the frontend…”. ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md:4 (user level) says npm: “Always run npm install before…”. Claude Code loads both."
        - **Tabs against spaces:** "Tabs and spaces are both asked for in Airport", in the same form ("says tabs", "says spaces").
        - **Two indent widths:** "Two indent widths are asked for in Airport", in the same form ("says width 2", "says width 4").
        - With several CLIs, the item shows the first pair found, taking the CLIs in the Doctor's usual order, and names every CLI that loads both files.
     8. **Reference notes are counted, and heavy use is flagged** (the user's answer: "i don't want marshell to raise the token usage too much"). Counting adds nothing to a session.
        - Marshell keeps, for each project, how many notes it added and their size for each of the last 30 days, and how often each alias was in one. It is in `<Marshell home>/projects/<id>/reference-notes.json`, beside the references. It holds alias names and numbers, never a prompt.
        - Info, for a project with at least one note: "Airport: 14 reference notes in the last 30 days, about 1.1k tokens", with "About 80 tokens a note. Marshell adds a note to a prompt that names an alias."
        - Waste, with at least 5 notes averaging over 150 tokens: "Reference notes in Airport average about 210 tokens", with "A note this long names several aliases at once." and "Shorten or remove aliases in Settings, Projects."
        - Waste, with at least 10 notes and one alias in more than half of them: "The alias “app” is in 12 of 14 reference notes in Airport", with "A name this common adds a note to most prompts." and "Rename it to something you only type when you mean it, or remove it, in Settings, Projects."
     9. **A finding's row opens its file** (the user's answer). "Open CLAUDE.md" closes the Doctor and opens the file at the finding's line in the side pane's text editor of the session on screen. A finding about two files gets a button for each. With no session on screen the press says so and the Doctor stays open. **The side pane without a session is its own slice, 6v, after this one** (the user's answer, 2026-11-25): the pane's three panels work for the sidebar's shell too, and "the focused shell/session decides which tabs are shown". A file in a WSL distro gets no button (issue #165).
     10. **7d1's two leftovers** (issue #197, the user's pick): decision 3 above, and **a user-level file over a CLI's limit is one item**, not one for each project: "Antigravity cuts ~/.gemini/GEMINI.md", with no project named. The same holds for every finding of this slice whose files are all at user level: it is one item, with no project in its title: "~/.claude/CLAUDE.md and ~/.claude/rules/style.md repeat a paragraph", "Claude Code loads ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md twice", "Two package managers are named at user level" (and "Tabs and spaces are both asked for at user level", "Two indent widths are asked for at user level").
     11. **A fix preview reads the instruction files only when an instruction fix is ticked** (amends 7d1's exit criterion 7). Any other fix reads none, as before.
     12. **What counts as naming a package manager** (the plan's ruling): a line with `npm`, `pnpm`, `yarn` or `bun` followed by `install`, `i`, `add`, `ci`, `run`, `test`, `exec`, `dlx`, `build`, `dev` or `start`; or one with the name after "use", "uses" or "using"; or one with the name and "package manager". A name after "not", "don't", "never", "avoid", "instead of" or "no" on its line is not named for use. `npx`, `bunx` and a `-g` or `--global` install never count. Commands inside fenced blocks count, and capitals don't matter ("Use Bun").
     13. **What counts as asking for an indent** (the plan's ruling): only a line outside fenced blocks that has "indent" in it (indent, indents, indented, indentation). With "tab" or "tabs" it asks for tabs; with "space" or "spaces" for spaces; with both, for the one that isn't after a negation, and a line that leaves that open is skipped. A number from 1 to 8 before "space", "spaces" or "-space", or after "indent" words such as "indent of", "indent width" and "indent size", is its width; a line that names two widths gives none. Two widths clash even when the lines are about two languages; the item quotes both lines, so the person judges.
     14. **Quoted lines stay on this PC** (the plan's ruling). 7d1's items quoted no file content; these quote one line of at most 80 characters. The report is held in memory and no diagnostics export has it; no log line carries a quote, since the logs are exported.
     15. **Auto memory and managed files are read for nothing here** (the plan's ruling): the CLI or the organisation writes them, so they get no duplicate, clash, stale-path or dead-import item, and neither does a file one of them imports.
     16. **In a WSL distro** the user-level files get dead imports (report-only), duplicates and clashes; there are no stale paths, since there is no project (7d decision 6).
     17. **HTML comments** (the plan's ruling, from its review). Claude Code and Qwen Code strip them before reading; Copilot CLI and Antigravity don't. So an import inside a comment is a dead import only for the CLIs that read comments, and when one of them reads the file the fix removes the import inside the comment too. Stale paths, repeats and clashes never read inside a comment.
8. **Editors + instruction library** (wired per CLI with a content hash and drift check; settings raw mode validated against the SchemaStore schema), smart and native recaps, diff drawer, step timeline, saved prompts, broadcast, and Extensions (moved from phase 6 by slice 6a decision 2).
   - Diff drawer comments (added 2026-10-15, after the Codex app's review pane): a **+** on a diff line adds a comment. "Send comments" puts them all in the prompt line as one message, with `file:line` before each. Like phase 2.5, it fills the prompt line and never sends on its own.
   - Instruction library items: frontmatter form, "what loads here" view, token estimate with a 4k warning, templates and one-click project profiles, per-project toggles, agent filter, globs, and "Improve this file" (headless, opt-in).
   - Exit: one doc wired into all three CLIs, verified by each CLI loading it; the 32 KiB Codex limit warns before it truncates.
   - **Phase 8 decisions (2026-11-27).** Asked of the user on 2026-11-27, when phase 8 was started.
     1. **Phase 8 runs as eight slices, the library first:**
        - **8a:** the instruction library: named docs at user and project level, copied into all five CLIs with a content hash, drift flagged in the library and the Doctor, on/off per project doc, a CLI filter and globs. It carries the phase's exit criterion.
        - **8b:** what loads here (the load order, a token count per doc, the 4k warning), templates, project profiles, and snapshot history with restore.
        - **8c:** editors for each CLI's own files (`CLAUDE.md`, rules, `AGENTS.md`, skills, subagents, commands): a tree by scope, the frontmatter form, and templates for a new file.
        - **8d:** the settings editors: raw mode checked against the schema, the project and local scopes, and builders for permission rules, hooks and environment variables.
        - **8e:** Extensions: MCP servers, skills and plugins across the CLIs, and an MCP entry translated between their formats.
        - **8f:** the opt-in model features: smart recaps, "Improve this file", and Claude Code's native recap.
        - **8g:** the turn view: the step timeline (tool calls, their times and tokens) and the changes since your last prompt.
        - **8h:** saved prompts with variables, and broadcast to several sessions.
     2. **Already built, and not built again:** the company tabs' form and raw mode for all five CLIs (slices 6e2 to 6g), the Doctor's instruction files and checks (7d1, 7d2), and comments sent from the Changes panel (6r-3, 6r-4b). The "diff drawer" of this phase is the Changes panel; 8g adds only "since your last prompt".
   - **Slice 8a decisions (2026-11-27).** The instruction library. The facts are in `docs/research/2026-11-27-instruction-library-wiring.md`, checked on this PC (claude 2.1.332, codex-cli 0.171.0, agy 1.5.16, qwen 0.26.5, copilot 1.0.106). Decisions 1 to 12, 31 and 32 are the user's answers; 13 to 30 and 33 to 38 are the plan's rulings, from those facts and from the code.
     1. **Codex gets a project doc through the repo's `.codex/config.toml`,** in a tagged section of `developer_instructions`, which only Codex reads. `AGENTS.md` is never touched, so no other CLI loads the doc twice. This replaces "Codex through a tagged block inlined into `AGENTS.md`" (BRIEF §5.6 and its Codex amendment). Codex loads the section only in a project it trusts, and the project's value replaces a `developer_instructions` set in `~/.codex/config.toml`, for that project (both checked with `codex debug prompt-input`).
     2. **A user doc loads in every project; globs narrow it.** With globs, it loads only when the agent works on matching files. Codex has no globs, so a doc with globs skips Codex and its row says why. On/off per project is for project docs only (this amends BRIEF §5.6's "an enabled toggle per project" for user docs).
     3. **The library is a page of its own, "Instructions",** full window like Doctor and History, opened from the app menu and the palette. The left list holds the docs; the right side holds the open doc's name, description, CLIs and globs, its text in the editor, and a "Loads in" list with one row per CLI.
     4. **A project doc lives in the repo at `.marshell/instructions/<name>.md`,** committed, with its description, CLIs and globs as frontmatter. A user doc lives in `<Marshell home>/instructions/<name>.md` (BRIEF §5.6).
     5. **The copies in a project are committed.** They are ordinary files in the repo, so a teammate without Marshell gets the doc in every CLI. Each copy names its source and holds a hash, so Marshell and the Doctor can tell a hand edit.
     6. **Ctrl+S saves the doc only.** A bar then reads "4 copies are out of date" with Review. Review shows the diff of every copy, and Apply writes them all with one Undo. Several saves can be reviewed together.
     7. **A copy edited outside Marshell gets two fixes,** each with preview and Undo: "Put the doc's text back" rewrites the copy, and "Use this copy's text" takes the edit into the doc, which then leaves the other copies out of date (decision 6). Both the library's row and the Doctor offer them.
     8. **Copies are named `marshell-<name>.md`,** flat in each folder: `.claude/rules/marshell-security.md`, `.agents/rules/marshell-security.md`, `.qwen/rules/marshell-security.md`, `.github/instructions/marshell-security.instructions.md`. A copy is never mistaken for the person's own file, and never clashes with one.
     9. **The live check confirms Claude Code with the user's sign-in.** The user signs in once to the check's scratch Claude Code home, at the PC, and `/memory` and `/context` show the doc. That uses their account for a turn or two.
     10. **A new doc's CLIs:** a user doc goes to the CLIs found on this PC, and the user folders of the others are left alone. A project doc goes to all five, since teammates may use any of them.
     11. **The page shows "Every project" and one project:** the focused session's, or the shell panel's when no session is open. A picker switches to any project Marshell knows, as Settings → Projects does.
     12. **WSL is in 8a.** A project in a distro gets its copies like any repo, written through `\\wsl$`. A user doc also goes to the home of each detected distro, while that distro runs; Marshell never starts a distro for it.
     13. **Where each copy goes** (the research note's table):
         - Claude Code: `~/.claude/rules/marshell-<n>.md` (under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` when set) and `.claude/rules/marshell-<n>.md`, globs as `paths:`.
         - Codex: a tagged block in `~/.codex/AGENTS.md`, or in `AGENTS.override.md` when that exists and isn't empty (Codex then reads only it), under `CODEX_HOME` when set; the project section of decision 1. Always loaded.
         - Antigravity: `~/.gemini/config/rules/marshell-<n>.md` and `.agents/rules/marshell-<n>.md`, with `trigger: always_on`, or `trigger: glob` and `globs:`.
         - Qwen Code: `~/.qwen/rules/marshell-<n>.md` (under `QWEN_HOME` when set) and `.qwen/rules/marshell-<n>.md`, globs as `paths:`.
         - Copilot CLI: `~/.copilot/instructions/marshell-<n>.instructions.md` (under `COPILOT_HOME` when set), and in a project the Claude Code copy of decision 14, or `.github/instructions/marshell-<n>.instructions.md` when the doc isn't for Claude Code. Globs as `applyTo:`.
     14. **In a project, Claude Code and Copilot CLI share one copy in `.claude/rules`,** because Copilot CLI reads that folder. A project doc for Claude Code therefore also reaches Copilot CLI, and the CLI menu shows Copilot CLI ticked and dimmed with "Copilot CLI reads .claude/rules, so it loads this too." At user level each has its own copy, since Copilot CLI doesn't read `~/.claude`.
     15. **Codex's user copy goes in `AGENTS.md`, not `developer_instructions`,** because a project's `developer_instructions` replaces the user's (decision 1): user docs would vanish in every project that has a project doc.
     16. **Codex's project section keeps the person's own text.** Marshell's docs sit between `marshell:begin` and `marshell:end` lines inside the `developer_instructions` string, edited with `toml_edit`; text outside them stays. When `~/.codex/config.toml` sets its own `developer_instructions`, Codex's row warns: "Your developer_instructions in ~/.codex/config.toml stop loading in this project, because the project sets its own."
     17. **Trust is shown, never changed.** Codex's row reads "Codex loads it once you trust this project in Codex." with no trust entry, and "Codex doesn't trust this project, so it loads none of its instructions." when untrusted. Qwen Code's row says the same under its folder trust. Both are read-only reads, as in 7d1.
     18. **Globs are written in each CLI's syntax:** a `paths:` list for Claude Code and Qwen Code, an `applyTo:` comma string for Copilot CLI, and `trigger: glob` with a `globs:` comma string for Antigravity, each project pattern prefixed with `**/` because Antigravity matches absolute paths (so `src/**` also matches a deeper folder named `src`, and Antigravity's row says so).
     19. **A doc's frontmatter** holds `description`, `clis` (a list of `claude`, `codex`, `antigravity`, `qwen`, `copilot`), `globs` (a list) and, for a project doc, `enabled`. The page's fields edit it, and the editor shows the body only.
     20. **A project doc's On switch is `enabled` in its frontmatter,** so the team shares it. Off removes the doc's copies through Review; the doc stays.
     21. **Deleting a doc** removes its file and its copies through Review, with one Undo.
     22. **What a copy holds:** the CLI's frontmatter first (none for an always-loaded Claude Code, Qwen Code or Copilot CLI copy), then one line, `<!-- marshell:doc <name> <hash> from <source path>; edit it in Marshell -->`, then the body. Claude Code and Qwen Code strip the comment; Antigravity, Copilot CLI and Codex get it as a few tokens of text. Codex's block and section carry the same line as their `marshell:begin`.
     23. **A doc's name** is letters, digits, `-` and `_`, unique in its scope, compared without case. Its copies use the name in lower case.
     24. **Size:** Antigravity cuts a rule over 24,000 bytes, and its row warns before Review. Library docs don't count toward Codex's `project_doc_max_bytes` (decision 1), so the phase's "the 32 KiB Codex limit warns before it truncates" now means: a Codex block in a user `AGENTS.md` adds to what the Doctor's 7d1 item measures, and that item keeps warning.
     25. **The Doctor's drift item** is an Instructions item, "<copy path> changed outside Marshell", with decision 7's two fixes, each unticked at first, as 7d2's fixes are.
     26. **The page has no shortcut** (no free letter, as Settings). It is "Instructions" in the app menu and "Open Instructions" in the palette, and Esc closes it as it closes the Doctor.
     27. **A CLI not found on this PC still gets a project copy** (decision 10). Its row says "Not installed on this PC. The copy is for teammates who use it."
     28. **In a distro,** a user doc goes to the CLIs Marshell finds there, as 7d1's distro line does. A stopped distro's row reads "Ubuntu isn't running, so its copies wait." and its copies join the out-of-date count when it runs again.
     29. **The doc itself saves like a file in the editor** (6q-4): a snapshot, then an atomic write, no preview, since it is the person's own text. The copies always go through Review.
     30. **8a's exit, as amended:** one doc, at user level and at project level, copied into all five CLIs and confirmed loaded by each: Codex with `codex debug prompt-input`, Copilot CLI with `copilot instruction list --json` and its fake model's request, Qwen Code with its fake model's request, Antigravity with `/context` (sign-in copied as in 6g decision 3), and Claude Code with `/memory` and `/context` (decision 9); and one project and one user doc in a running distro.
     31. **`marshell uninstall` removes the user-level copies and keeps the docs.** It lists each copy with its diff, as it lists the hooks, and removes it, on Windows and in each distro it already visits: every `marshell-*.md` copy whose marker line (decision 22) parses, and every tagged block in Codex's `AGENTS.md`. A file of that name without a marker stays. The docs stay in `<Marshell home>/instructions`, so nothing written is lost. Project copies are committed and always stay.
     32. **The Doctor also reports copies that are out of date,** one Info item per project and one for the user level, such as "4 copies of SECURITY are out of date in Airport", with a fix that updates them through the same preview and Undo. The page's bar (decision 6) counts the same copies.
     33. **A doc can be renamed.** The doc is saved under its new name and the old file goes; the old name's copies then have no doc, so Review removes them and writes the new ones.
     34. **A copy edited outside Marshell is a Broken item, unticked at first** (decision 25), because the CLI loads text the doc doesn't hold. Its checkbox fix is "Put the doc's text back". "Use this copy's text" is a button on the row (a new `DoctorAction`), since a Doctor item has one checkbox fix; it saves the doc and leaves the other copies to decision 32's item and the page's bar.
     35. **"Use this copy's text" takes the body only,** the text after the marker line (inside the block or section for Codex). The copy's frontmatter is the CLI's rendering of the doc's settings, so a hand edit to it is put back by the next update, and the row says "Takes the text, not the frontmatter."
     36. **The config service may write `<Marshell home>/instructions`,** as it may write `<Marshell home>/agents`, so deleting a user doc and removing its copies are one change with one Undo (decision 21). The rest of the Marshell home stays closed.
     37. **The library's frontmatter is read by its own small parser,** for the four keys of decision 19 (quoted or plain strings, flow `[a, b]` or block `- a` lists, `true`/`false`). A key it doesn't know is kept as it is on save. No YAML crate is added.
     38. **A distro gets copies when the Doctor would check it:** a running distro Marshell set a CLI up in, with the CLIs found there (`distro_facts`, as 7d1's distro line).
9. **Release.**
   - Scope: tagged-build workflow, signed and notarized artifacts (NSIS per-user + MSI, dmg, AppImage/deb/rpm), checksums, release notes, the Tauri updater with a signing key and a rollback path, installers that run the unhook on uninstall, a manual release checklist file (including the macOS run), the WebView2 bootstrapper and the arm64 build (moved from phase 2 on 2026-10-15: installer concerns), `cargo-deny` and `pnpm audit`, licence notices for bundled fonts, sounds and OpenConsole and for every bundled crate and npm package (slice 9a decision 15), and the formal trademark search.
   - Exit: a signed build installs, updates and uninstalls on all three OSes with zero trace left in any CLI config.
   - **Same experience on another PC (2026-10-23).** A person installing Marshell on a fresh PC gets what the development machine gets. Decisions:
     1. What it covers: the app and its bundled assets (fonts, sounds, ConPTY and OpenConsole, the WebView2 bootstrapper, banners under Marshell's own name with click-to-focus, the tray, first run in under 60 s); agent setup (first run detects the installed CLIs and installs hooks and the status line the same way on a PC with no earlier Marshell and a different CLI config); and settings carry-over (Settings → Advanced exports Marshell's settings, layout and switches to one file and imports it on another PC, through the preview and backup rules of the config safety layer). Shells and fonts that only some machines have (pwsh 7, Git Bash, a Nerd Font, Windows Terminal settings) are not part of the promise; the documented fallbacks apply.
     2. How it is checked: a CI job installs the built NSIS installer per-user on a clean Windows runner and runs a smoke test (the app starts, the core answers on loopback, a shell session opens and echoes, the bundled fonts and `conpty.dll` load, uninstall leaves nothing behind). The hand check runs in Windows Sandbox (a fresh Windows on every start): install, first run, one Claude Code session with hooks, a banner and its click, the tray, and a settings import, written to `docs/research/` with the build's version.
     3. Exit addition: both checks pass on the release build before it is tagged.
   - **Slice 9a decisions (2026-11-14).** Alpha builds that the user's friends install with winget, before v1 (the user: "not v1 yet, still alpha versions. its for my friends who wants to try it"). The evidence is `docs/research/2026-11-14-winget-alpha.md`. The slice runs next, before 6q-3b.
     1. **What moves forward from this phase:** a workflow that runs on a tag, builds the per-user NSIS installer for x64 and publishes it as a GitHub pre-release with its checksum. Signing, the MSI, the updater, arm64, macOS and Linux stay in phase 9.
     2. **It costs nothing** (the user: "something that wont cost me any money"). So there is no certificate and the alpha installers are unsigned. Windows SmartScreen warns on the first run, and the release notes say so.
     3. **Where: Microsoft's community repository** (`microsoft/winget-pkgs`), so a friend types one `winget install`. Each GitHub Release also carries the manifest folder as the fallback when a submission is stuck in review or refused: a friend runs `winget settings --enable LocalManifestFiles` once as administrator, then `winget install --manifest <folder>`.
     4. **The id is `Doofess.Marshell`,** one id for the alpha and everything after it. Alpha versions read `0.1.0-alpha.N`, which sort below `0.1.0`, so a friend moves to v1 with `winget upgrade`. This replaces the user's first answer the same day, to take the id from the signing identity: the alpha has none.
     5. **The release workflow submits each version** with Microsoft's `wingetcreate`, after the release is published. It needs a classic GitHub token of the user's with the `public_repo` scope, stored as a repo secret; making the token is the user's step. The first version is submitted by hand, since the tool only updates a package that exists.
     6. **Friends update with `winget upgrade` only.** The alpha has no in-app updater, so nothing changes the installed version behind winget.
     7. **Decided, not asked:** the manifest says `InstallerType: nullsoft` and `Scope: user`, and its name and publisher match the Add/Remove Programs entry. The uninstaller runs the unhook, which moves forward from this phase too: today it only removes the `marshell://` link (`src-tauri/windows/hooks.nsh`), and a friend who uninstalls must not be left with hooks in a CLI's config that point at a program that is gone. `winget uninstall` runs it silently, so the unhook works with no window. The clean-runner smoke test (decision 2 of "Same experience on another PC") moves forward in the part the alpha needs: install, start, uninstall, nothing left behind.
     8. **Open, settled by the first submission:** the community repository has no rule against an unsigned installer or an alpha, and none that allows them; its malware scans decide, and review takes from hours to days. If it refuses the package, the fallback in decision 3 stands and the user is asked what next.
     9. **Uninstall keeps `~/.marshell`** (the user's choice). The uninstaller takes Marshell's hooks and its skill out of every CLI and removes `~/.marshell/bin`, as `marshell uninstall` does. Settings, layout, history, recordings and snapshots stay, so a reinstall finds them and the snapshots still undo the removal. The release notes name the folder to delete for a full clean-up.
     10. **The publisher is `Doofess`** in winget and in Add/Remove Programs (the user's choice). Tauri would otherwise write "marshell", the second part of the app's identifier.
     11. **The first release goes out without another question** (the user's choice). Once the build and the installer smoke test pass, the lead tags `v0.1.0-alpha.1` and the workflow publishes the pre-release. The lead then says when to add the token. With it in place the workflow opens the pull request on `microsoft/winget-pkgs`, and the user signs Microsoft's contributor agreement there.
     12. **The installer smoke test runs in the release workflow, before anything is published, and as a dry run on pull requests that touch the installer** (the user's choice): the installer script, the Tauri config, the release workflow or the release scripts. `ci.yml` stays as it is.
     13. **Built by Sonnet workers and the lead, on branch `phase-9a` in the main checkout** (the user's choices).
     14. **Decided, not asked:**
         - The installer carries `marshell-hook.exe`, `marshell.exe` and `marshell-host.exe` next to the app. No installer had been built before this slice, and the bundle held only the app and ConPTY, so an installed copy would have had no hooks, no `marshell uninstall` and no administrator sessions.
         - The installer writes a `QuietUninstallString`. Tauri writes only `UninstallString`, and winget runs the quiet one when there is one. Without it `winget uninstall` would likely open the uninstaller's window (not confirmed; the smoke test settles it).
         - The unhook is `marshell uninstall --yes`, run by the uninstaller before it removes files. An uninstall that is part of an update skips it. If a CLI's file can't be changed, the uninstall goes on and `~/.marshell/bin` stays, so the hooks left behind still find their bridge.
         - The version comes from the tag: `v0.1.0-alpha.3` builds version `0.1.0-alpha.3`, stamped into the build and not committed.
         - The workflow submits with `wingetcreate submit` and the manifest folder it built, for the first version too if the tool accepts a new package that way. Decision 5's "by hand" stands only if it doesn't. The secret is named `WINGET_TOKEN`. A release published before the token exists is submitted later by a manual run of the workflow.
         - The smoke test never reads the core's token. It asks the installed `marshell list`, and a small example program (`crates/cli/examples`) puts real hooks in scratch CLI folders with the app's own install plan.
         - After a release is published, a job on a fresh runner installs it with winget from the release's manifest folder, starts it and uninstalls it with winget. That is the exit check's "PC that never had Marshell". The same job runs against the community repository once Microsoft accepts the package.
     15. **alpha.1 ships with `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` as it is** (the user's choice, 2026-11-16). The file covers ConPTY, the fonts and eight crates. Listing every bundled crate and npm package is phase 9 work, so it lands before v1. Until it does, each alpha's release notes say the file isn't complete and point at the lock files; that paragraph in `scripts/release/notes.mjs` and its test go when the list is complete. alpha.1 is also published before the user has added the token (the user's choice), so its submission is the manual run decision 14 describes.
     16. **Decided, not asked (2026-11-16), from alpha.1's first run:** winget starts the unsigned installer through the shell with the internet mark on it, so SmartScreen's "Windows protected your PC" dialog comes up for an install from the manifest folder too, and winget waits on it without a limit. The release notes tell a person to choose "More info", then "Run anyway". On the runner nobody can, so the smoke test sets `SEE_MASK_NOZONECHECKS=1` for winget; it changes nothing on a friend's PC. Every program the smoke test starts now runs under a time limit, and both smoke steps have one in the workflow. The evidence is `docs/research/2026-11-14-alpha-installer.md` section 3.
     - **Where alpha.1 stands (2026-11-17):** published as a pre-release on 2026-11-16 by the tag's run. That run's own `winget-check` did not finish (decision 16), so the rest came from runs by hand: after the fixes, `winget-check` on `main` installs, starts and uninstalls from the manifest folder (20 checks passed, 5 skipped: a run by hand has no fixture, so it checks no hooks); the hooks' removal through winget is shown by the probe's runs with the fixture; and `winget-submit` opened [pull request 482075](https://github.com/microsoft/winget-pkgs/pull/482075) on `microsoft/winget-pkgs`, which Microsoft's bot closed within a minute, as it did every pull request opened in those minutes. After pull request #134 (the title says "New package:" until the package is listed, issue #133) the submission was run again and opened [pull request 482096](https://github.com/microsoft/winget-pkgs/pull/482096), which stayed open and waits for the owner's contributor agreement and then for Microsoft's validation and review. Until Microsoft has the package, `winget install Doofess.Marshell` finds nothing and the fallback manifest is the winget route. Nobody has checked the exit below on a friend's PC yet. The evidence is the research note's section 3.
     - Exit: on a PC that never had Marshell, `winget install Doofess.Marshell` (or the fallback manifest, until Microsoft has the package) installs the alpha with no administrator prompt, the app starts, and `winget uninstall` leaves no trace in any CLI config.
   - **Slice 9b decisions (2026-11-26).** The release blocker in issue #212: an uninstall with Marshell open left `OpenConsole.exe` and the install folder behind, so the dry run for alpha.2 failed. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phase-9b-uninstaller-waits.md`; evidence: `docs/research/2026-11-26-uninstall-leaves-openconsole.md`.
     1. **It is fixed on its own, before alpha.2** (the user's choice), not worked around in the smoke test and not shipped as a known limit.
     2. **The uninstaller waits** (the user's choice): after the app is closed it waits, with a limit, until the programs Marshell started have ended, and nothing is killed. That also covers an app that was killed or had crashed.
     3. **alpha.2 is tagged once this is merged and a dry run on `main` is green, and its winget submission is held** (the user's choices): the tag's run is cancelled while `winget-check` runs, so `winget-submit` is skipped, and the check is then run by hand. The user submits later with `gh workflow run release.yml -f action=submit -f tag=v0.1.0-alpha.2`.
     4. **Decided, not asked:**
        - How the wait is built. Tauri's NSIS template (CLI 2.12.1) closes the app and deletes the files with no hook between the two, so the uninstaller's last hook tries Marshell's program files again, every quarter of a second, until they are gone or 20 seconds of pauses have passed, and then removes the folder. It does so first in that hook, before the hooks are taken out of the agent CLIs: the uninstall entry is gone by then, taking the hooks out can last minutes, and an install that follows may write the same files in that time. (It was built last in the hook; the pre-merge review led to the move, and the user chose to fix it before the merge, 2026-11-26.) "The helper has ended" is tested by deleting its file, never by a process name: Windows Terminal runs an `OpenConsole.exe` of its own for every tab.
        - The limit is 20 seconds. After it the uninstaller gives up without a word and exits as before; what is still in use stays, and the folder with it. That is what an administrator session's `marshell-host.exe` does, which nothing here closes.
        - The hook names the six programs the installer puts in the folder (the app, `marshell.exe`, `marshell-hook.exe`, `marshell-host.exe`, `OpenConsole.exe`, `conpty.dll`) and touches nothing else. A test (`scripts/release/hooks.test.ts`) fails when a config gains a program (a resource that is an exe or a dll, or a sidecar) that the hook doesn't name.
        - It runs for every kind of uninstall. That includes the one a newer installer starts when the person picks "uninstall before installing", which is the installed version's own uninstaller: that install meets free files only once the installed version carries the wait. A silent update (`winget upgrade`) runs no uninstaller at all, so the wait does nothing for it (issue #214).
        - The wait and its limit are tried by `scripts/release/hooks-check.ps1`, on stand-in files in the temp folder, with no install. It is run by hand after a change to the hooks file. It ran once in the release build job, on this slice's dry run, and passed there; the step was taken out again, because a change to CI is the user's to decide (`SCOPE.md`) and the user had not been asked. Open for the user: whether the release build should run it.
        - The smoke test now says what runs from the install folder when an uninstall leaves a file.
        - **The installer is not changed.** A silent install over a running Marshell skips a file that is still in use and exits 0, so an update can keep an old `OpenConsole.exe`. The template has no hook between closing the app and copying the files; a hook that came after could only carry a second copy of each program and would need the same wait. The bundled ConPTY, `marshell-host.exe`'s code and its protocol are the same in alpha.1 and alpha.2, so a kept file does no harm today. Left as issue #214.
        - **Left for later (issue #215):** the two questions for the user (the check in the release build; a line in the release notes about closing Marshell before an update), a gap in `hooks.test.ts` (nothing pins that the wait only looks at files in the install folder), and what was not watched: whether the second try was needed in the green run, an uninstall beside an open administrator session, and the dry run that hung.
     - Exit: the pull request's release dry run passes, with "A7 uninstall: the install folder holds no file" among its passes.
   - **Slice 9c decisions (2026-11-26).** Issue #216: cancelling the `v0.1.0-alpha.2` run didn't hold its winget submission, which failed only because the fork `Doofess/winget-pkgs` couldn't be synced. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phase-9c-release-hold.md`; evidence: `docs/research/2026-11-26-winget-hold-and-fork-sync.md`.
     1. **A repository variable holds the submission, and a cancel stops the winget jobs** (the user's choices). While the variable `WINGET_HOLD` is set to anything but `false`, a tag's run builds, publishes and runs `winget-check`, and `winget-submit` ends green with a notice that says how to submit later. The two winget jobs' conditions use `!cancelled()` in place of `always()`, so cancelling the run stops them too; a cancel is a race, and the variable is the hold.
     2. **The hold goes on now** (the user's choice): it is set once this slice is merged, because alpha.1's new-package pull request (microsoft/winget-pkgs#482096) is still open and any tag would open a second one. It comes off once #482096 is merged, and alpha.2 is then submitted by hand.
     3. **The workflow brings the fork up to date first** (the user's choice): before `wingetcreate` runs, `winget-submit` asks GitHub, with the owner's token, to sync the fork with `microsoft/winget-pkgs`. When GitHub refuses, the job stops with an error that says to press Sync fork on the fork's page and run `submit` again. The token keeps `public_repo` and nothing else.
     4. **Decided, not asked:**
        - A manual `submit` ignores the hold: it is an explicit request for one release.
        - `winget-check` still runs while the hold is on. It installs the release on a fresh runner and submits nothing.
        - Without the token, the notice is the token's, as before, whatever the hold says.
        - The decision lives in `scripts/release/winget-hold.mjs` with its own tests, as the version and the notes do, and `scripts/release/workflow.test.ts` pins how the workflow is wired to it.
        - A fork that doesn't exist yet is left to `wingetcreate`, which makes it. A fork whose `master` has commits of its own isn't synced: the job stops with an error, because a merge would carry those commits toward the pull request.
        - Only an empty value or exactly `false` lets a tag submit. `FALSE` and blanks hold, so a typo holds rather than submits.
     - Exit: a manual `check` run of this branch's workflow starts `winget-check` with `publish` skipped, and cancelling the run stops the job; the tests pass; after the merge, `gh variable list` shows `WINGET_HOLD`.
   - **Slice 9d decisions (2026-11-26).** Issue #214: a silent install over a running Marshell (`winget upgrade`, the smoke test's step A5) skips a program file that is still in use and exits 0, so an update can keep an old `OpenConsole.exe` beside a new `conpty.dll`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phase-9d-installer-waits.md`; evidence: `docs/research/2026-11-26-installer-skips-held-file.md`.
     1. **The installer waits** (the user's choice). Its first hook (`NSIS_HOOK_PREINSTALL`) closes a running Marshell with the template's own macro (`CheckIfAppIsRunning`, which asks first when the installer has its window), then waits, every 250 ms for up to 20 seconds of pauses, until none of Marshell's six program files in the install folder is in use. The template's own check, which comes next, then finds nothing to close. As in the uninstaller (slice 9b decision 2), nothing else is closed or killed.
     2. **A file still in use at the limit stops the install before anything is copied** (the user's choice). A silent or passive install stops, the installed version stays as it was, and the message names the file. With the installer's window it asks Retry or Cancel (the user's choice): Retry waits another 20 seconds, and Cancel stops the same way.
     3. **The copy** (the user's choice; `<file>` is the first program still in use):
        - while it waits, the details line "Waiting for Marshell's programs to end (up to 20 seconds)", and the uninstaller's line becomes the same;
        - the dialog: "Marshell has closed, but its program `<file>` is still running, so the installer can't replace it." and, as a second paragraph, "Retry waits for it again. Cancel stops the installer and changes nothing.";
        - the stop: "Marshell wasn't updated: `<file>` was still running 20 seconds after Marshell closed. Nothing was changed. Run the update again."
        - **When the earlier version is already removed** (the user's choice, after the plan's review found it): a windowed upgrade has "uninstall before installing" checked by default, and that runs the installed version's uninstaller before the wait. When Marshell's main program file is gone by the time the wait gives up, the dialog's second paragraph reads "Retry waits for it again. Cancel stops the installer; the earlier version has already been removed." and the stop reads "Marshell wasn't installed: `<file>` was still running 20 seconds after Marshell closed. The earlier version had already been removed. Run the installer again."
     3a. **The stop exits 32, and the winget manifest says what that means** (the user's choice, after the plan's review). Every stop sets the error level to 32 before it aborts; the template's own aborts keep 2. The installer manifest maps 32 to winget's `fileInUse`, so `winget upgrade` prints winget's own "One or more files are being used. Exit the application then try again." rather than only the code. winget has no default return codes for NSIS installers (`GetDefaultKnownReturnCodes`, winget-cli `ManifestCommon.cpp`).
     4. **The release notes' Update section gains one line** (the user's choice): "If an update stops because one of Marshell's programs is still running, run it again; its message says whether the earlier version is still installed." Its wording follows decision 3's second case; the first draft said "nothing has changed", which isn't true when the earlier version was removed first.
     5. **The release build runs `scripts/release/hooks-check.ps1`** (the user's choice, issue #215), before the smoke test. It tries both waits on stand-in files.
     6. **From issue #215** (the user's choices): a test pins that both waits look only at files in `$INSTDIR` and never wait by process name; the uninstaller's details line says how long it waits (decision 3); the smoke test also holds `OpenConsole.exe` during the uninstall with the app open (step A7), so the dry run shows the uninstaller's wait waiting.
     7. **Failing first** (the user's choice): the smoke test's new checks are pushed before the fix, so the pull request's dry run shows today's installer skipping the held file. Then comes the fix and its green run.
     8. **Decided, not asked:**
        - "In use" means that opening the file for writing, with the sharing `File` uses (`CreateFileW` with `FILE_SHARE_READ`), fails with a sharing or lock violation (errors 32 and 33), which is what a running program, a loaded library and a file another program holds all give. A read-only file gives error 5 first, whether or not it is in use (the final review found that), so the wait clears the read-only flag, tries again, and puts the flag back; `File` clears the flag itself when it copies. A read-only file that nothing holds therefore isn't waited for. A missing file isn't waited for, so a first install doesn't wait. All of this was tried on stand-ins with the NSIS Tauri uses.
        - One list of the six programs (`MARSHELL_EACH_PROGRAM`) feeds both waits, and `scripts/release/hooks.test.ts` keeps it in step with the bundle.
        - The installer uses the uninstaller's limit and step (20 000 ms of pauses, 250 ms). The seconds in the copy come from the limit.
        - The silent stop's line goes to the installer's standard output, after attaching to the console that started it when there is one. Whether winget shows it isn't known.
        - Cancel stops with the same line as a silent stop.
        - The smoke test tells a replaced file from a skipped one by its write time. A silent update reinstalls the same build, so a hash would match even for a skipped file. Before A5 the test sets every installed file's write time to 2001-01-01, and `File` puts back the build's own time. The test holds `OpenConsole.exe` open from before the update until 8 seconds after the old app has ended, then checks that no file kept the mark and that the installer ended after the file was let go. The installer writes `OpenConsole.exe` straight after the 34 MB main program, so a shorter hold could end before today's installer reaches it, and 8 seconds still leaves 12 of the wait's 20.
        - Nothing runs the Retry dialog: `hooks-check.ps1` runs silently, and no installer runs on the development PC.
     - Exit: the pull request's first dry run fails the new A5 check that every program was replaced, naming `OpenConsole.exe`; after the fix the dry run passes them, "A7 uninstall: the install folder holds no file" with the hold, and the build job's `hooks-check.ps1` with `RESULT … 0 failed`.
10. **Accounts and cross-device** (after v1; proposal, research and sources in `docs/research/2026-10-22-accounts-and-cross-device.md`). Sign in with GitHub, a device registry with approval from an existing device, remote terminals through an end-to-end encrypted relay, and conversation handoffs between PCs. Slices: 10a spikes and the GitHub App, 10b sign-in, 10c devices, 10d remote view, 10e remote input, 10f handoffs, 10g later (remote approve cards, remote banners, a GitHub mirror, a direct path).
   - Exit: on two PCs, a session on one is viewed and typed into from the other with the host showing who is connected; a closed session's handoff reaches the next session on the other PC; signed out, the app is today's app and a test asserts it opens no outbound socket.
   - **Phase 10 decisions (2026-10-22).** Every question in the proposal's §8 was answered with its recommendation:
     1. Sign-in is GitHub's device flow through a GitHub App that asks for no permissions. The Worker checks the user token once with the client secret, keys the account by the numeric user id, and revokes the token: nobody keeps a GitHub token. The device keeps only Marshell's own rotating refresh token, in the OS keychain, never in a file. The loopback web flow is a documented fallback that spike S13 tests.
     2. The backend is a Cloudflare Worker, one Durable Object per account on the WebSocket Hibernation API, and D1, open source under `cloud/`, run on the owner's Cloudflare account, with the relay URL as an Advanced setting for self-hosters.
     3. Transport is the relay with Noise KK end to end between device pairs, so the relay sees ciphertext, sizes and timing only. No WebRTC in this phase; a direct path can come later behind the same device-to-device frames.
     4. A new device is pending until an approved device compares a six-digit code derived from both devices' keys and seals the account key to it. No server escrow. A recovery phrase, shown once at account creation and on demand, unwraps the account key when every device is lost. Revoking a device rotates the key, and every trust change is signed by an approved device.
     5. "Not mine: start over" at a first sign-in that finds an unknown device wipes the account at once when it has one device and no handoffs, and otherwise starts a seven-day reset announced to every approved device and cancellable from any of them.
     6. Remote input is on by default for approved devices, with a host-side switch per device, an always-visible "Viewed from" or "Controlled from" indicator, and one-action disconnect. The host refuses input for a paused session or from a device whose switch is off.
     7. A remote row keeps its host's number and shows the device name ("desktop · 6"). Ctrl+Shift+1–9 stay local; the palette's Go to session accepts "desktop 6". Remote rows never enter `layout.json`.
     8. Remote sessions join the needs-you count, lane and tray from slice 10e. Banners and sounds for them are a switch, off by default, in 10g. Remote approve cards come in 10g after their own security review, as slice 3d had.
     9. Handoffs: the core always writes a zero-token record at close, at every Stop, and after a crash from the archive. A smart summary through the user's own CLI headless (`claude -p` first) is opt-in per agent, and a "Hand off" row action fills the prompt line for the person to send. Marshell still never presses Enter.
     10. Delivery: Claude Code through a per-launch flag or the context hook role; Codex and Antigravity through `MARSHELL_HANDOFF` plus a two-line tagged block in the CLI's user-level instruction file, installed through the config service's consent card and removed on uninstall. The repository's own instruction files are never touched. 10f waits for phase 6's adapters.
     11. Handoffs are stored as ciphertext under the account key on the backend with a blind project index (an HMAC of the normalised git remote URL, or an assigned project key): newest 20 per project, 90 days, 256 KiB each, 10 devices per account, and a monthly relayed-bytes cap set from spike S14. Each PC keeps an owner-only plaintext copy under `~/.marshell`, redacted before sealing. Export to a synced folder is off by default.
     12. The phase starts with its spikes after 4d merges and runs beside phase 5; 10f runs after phase 6.

### Accessibility safety net

Two bugs (a prompt line announced twice, a selected rail item that vanished in Windows High Contrast) were found by a late review, not by a test. Four layers now catch that kind of problem early.

1. **Shared building blocks** (`src/components/a11y/`). `Announcer` is the only live region in the app: silent on first render, never the same words twice running, at most one message per 1.5 s with the latest winning, and an assertive message is not held back. `SelectedMark` (`.is-selected-mark`, `selectedProps()`) gives every selected state three independent cues: a fill, a 2 px bar on the end edge and, in forced colours, a Highlight outline. Features use these and do not draw their own.
2. **Automated rules** (`sharedBlocks.test.ts`, `rules.test.tsx`). Fail the build when a file other than the Announcer adds a live region (the few exceptions are listed with reasons, and a debt list may only shrink); when a selected-state selector has no forced-colors rule in its file; when a status lacks a drawn shape or words, or shares them with another; when a toggle changes its name with its state; when a window renders more than one live region; or when a forced-colors rule uses a non-system colour or a border colour.
3. **Matrix** (`pnpm a11y:matrix`, `docs/a11y/a11y-matrix.md`). Windows High Contrast, reduced motion, 150% zoom and four colour-vision filters, with screenshots and in-page checks for selected markers, focus rings, clipped text, looping motion and live regions.
4. **Screen-reader script** (`docs/a11y/screen-reader-script.md`). Ten minutes with Narrator and NVDA at every sub-review sign-off, with the exact phrases expected. A fail is fixed before sign-off.

**What is announced (the setting `announce`).** The default, `needs-you`, announces a request or question arriving (for the focused session by name, and "N sessions need you" when the count rises anywhere), a stop or an end, the session you switch to ("Session 6, billing", plus what it needs), and "No session selected". It never announces progress, "working", a finished turn or anything in a pane you are not in. "Read the focused session's status" (Ctrl+Shift+G, ⌘G) says the focused session's state on request, every time. `everything` (every status change in every visible pane) and `on-request` (nothing until asked) are typed in the setting but not built: until they are, `everything` behaves like `needs-you`.

### Batch 3 design decisions

Onboarding, Departures, the doctor, the settings page, the banners and the tray (phase-0 rows 7 to 11), as they were designed and reviewed.

- **Unknown values.** Rows and the status strip leave an unknown value out (nothing guessed). A table or a form, where a missing cell would break the shape, shows an en dash: Departures, the doctor, the settings page, the consent card ("line –").
- **Pages scroll in their body.** A page (Doctor, Settings) scrolls in `.page__body`; the fix sheet scrolls in `.fix-sheet__body`; the onboarding frame scrolls in `.onboarding__body`. Each has a visible themed scrollbar (like the drawer, since these are not the live window) and its actions are a last row outside the scrolling part, so Cancel, the fix and "Allow these changes" are reachable however long the change is, even at 720 by 480. `dialogBodies.test.ts` guards the exception so it cannot spread.
- **Announcing.** An action the user caused (a fix, an apply) is announced in every announce mode, as the `action-result` kind. The pages add no live region of their own.
- **Config safety copy.** A card or a strip says exactly what changes and where. Undo copy only names things that exist and says what removal does for that CLI, built from its switches (`removalCopy` in `consent.ts`): Claude, "Marshell removes the hook entries it added and puts your own status line back from statusline-chain.json; the rest of the file stays as it is"; Codex, "Marshell removes the hook entries it added; the rest of the file stays as it is"; Antigravity, "Marshell removes the hook entries it added, deletes hooks.json if nothing else is in it, and puts your own status line back from statusline-chain.json; the rest of each file stays as it is". It never says "removes only the entries it added" where a status line command was replaced rather than added. A restore from a snapshot (the fix sheet, the settings change strip) is only promised for the snapshot taken by that same apply, for as long as the file is unchanged since. A chained status line shows where the user's own command is kept (`~/.marshell/agents/claude/statusline-chain.json`) as an added file in the diff. A file that is read-only, does not parse, or is managed is never described as about to change. A conflict offers "Review the difference" and "Reload", never an overwrite.
- **Consent and the diff.** One card per CLI, each with its own diff; no bulk "allow all". The count in the headline is the number of entries in the diff (a test), the bridge path is quoted inside every command (it has a space), and a stand-in made long on purpose says so in its own headline.
- **Fix sheet.** Focus starts on the title (never an action); Tab stays inside, Escape closes and focus returns to the opener (`useDialogFocus`, the pure `trapKey`).
- **Settings.** The change strip is small and sticky; Review opens the exact diff in the page above it, so a long change never covers the form. A managed field shows the value the organisation enforces and says the user's own is not used.
- **Banners.** Allow and Deny appear only when the whole command fits on one line (160 characters) and is shown; an escalation, a long or multi-line command and a risky request offer only Open or Review. Linux shows just Open.
- **Departures.** Rows are held while the pointer is over the board; a row that now needs you says it moves up. Y opens the request with focus on the card, N with focus on Deny (neither answers); the letter shown comes from the user's own layout when the browser can tell. The board has explicit table roles (the grid layout would drop them in some browsers), one row group per group, a roving tab stop, and counts its "+N more" line and an open request in the height it fits.

## Cross-cutting requirements (every phase)

- **Privacy:** no telemetry and no crash reporting, ever. The app makes no network calls except ones you start (updates, a model-backed recap you turned on). Signing in to an account (phase 10) is one of those: while signed in, the app keeps one connection open to the relay and refreshes its token, and signing out closes both. Crash information stays local in the diagnostics export. The onboarding says so in one line.
- **Zero token cost by default:** no model call happens unless you enable a model-backed feature; a test asserts it.
- **Secrets at rest:** transcripts and recordings are stored with owner-only permissions (0700 / owner ACL), not encrypted, so search stays fast. Redaction happens on export.
- **Languages:** English only in v1. All user-facing copy lives in one string catalog so translation can be added later. Layout already handles right-to-left names.
- **Accessibility:** besides Storybook axe, each UI phase ends with a keyboard-only walkthrough; the terminal ships with xterm's screen-reader mode available; one screen-reader pass before release.
- **Budgets beyond latency:** memory per session, idle CPU and startup with 1000 sessions are measured in the perf panel and given numbers when phase 2 lands.
- **Nothing depends on this machine:** the app works from what it ships and what it detects, never from state that happens to exist on the development PC (an installed font, a fetched DLL, a PATH entry, an existing `~/.marshell` or CLI config). A PR that adds such a dependency names it under "Known limits" and adds it to the clean-machine check in phase 9.
- **OS integration:** autostart at login, `marshell://` registration and "Open in Marshell" are settings, all off by default.

## v2 and later (not in v1)

From brief §5.9 and §6: the installer runner (install CLIs; the update button and version display moved to phase 6 on 2026-10-16), review-to-merge (for worktree tasks it moved to slice 2e+ on 2026-10-16), start-from-issue, checkpoints, file tree, phone check-in, and the `marshelld` daemon; accounts and cross-device are phase 10 above (decided 2026-10-22). v1 keeps the core protocol-first so these are additions, not rewrites. Also deferred: Airport mode (relabel statuses), if wanted at all.

## 10. Testing strategy

- **Rust unit tests**
  - Adapters and normalizers run against **recorded fixtures**. `marshell-hook record` writes the real stdin to `crates/core/tests/fixtures/<agent>/<cli-version>/<event>.json`, with insta snapshots of the `SessionEvent` output.
  - Each new CLI version gets a new fixture directory, which becomes a compatibility matrix.
- **Config service**
  - Property tests (`proptest`): bytes outside the edited region never change; apply followed by undo restores the identical bytes.
  - Conflict tests.
- **PTY**: tests drive `fake-agent`, which emits output, BEL, OSC 7/133/9/777 and calls the bridge, so status flows are deterministic in CI without real CLIs.
- **Frontend**: Vitest + Testing Library for components; Storybook test-runner with axe for a11y on every story.
- **E2E per OS**
  - (a) Playwright drives the real frontend against a headless core binary with `fake-agent`. This works on all three OSes because of the protocol-first design.
  - (b) `tauri-driver` WebDriver smoke test of the packaged app on Windows and Linux. macOS has no WKWebView driver, so it relies on (a) plus a manual checklist per release.
- **Performance**: criterion bench for the pty to WebSocket path in CI; a hidden perf panel measures keypress→paint latency.

## 11. Risks and open questions

- **WebKitGTK** is the biggest platform risk; the S1 gate is the decision point and Electron is the fallback.
- **CLI churn.** Claude's transcript format is officially unstable, and hooks and status fields change monthly. Mitigations: versioned fixtures, defensive parsing, and treating the raw archive copy as the durable artifact.
- **PermissionRequest hold UX** (S9). A poorly chosen hold time could hide Claude's own prompt.
- **Codex hook trust friction.** Installed hooks don't run until the user trusts them, so onboarding has to show this.
- **Antigravity CLI is young.** Its hooks are known from the bundled doc and strings in agy 1.3.3, not yet from a captured payload (see "Corrections to the brief"); the adapter records one fixture per event before it ships, and the shape may move between versions.
- **Windows unpackaged toast limits** in dev builds.
- **macOS notifications need signing**, which needs an Apple Developer account (Account Holder).
- **The 30-day Claude cleanup** deletes transcripts if the app isn't opened for a month. The doctor offers to raise `cleanupPeriodDays`, with consent.
- **Scope.** v1 is large for one developer, so phases ship independently usable increments.
- **Name.** Marshell is clear on a web search only; get a proper trademark search before the first public release.
- **Crowded category** (Maestro, Orca, Agent Desk, Termexo, Schaltwerk, Belfry, herdr). The differentiators stay as in brief §6, "Borrowed from the field".

## First actions after approval

1. `git init` in `C:\dev\Airport`. Ask whether to rename the folder to `marshell`. Move the brief to `docs/BRIEF.md` and update it with the rename and the corrections above.
2. Run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills`. Create the GitHub repo (MIT) and file the tickets via `to-spec` → `to-tickets`.
3. Phase 0 design and the phase 1 spikes run in parallel.

## Verification (of the plan's execution)

Each phase's exit criteria above are the checks. Before claiming any phase done:
- the CI matrix is green on all three OSes;
- the fixture and E2E suites pass;
- a manual run on this Windows machine with real Claude Code shows the behaviour.
